# MEGA - Technical Feature Detail (EN)

## Mega Assistant

The native `top_contact_interactions` tool ranks contacts by incoming, non-private messages over 1–365 recent days. It returns exact top-contact counts and up to 40 recent text excerpts from the leader; topic summaries must disclose this sample. Account action discovery now returns each curated tool's actual input schema, including parameters for reports, searches, Kanban, calendar, and nested write bodies; it rejects calls that fail feature or role eligibility and gives argument guidance for rejected API requests. `db:mega_prepare` queues a semantic index refresh after successful migrations when an embedding provider is available; the indexer skips unchanged definitions.

The `inboxes_health` action is described as applicable only to WhatsApp Cloud, Notificame, and Twilio (SMS or WhatsApp). In the assistant dispatcher, a 400 for an unsupported channel becomes a `not_applicable` diagnostic; a structured provider authorization 422 becomes `authorization_required`. Both retain `source_status` and guidance against repeating the same call, while successful health responses keep their original result.

CSAT and V2 report CSV exports require `since` and `until` to avoid invalid periods. The traffic heatmap uses `days_before` and `timezone_offset`, defaulting to a zero UTC offset. The V2 time series (`GET /api/v2/accounts/:id/reports`), its drilldown and the summary also take an optional `timezone` (IANA or Rails name, which the dashboard sends from the browser): a known zone wins over `timezone_offset` (`TimezoneHelper#timezone_name_from_params`), so dates in the other daylight saving season stay on the browser's days, and an unknown or missing name keeps the offset (the traffic heatmap still uses only the offset). Lookups of missing automation rules, custom roles, and SLA policies return 404.

Before running a read or creating a proposal, `curated_action_call`, `account_action_call` and named tools validate the arguments against the tool's published schema. Equivalent scalars are converted (a numeric ID where the schema expects a string, `"5"` where it expects an integer); anything still wrong returns `invalid_arguments` with the exact missing fields, out-of-enum values, or unaccepted fields, and nothing is executed or proposed. The model can fix the call in the same turn instead of the user confirming a write the API would reject. Formats (URI, date) are still validated by the API. Within one response, an identical read returns the result already fetched, an identical call that already failed answers `repeated_failed_call`, and an identical write returns the pending proposal instead of creating another card. An unknown tool name returns `similar_tools` with the closest real tools. For `account_action_call`, the schema is that of the route's named tool, without the IDs already in the path; an unknown route also names the methods (`accepted_methods`) or API version (`api_version`) where it does exist.

Each response allows 12 lookups (reads and discovery) and, separately, up to 6 write proposals; presenting blocks is free. Once lookups are spent, only `present_blocks`, named write tools, and up to 2 `account_actions_list` searches to find a missing write tool remain (discovery reads no account data), so requested changes can still be proposed without further reads; a write past its allowance counts as a lookup. The assistant must not claim it prepared or proposed a change without a `proposal_id`. An unexpected error inside a tool (for example an SQL error) no longer fails the whole response: it is logged and the model receives `server_error`. For `/conversations/filter` and `/contacts/filter`, the dispatcher sets `query_operator` to `and` on conditions that omit it and removes it from the last one, as the dashboard does.

For Captain setup, the assistant has closed schemas for creating and editing the assistant (name, description, `guardrails`, `response_guidelines`, and `config`), FAQs (`question`, `answer`, `assistant_id`), and documents (`external_link`, `assistant_id`). Before proposing a scenario it checks that every tool assigned in `tools` or referenced with `tool://` in the instruction exists for that assistant: the 7 built-in ones (`add_label_to_conversation`, `add_private_note`, `add_contact_note`, `update_priority`, `faq_lookup`, `resolve_conversation`, `handoff`) plus the account's custom tools and the tools of MCP servers connected to the assistant. It also checks the enabled-scenario limit. If a check fails, no proposal is created and the model receives the list of available tools. The `captain_configuration_guide` tells it to call `captain_assistants_tools` before writing a scenario.

`account_actions_list` returns a single list of up to 10 tools per page, each schema once (it used to repeat it three times and resend the resource list: about 40 KB per search, now about 6 KB). It accepts several searches in one call separated by `;`, keeping the 3 closest tools of each, and ranks first the tools whose name contains the searched terms. Media send schemas are included only when the search mentions messages or files. Tools for `create` and `update` actions (except `notifications_update`) require a `body` with at least one field, so an empty proposal never reaches confirmation. For the assistant, the most used writes (labels, teams, SLA policies, custom roles, webhooks, automation rules, macros, and contacts) have closed schemas with the fields each controller accepts, so an invented field (`name` instead of `title`) is rejected before confirmation; an SLA policy requires at least one time. The contract shared with MCP is unchanged. When a resource is read with a list, search or detail tool (for example `labels_list`), the assistant automatically receives its `create`, `update`, and `delete` tools, without relying on the model to discover them; the active set holds up to 48 named tools. `conversations_bulk_update` applies the same status, assignee, team, or label change to several conversations (up to 500) in one proposal through `/bulk_actions`; the assistant uses it instead of proposing one update per conversation. The assistant proposes independent changes even when other parts of the request remain unresolved, and asks for the values that define a record (such as SLA times) instead of proposing an empty one.

### Current behavior (September 2026)

This block takes precedence over older paragraphs in this section.

- **Who can use it:** account administrators and members whose custom role includes “Use the AI assistant”. Administrators can read their team’s assistant chats (read-only) from the Team tab of the full page; chats are no longer private to their owner.
- **History:** search, Pinned, Today, Previous 7 days and Older, shared by the full page and the floating panel. An amber dot marks chats with an action waiting for confirmation.
- **Actions:** each proposal shows a card with its title, status, recipient, a link to the conversation and a preview of the text and files that will be sent, or the fields that will change with their current and new values. Text messages can be sent or kept as a draft in the reply box.
- **Progress and errors:** while answering, the assistant lists the tools it is using. Failed replies say why (no AI model available, AI service not responding).
- **Rich replies:** when you ask for a report, table, chart or comparison, the assistant answers with visual blocks below its text: text, markdown, table (sortable, CSV export), metrics, card, chart (bar, line, area, pie, doughnut), timeline, contact, conversation, kanban, form, suggested actions and HTML report. An executive report is built in the chat with blocks (metrics, chart, findings, table) and the assistant offers the PDF version as a suggested action; the HTML report is only generated when you ask for it, opens by itself in the full-page canvas, and can be downloaded or saved as PDF through the browser's print dialog. Forms and suggested actions answer in the same chat, so the assistant keeps the context. In the pet, blocks adapt to the width: metrics in 2×2, tables show 3 columns and expand, kanban scrolls sideways. Reports and PDF, HTML, Markdown and text files open in a canvas: on the full page it is a column to the right of the chat (you can keep chatting), and in the pet it opens over the screen, with preview or code view, download and print.
- **Plans and AI Actions:** each completed reply is charged the AI Actions of the model that answered (the automatic reply after a confirmation is not). With no AI Actions left, messages are refused and administrators get a link to buy a package; administrators see the remaining balance. Plans can include the assistant for questions only or with actions. Administrators of accounts without the assistant see what it offers and a link to the plans.

Technical detail: features `mega_ai_assistant` (access) and `mega_ai_assistant_actions` (write proposals and confirmed execution; migration `20260922140000` enables it for accounts that already had the assistant). Access rule `MegaAssistantThreadPolicy.access?`; actions rule `MegaAssistantThreadPolicy.actions?`. Charging in `MegaAssistant::ResponseJob` (`Llm::UsageTracker.track`, `increment_response_usage(feature: 'mega_assistant')`); `402 ai_actions_exhausted` in `MessagesController`; `availability` returns `ai_actions` (administrators with a real limit) and `actions`. Messages store `error_code` and `steps`; `MegaAssistant::ChangeSnapshot` keeps current values in `result.before` of pending updates; decision `draft` (status `drafted`) for text-only `conversation_message_send`. The route no longer requires the feature: the page explains the locked state. `ModelRouter` falls back to `MEGA_ASSISTANT_LLM_MODEL` for chat and documents. Tracking: `docs/MEGA_ASSISTANT_NEGOCIO_UX.md`.

Generative UI detail: the model calls `present_blocks` (`MegaAssistant::Tools::PresentBlocks`, outside the 8-call tool budget, at most 3 calls per reply and still available once the budget is spent). `MegaAssistant::BlockSchema` normalizes the 13 block types, drops malformed ones, caps them at 12 blocks / 250 KB, only accepts dashboard links under `/app/accounts/:id/`, loads `contact`/`conversation` from the account by id behind their Pundit `show?` policy, and sanitizes `html_artifact` (no scripts, event handlers, remote URLs or external images). Blocks are stored in `mega_assistant_messages.blocks` (jsonb, migration `20260923120000`) and returned in the thread payload; later turns replay them as a compact summary (HTML omitted) so follow-ups keep the data. The frontend renders them with `components-next/megaAssistant/blocks/AssistantBlocks.vue`; the report preview is an `iframe` with `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-modals"` and a `default-src 'none'` CSP, and PDF export uses the browser print dialog. Form answers and suggested actions are sent as the user's next message (one AI Action, like any message). `AssistantCanvas.vue` renders reports and attachments (PDF via the browser viewer on a typed object URL, HTML in a sandboxed iframe — only the assistant's own reports get `allow-same-origin allow-modals` for printing — and Markdown/text through `MessageFormatter`); it docks as a column in the full page from 1024 px and opens as a native `<dialog>` elsewhere, provided to blocks and attachments via `provide('megaAssistantCanvas')`. CSV report downloads are parsed into rows for the model instead of becoming chat attachments; other non-JSON exports are named after their endpoint.

Realtime voice detail: the browser opens a WebRTC peer and posts its SDP offer to `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/mega_assistant/threads/:thread_id/voice_sessions` (`takeover: true` on a user start ends the user's own leftover session); `MegaAssistant::Voice::SessionService` proxies it to OpenAI `POST /v1/realtime/calls` with the API key server-side (no credential reaches the browser), stores a `mega_assistant_voice_sessions` row (one active per user and account), charges 5-minute blocks (`audio_seconds`, at most 30 minutes, renewed by `POST :id/renew`) and hangs up on end or timeout (`VoiceSessionTimeoutJob`). The voice model gets its tools: `ask_assistant` starts the same text turn as a typed message (the browser also sends `heard`, the `gpt-transcribe` transcript of the same audio item, waited for up to 1.5 s and kept in the message `interaction` when it differs, so the text model tries that spelling of a name too) (`MegaAssistant::TurnStarter`, one AI Action) and the browser polls `GET :id/replies/:reply_id` (`MegaAssistant::Voice::ReplyOutput`: reply text, blocks shown and pending proposals with `voice_confirmable`) to hand it back; `confirm_proposal` and `cancel_proposal` run in the browser: a 5-second countdown on the card, then `PATCH actions/:id` with `via: 'voice'` (the voice then hears what the action ended in, or waits for the follow-up reply the server started, so it never says a failed change was done), which the server refuses for deletions, merges/irreversible POSTs, bulk changes, batches and when the user has no live voice session on the thread. The call is held in the user's dashboard language (`Voice::Instructions` names it; one word heard in another language, a name or an English system message never switches it). Hearing (`MegaAssistant::Voice::Hearing`): `gpt-transcribe` (override `MEGA_ASSISTANT_VOICE_TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL`), the user's language, up to 50 account names as keywords (screen record, the assistant's name and Captain's displayed name, contacts of the user's latest visible conversations, inboxes, teams, agents, labels; the same list goes into the voice instructions, because the `ask_assistant` request is written by the realtime model from its own hearing), near-field noise reduction, server VAD with 700 ms silence (`MEGA_ASSISTANT_VOICE_SILENCE_MS`, 300–2000, or `MEGA_ASSISTANT_VOICE_TURN_DETECTION=semantic_vad` with `MEGA_ASSISTANT_VOICE_EAGERNESS` low/medium/high/auto to compare in real calls). Voice settings live in `accounts.settings.captain_voice` (`name`, `speed` 0.25–1.5, `custom_id`, `greeting`), edited in Captain settings via `PUT captain/preferences`. Dictation: `POST mega_assistant/transcriptions` (multipart `audio`, 10 MB, 60 per hour, not charged) uses the assistant's transcription model or, without one, Captain's audio transcription route; `availability` reports `voice` and `dictation`. Feature flag `mega_ai_assistant_voice`. Navigation and continuity: the voice call is one module-level session for the whole dashboard (`useMegaAssistantVoice`), not ended by route changes or panel unmounts; `AssistantVoiceDock.vue` (mounted in `Dashboard.vue`) shows it when no panel is open: title with the assistant name, state, mute, "Open chat" and a red hang-up, taking the pet's place on desktop with the pet inside it, ringed in the state's color, while `AssistantPet` steps aside (`petPlacement` shares where the pet is; `petMover` lends the pet's drag, so dragging the call by the pet or its text moves and saves the pet), and centered above the tab bar with the orb on phones or with the pet hidden. The tools of a module take the feature its page needs when they name none (`AccountActions::Definitions::MODULE_TOOL_FEATURES`: Kanban, scheduled messages, campaigns, calendar, tasks, help center, chat rooms, assignment and capacity policies, agent bots, audit logs), so a module that is off offers no tools to Mega Assistant or the account MCP; the dashboard apps tools also come with the integrations settings. The inbox settings page sends the tab open now (`inboxSettingsTab`, as picking a tab rewrites the URL outside the router) and each tab says what it changes and its tools (`ScreenContext::INBOX_TABS`: general, collaborators, business hours with the `working_hours` shape, CSAT, pre-chat form, agent bot, channel configuration). A settings list that opens a record in its side panel (labels, teams, agents, macros, SLA, custom roles, chat rooms, assignment policies, webhooks, dashboard apps, canned responses, custom attributes, agent bots, automation rules; `useSettingsPanel` publishes `openSettingsPanel`) sends `panel: {id, mode}` (view, form, create, members); the server names that record with the checks of its `<list>_item` page and gives the list its purpose and tools, and `open_page` opens `<list>_item` (one record) or `<list>_new` (the creation form). A form that opens in a dialog over another page reports itself with `reportOpenPanel({id, mode, kind})` and `clearOpenPanel()`: Captain (new assistant from any Captain page, FAQs, FAQ suggestions, documents, scenarios, guardrails, guidelines, custom tools, MCP servers and their tools, inboxes, Captain MCP server settings), the campaign pages and scheduled messages (`kind` reschedule or resend). The report carries the page path, so a kept-alive page behind another one never lends its form to it. These form dialogs open with `Dialog`'s `assistantReachable` (show() under their own backdrop, not showModal(), which makes the rest of the page inert), so the assistant panel, its pet and the voice dock (`data-mega-assistant`) stay usable over them; clicks and Escape inside the assistant never close the form, while the backdrop and Escape elsewhere still do. Confirmation dialogs stay modal. The server (`ScreenContext::PAGE_PANELS`, `PANEL_KINDS`) names the form and, when the user may open the page, its record, a Captain one only inside the assistant on the page. Write tools added with it: `dashboard_apps_create/update/delete`, `message_templates_update`, `conversation_monitors_create/update/resume/delete`, `kanban_offers_*`, `inbox_csat_template_create` (the CSAT survey itself changes through `inboxes_update` with `csat_config`), and the native `profile_update` (only name, display name and signature; email, password, avatar and 2FA stay on the profile page). The help center editors also name the article and category on screen (within that portal), the new inbox wizard its picked channel and steps (`INBOX_WIZARD_GUIDE`), and search its tab; `open_page` opens `settings_inbox_new` and `portal_article_new`. A conversation opened from Notifications (`inbox_view_conversation`) is described as a conversation screen, and a list filtered by a folder or segment names it (`filters.folder`, `filters.segment`: `{id, name}`, only the user's own saved filters). The Kanban and Flow Builder screens carry their purpose and tools (`ScreenContext::MODULE_GUIDES`, while the feature is on; the boards list (`view: boards`), the offers (`view: offers`, no tool yet), a new unsaved flow (`view: create`, from `flowEditorState`) and a flow's runs (`view: executions`) have their own guide too, and the board settings (`view: settings`, `kanban?view=funnels&edit`) have their own: `kanban_boards_update` for the board and `kanban_steps_*` for one stage at a time, never the whole stages hash), pinned like page tools, and a Flow Builder screen always brings the `flows` guide. Screen context publishes a generic `{module: 'page', route, section}` for any allowlisted route (`ScreenContext::PAGE_SECTIONS`) plus the route params; on a page of one record (`ScreenContext::PAGE_RECORDS`: Captain assistant screens, chat room, company, portal screens, campaign and report detail pages, dashboard app, capacity policy, monitor, data import, and the task the Tasks page opens from its `task_id` query, which `open_page` can now open too) the server reads only the param naming the record and adds `page.record` (`{type, id, name}`, a portal's locale when allowed) through `Pages#shown_record`, the same permission, feature and visibility checks as opening that page, dropping it otherwise; a page with a guide (`ScreenContext::PAGE_GUIDES`, today Scheduled messages, Calendar (scheduling there is a calendar event) and Tasks (a task of the Tasks module), the campaign pages (a message for many contacts is a campaign, never one send per conversation), Agent bots (webhook bots, kept apart from Captain in the captain guide too), Chat rooms, whose messages the messaging guide also sends to `chat_room_messages_create` instead of a conversation, plus Call reports (`call_reports_list`, `call_reports_metrics`), Captain conversation monitors (`conversation_monitors_list`, `conversation_monitors_get`) and an assistant's MCP servers (`captain_assistant_mcp_servers_list`, `_add`, `_remove`), pages that had no assistant tool before; these tools are also on the account MCP server) also says what it is for and lists its tools, which `ResponseService#preselect_tools` sends up front and `ToolRegistry#pin` keeps for the whole turn (later searches never push them out); the assistant page keeps the previous screen; voice instructions carry a bounded thread recap (`MegaAssistant::ThreadHistory.recap`, up to 6000 characters, newest first kept, naming the files each message attached; then the thread's proposals still waiting, at most 5, to be checked again before being called valid, and the rule that an earlier reference the recap lacks goes to `ask_assistant`, which reads the whole recent chat, instead of "I do not remember"; the chat assistant in turn reads older messages of its own thread with the `chat_history` tool (by words, or the messages before one, finished messages only) when the reference is older than the history it was given), and a message typed or a file attached in the call's chat during the call is the user's own turn in the call: the browser posts it with `voice_session_id` and, while that session is live on the thread, `MessagesController#create` stores it (`interaction.intent` `voice.typed`, files kept, no reply started, not charged) and the voice model receives it as a `user` item and answers it out loud as the one assistant of the chat (a typed greeting gets a spoken greeting back; its words are written into the chat like any spoken reply; attached files reach it by name and it reads them through `ask_assistant`, whose turn still receives the last two requests' images and PDFs); once the call ended there, the message gets a normal written reply. A form answered in the call, or a written reply still running when the call starts, is followed as the call's request and the voice says it in the first person, as its own answer, never as a reply from someone else (`useMegaAssistantVoice#typedOnScreen`, session instructions); a spoken request keeps the user's own words as its chat message (the transcription, `heard`), with the request as the voice wrote it in `interaction.request` for the text model, and its reply is written to be said word for word by the voice (`ResponseService#spoken_hint`: one or two short spoken sentences, no markdown, details in blocks), so the chat keeps what the user heard; the voice talks in short turns, never claims an action without calling `ask_assistant` nor a screen it was not told, and gets a silent screen note each time the user settles on another screen (`AssistantVoiceDock` → `screenChanged`); the transcription hints leave out the assistant's own names, and a user line that is only that name (made up from noise or echo) is not written (`transcripts` answers `skipped: unheard`); a call on a chat with history opens naming what it was last about. The free tool `open_page` (`MegaAssistant::Pages` catalog: route path, permissions, record visibility through `RecordSelector` + Pundit, the route's feature flag plus the sidebar's `menu_feature`, installation types and router configuration guards; labels by title, portals by slug and locale; `kanban_board_settings` opens a board's configuration for who may change it, and its result names the page that really opened so the reply never calls a board its settings) covers every dashboard route: `spec/services/mega_assistant/pages_routes_spec.rb` parses the route files and fails on a route that is neither a page nor in `Pages::EXCLUDED_ROUTES` with a reason (redirects, creation wizards, onboarding) writes a `page` block; `useAssistantNavigation` follows both the panel's thread and the call's own thread (`useMegaAssistantVoice#callThread`, so a page asked by voice opens with the pet closed or showing another chat) and opens a new page block once with `history.state.megaAssistantOpen`, and the launcher then opens the pet on the same chat. While a spoken request runs, `GET voice_sessions/:id/replies/:reply_id` also returns `progress` (`steps` count and the last step, tool name and detail, internal steps left out) and the browser has the voice tell it in one sentence once the call was quiet for 4 s (at most every 7 s, never while the user or the voice speaks; after 10 s with no new step it says it is still working, twice per request at most), with a system item and `tool_choice: none`. Measured on dev data: replies start at once, a routing model call takes 1.5–3 s, then 3–8 model rounds of 2–5 s, 5–24 s in total. It also keeps one queued correction (a newer one replaces it) and runs it right after; `cancel_request` cancels the running reply through `POST voice_sessions/:id/replies/:reply_id/cancel` (`MegaAssistant::ReplyCanceller`: the reply fails as `cancelled` under its own row lock and its lease is cleared, so the job stops without writing or charging). A change of one conversation goes through its own tool (`conversation_assignments_assign`, `conversations_set_labels`, both supplied up front with `conversations_bulk_update` in `ToolRegistry::CORE_WRITE_TOOL_NAMES`), so it revises its card and a spoken yes may confirm it; a bulk proposal of the same conversations with another value also supersedes the one waiting (`BaseTool#replaced_bulk_proposal`). What a spoken yes may confirm comes from `MegaAssistant::ActionRisk`, explicit risk metadata per write tool (delete, bulk, irreversible, sends external, admin configuration); a spec fails when a write tool has none. Assistant name: `MegaAssistant::AssistantName` (user preference per account in `users.custom_attributes['mega_assistant_names']` → `accounts.settings` name → "<INSTALLATION_NAME> Assistant"), validated (1–30 chars, letters/digits/space/.-'), used by the text and voice instructions and returned in `availability` and thread payloads; `GET/PATCH mega_assistant/assistant_name` for the profile and the `assistant_name_set` tool (a proposal, voice-confirmable) for the conversational rename. Each call greets once when `session.created` and the audio track have arrived, with a system item and `tool_choice: none`; never on reconnect, navigation or the dock. Preferences: `MegaAssistant::Preferences` keeps per user and account, in `users.custom_attributes['mega_assistant_preferences'][account_id]`, only the values that differ from the defaults (`language` auto/es/en/pt_BR, `style` balanced/brief/detailed, `lists` auto/table/cards, `voice_greeting` true/false), checked against that allowlist on write and on read (unknown keys dropped; a value outside it answers 422 `invalid_preferences` with `field`); `GET/PATCH/DELETE mega_assistant/preferences` acts on the current user only, and DELETE removes the account's entry (and the key once empty). They are appended to `ResponseService.instructions_for` only when set (a fixed language also replaces the per-response "language of this latest message" hint) and to the voice instructions (language and style; lists are applied by the text assistant), worded as style hints, never permissions; nothing learns them from conversations. Greeting: `captain_voice.greeting` (plain text, squished, at most 200 characters, no `<`/`>`, `{name}` = the effective assistant name) goes into the voice instructions as "Greeting for the start of the call: ..." unless the user turned `voice_greeting` off, and `POST voice_sessions` returns `greet` (false when off) for the browser. Privacy and retention: preferences and the per-user assistant name live in `users.custom_attributes`, which the profile and the agents list serialize whole (`api/v1/models/_user.json.jbuilder`, `_agent.json.jbuilder`), so other members of an account can read them through the API; DELETE (the name: a blank PATCH) or deleting the user removes them. A voice call stores its `mega_assistant_voice_sessions` row (thread, user, provider/model, provider call id, times, blocks charged, tool names, end reason), the usage events of each block (reference = the session) and, as thread messages, the requests sent through `ask_assistant` (text, `interaction.intent = voice.request`, `voice_session_id`, `heard` when it differs), the text replies and the call transcripts (`interaction.intent = voice.transcript`, `voice_session_id`, `item_id`); the audio is not stored by us (no attachment, no recording). A transcript is written once per item (under the thread lock), retried after a network or server failure (1 s, 3 s) and reported to Sentry as `mega_assistant_voice_transcript_lost` when it never gets in; the endpoint still takes lines for 2 minutes after the call ended (`TRANSCRIPT_GRACE`, later refusals logged), and on hang-up the browser writes what the user said that no response answered yet and keeps the connection open up to 1.5 s for transcriptions still on their way. Transcripts: `POST voice_sessions/:id/transcripts` (`role` user/assistant, `text` squished and cut at 4000, `item_id`; live session only, idempotent per thread and `item_id`, 422 `invalid_transcript` when blank, not charged) writes a completed message. The browser posts the user's audio item (its `conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.completed` text, waited up to 1.5 s) when the response that answered it called no `ask_assistant` (the request message stands for it), and the voice's `output_audio` transcript only for the provider's own turns with no function call: every `response.create` the browser sends carries `metadata.kind` (`greeting`, `progress`, `result`) and is skipped. Transcripts are never the request: `ThreadHistory.recent(thread, before: reply_id)` leaves out what came after the reply started, `ResponseService` and `Tools::ConversationsReview` skip them when they take the user's latest request, `with_interaction` sends their words alone, and `Voice::ReplyOutput#proposals` does not let one end the reply's proposal window; `ThreadHistory.recap` keeps them, so the next call remembers. A click confirm or cancel on the call's chat calls `useMegaAssistantVoice#decidedOnScreen` (ids the voice resolved itself are ignored): the voice gets a system item with the decision and, for the follow-up reply the server started, its `replies/:reply_id` output once it ends (run as the call's request meanwhile, so a spoken request queues behind it). While a call runs on the account, the panel opens its chat on mount (even when not in the loaded list) and the voice button opens it instead of creating a thread. Thread messages are readable by the owner and account administrators and go with the thread (`DELETE threads/:id` also deletes its voice session rows); usage events stay in the ledger. Audio flows from the browser to OpenAI over WebRTC (only the SDP goes through our server), so its retention follows OpenAI's API data policy for the provider key.

Functional advisor (#2390). Confirming re-runs the checks that let a write be proposed (`MegaAssistantAction#confirmation_refusal`, after the claim so the outcome is recorded and the follow-up reply explains it): the named tool's `feature`/`admin_only` through `ActionCatalog#eligible?`, or for an endpoint call `ActionCatalog#entry_allowed?` (shared with `account_action_call`), with a fresh account and membership; a refusal fails the action with `no_longer_allowed` and runs nothing. A proposal whose card showed `before` values is read again through `ChangeSnapshot`; any field whose current value differs fails it with `stale_proposal` and `changed` (current values), so a teammate's concurrent edit is never silently overwritten. Native tools without a catalog spec keep their own checks. Capability inventory: the free tool `feature_guide` (`MegaAssistant::FunctionalModules`, 35 modules, each naming its main page in `Pages` and a tool-name pattern over `COMMON_TOOL_SPECS`) returns per module `status` (`disabled`, `not_configured`, `needs_permission` from the page's own refusal; else `configurable` when a write tool is eligible for this user, `guide_only` when none exists, `disabled`/`needs_permission` when they exist but their feature is off or they are administrator-only), `can_read`, `change_tools`, the page to open and a `next_step`; without a feature it lists every module's status. The instructions tell the model to check it before explaining or offering a setup and never to offer configuring a disabled, unconfigured or forbidden module. `spec/services/mega_assistant/functional_modules_spec.rb` fails on a module naming an unknown page, a page of one record or a pattern that matches no tool. Functional docs: `config/mega_assistant/functional_docs/<module>.md` (one per `FunctionalModules` key; front matter `version`, `sources` as `docs/features/...#heading`, `keywords` in English, Spanish and Portuguese; sections What it is, How it works, Requirements, Limits, Setting it up, Examples), loaded by `MegaAssistant::FunctionalDocs` (cached; keyword search, a keyword or the module name scoring over a word of the text, at most 3 matches) and returned by `feature_guide` with `feature` or `question`, with `version`, `sources` and `sections`, plus the module's `features` (its page's and tools' feature flags). docs/ is not in the production image, so the assistant never reads it. `spec/services/mega_assistant/functional_docs_spec.rb` is the staleness check: it fails when a module has no doc or a doc no module, a section is missing or empty, the version is not a past date, a source heading no longer exists, or a module depends on a feature missing from config/features.yml. Thread goal: `mega_assistant_threads.goal` (jsonb, migration 20261110190000) holds `objective`, `module`, `decisions` (at most 12 lines), `steps` (`title`, `status` pending/done/skipped, at most 12) and `updated_at`, written by the free tool `thread_goal` (`MegaAssistant::ThreadGoal.record!`: fields given replace, others stay; `finished: true` clears it). `ResponseService#add_response_context` appends it after the conversation on every reply (data, not instructions), and `ThreadHistory.recap` ends with it for voice calls (objective, decisions, steps not done). The Captain guide's method records one step per phase. It never grants anything: changes still go through proposals. Flow edits: the native proposal tool `flow_builder_flow_edit` (offered when `flow_builder_flows_update` is eligible, so administrators with Flow Builder on) applies `add_node`, `update_node` (data deep-merged, position), `remove_node` (with its edges), `add_edge` and `remove_edge` to the stored graph (`MegaAssistant::FlowGraphEdit`, at most 40 operations, unknown ids refused), checks it with `FlowBuilder::FlowValidator` and proposes the resulting `flow_builder_flows_update` with the stored definition as `before`, so a concurrent edit fails confirmation as `stale_proposal`. It reports the changes and how many blocks it left untouched, and never publishes. Read-back: after a confirmed update succeeds, `ChangeSnapshot.verify` reads the record through the same GET as the snapshot and stores `result.verification` (`verified`, `checked`, `differing` with the stored values; nested objects compared only by the keys sent, so custom attributes keep the others; nil for creations, sends, native changes or fields the API does not return). The card shows checked or differing, and the follow-up reply reports it. Telemetry: `ResponseJob` gives each reply a `MegaAssistant::TurnTelemetry` that `Tools::BaseTool#call` feeds with every tool result, stored on the reply as `mega_assistant_messages.metrics` (migration 20261110200000) when it completes or fails: `latency_ms`, `tool_calls`, `proposals`, `failed_tools` (`tool:error`, an `ok: false` API answer as `status_<code>`), `denied_tools` (`not_authorized`, `actions_not_in_plan`), `unsupported` (feature_guide modules disabled, not_configured, needs_permission or undocumented), `outcome`, `model`. `MegaAssistant::TelemetryReport` (`rake mega_assistant:telemetry[account_id,days]`, JSON) sums replies and failures, average and p95 latency, the top failed and denied tools, unsupported modules, confirmed changes completed/failed by reason (`stale_proposal`, `no_longer_allowed`…) with read-back `verified`/`differing`, and the `llm_usage_events` cost of the feature. QA: `spec/services/mega_assistant/qa_scenarios_spec.rb` sweeps every feature-gated write tool (named and as an endpoint call) and every administrator-only tool through the confirmation re-check, a proposal confirmed from another account, tools called with a chat of an account the user is not in, each account's own module status, the change tools of every module against what the user is really offered, and changes that fail halfway (a flow edit with one bad operation proposes nothing; a failing tool leaves the reply going and is recorded). `FunctionalModules` only counts tools the assistant has a route for (the global profile API is not one) plus native proposal tools, so the profile module offers `profile_update`. Modules an agent may open but only an administrator may change (Captain, campaigns, the help center) name the policy that decides it (`Captain::Assistant#create?`, `Campaign#create?`, `Portal#update?`), so they answer `needs_permission` to agents instead of `configurable`. `spec/enterprise/services/mega_assistant/captain_guided_setup_spec.rb` runs a whole Captain setup the way the guide orders it: module status, the goal with one step per phase, the assistant created and its guardrails updated as confirmed actions read back on the record, the goal resuming at the first pending phase, and a change refused once Captain is turned off. Continuity: a goal last updated a day or more ago comes back with its age and the instruction to confirm it and read the configuration again (`ThreadGoal::STALE_AFTER_DAYS`). `spec/services/mega_assistant/continuity_scenarios_spec.rb` covers a cancelled reply keeping the goal, a reply regenerated after a crash proposing the same change once, a correction superseding the proposal it corrects, the stale goal, and the same goal reaching a voice call on that chat only. Ungrounded answers: a reply of at least 200 characters whose tools returned nothing it could stand on (no successful call other than `present_blocks`, `task_plan`, `thread_goal` or `open_page`) is stored with `ungrounded: true`, except the automatic follow-up turns, and the report counts them. It is a heuristic for answers from memory, not a fact check. Dashboard: `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/mega_assistant/telemetry?days=7|30|90` (`TelemetryController`, administrators through `MegaAssistantThreadPolicy#team_index?`, other periods fall back to 7) returns the report, shown by `AssistantMetrics.vue` in the history's third tab (icon only, labelled "Metrics"), also in the phone bottom sheet. Voice and blocks: `Voice::ReplyOutput#shown_on_screen` now carries each block's `facts` (`MegaAssistant::BlockFacts`: metric label, value and delta; the first rows of a table as `Column: value`; card fields; chart points; timeline items; the start of a text; at most 8 lines of 160 characters, no ids or links), the voice instructions tell it to say the one or two figures that answer and to answer follow-ups from those facts, and `ResponseService#spoken_hint` asks the spoken reply to say the figures instead of only pointing at the screen. The call recap (`ThreadHistory.recap`) keeps block titles only, as before. Consulting method: the `setup` guide (`MegaAssistant::Guides`, chosen by goal wording in ES, EN and PT-BR such as configure, automate, set up, quiero que, necesito, and offered to the Decision Router) orders a goal into needs, `feature_guide` per need, reading what exists to reuse it, one recommended solution with its reasons, the plan in `thread_goal` with one question for the missing values, then each change proposed, confirmed, checked and followed by the next step. It maps common needs to the modules the docs describe (Captain or agent bots for FAQs and handoff, Flow Builder for guided conversations, automation or assignment policies for routing, Kanban for opportunities, scheduled messages or campaigns for follow-ups, canned responses). A goal does not take over the chat: when the latest message is about something else the reply answers it first and then offers to continue; "where were we?" resumes at the first pending step, never from the start. Residual race: the stale re-read and the write are adjacent but not atomic, so an edit landing in those milliseconds can still be overwritten. A row lock around the in-process write was tried and reverted: a non-joinable transaction runs `after_commit` callbacks (event dispatch, Flow Builder listeners, WhatsApp group sync, `Labels::UpdateJob`) before the real commit, and a joinable one swallows a controller's `ActiveRecord::Rollback` (inboxes#update). Closing it needs an optimistic precondition the controllers check themselves (`lock_version` or `If-Unmodified-Since` on `updated_at`). Live evaluation: `rake mega_assistant:eval ACCOUNT_ID=… [USER_ID=…] [ONLY=…]` (real model, costs money, everything rolled back) now runs multi-turn cases (`turns`, each with its own `expect_tools`, `expect_any_tools`, `forbid_tools`, `expect_text`, `reject_text`, optional `confirm` of that turn's non-send proposals and `screen_context`) and `disable_features` per case. `spec/fixtures/mega_assistant/evals.yml` adds 11 cases for #2390 (a business goal turned into a plan, the plan continuing, a module explained from its docs, a disabled module, no tool, a goal resumed after another topic, a correction, a multi-block flow without publishing, editing a flow keeping its blocks, a Captain setup by phases, an agent told who may set it up); a spec fails when a case names a tool the assistant does not have. These cases have not been run against a real model yet.

Screen context detail: `dashboard/composables/megaAssistant/useScreenContext.js` derives a reference-only snapshot from the route and existing stores for the conversation routes (detail and list, both `conversation_id`/`conversationId` param spellings, inbox/team/label/conversation-type filters, `bulkActions/getSelectedConversationIds` up to 500) and the contact routes (detail and list with label), with a module-wide `version` that only grows; it clears on route exit and account switch because it is route-derived. The panel sends it once per turn as the `screen_context` form field (JSON, capped at 16 KB by `requested_screen_context`), and the per-user opt-out lives in `localStorage` key `mega-assistant-screen-context`. The hint is stored in the reply's `run_arguments` so `RecoverRunsJob` replays it, and `MegaAssistant::ScreenContext` resolves it when the reply is generated: conversations through `RecordSelector#visible` plus `ConversationPolicy#show?`, contacts through the account plus `ContactPolicy#show?`, filter names only for inboxes the user is assigned to; cross-account, deleted, inaccessible or malformed references are dropped and listed in `dropped`. Selected conversations are saved as a `MegaAssistantSelection` (`filters: { source: 'screen' }`, reused when the same set is sent again). The resolved screen is added after the history in `add_response_context` (the instructions stay static for the prompt cache) and is skipped on automatic turns. `Tools::BaseTool#propose` stores `result.target` (conversation display_id or contact id, names, `on_screen`, `screen_version`) derived from the request path; `MegaAssistantAction#resolve!` re-checks that target before a confirmation (409 `invalid_or_expired_confirmation` when it is gone or no longer visible), and `AssistantActionCard` shows the target and a review notice when the user navigated away. The same snapshot covers the Kanban board and open task (`kanban_board` route; `query.item`, board from `funnel/getSelectedFunnel`), the flow open in the Flow Builder (`query.flow`) and the inbox whose settings are open (`settings_inbox_show`), resolved with the `kanban_board`/`flow_builder` feature flags, `FunnelPolicy`/`KanbanItemPolicy`/`FlowBuilder::FlowPolicy#show?` and `InboxPolicy#update?`; contacts selected in the list live in the contacts store (`contacts/getSelectedContactIds`) and are saved as a `contacts` selection.

Proposal revisions detail: `mega_assistant_actions` has `revision` (default 1) and `supersedes_id` (migration `20261109130000`) and the status `superseded`. `Tools::BaseTool#propose` keeps reusing an identical pending request; another value for a replacing write (PATCH/PUT, conversation or contact labels, conversation assignment) on the same operation, filled path and target creates the next revision through `MegaAssistantAction#supersede_with!` (thread lock, only while pending). `PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/mega_assistant/threads/:thread_id/actions/:id/revise` takes `arguments` (named tools) or `body` (raw API requests), only for keys the original request has or its tool declares, never path ids, a send's `conversation_id` or a bulk change's `body.ids`; the merged request goes through the same validator as the model (422 with issues, 409 `invalid_or_expired_confirmation` when not pending). The stored `request` is never changed in place. `action_preview` returns typed views (`kind`: `message`, `assignment`, `labels`, `task`, `flow`) with names only for records the user can see; `AssistantActionCard` shows them with a revision badge, running/failed/done states and in-place editing of team, agent and labels.

Block contract detail: blocks carry `schema_version` 1, `block_id` (model-chosen `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}` or generated `r<reply_id>-<n>`, unique in the thread), `revision`, `type`, the props `MegaAssistant::BlockSchema::PROPS` allows for that type (mirrored by `blocks/blockRegistry.js`) and an optional `action_id` of the same thread. `present_blocks` updates a block of this or an earlier reply in place with `block_id` + `expected_revision` (`stale_revision` returns the current one; identical content is a no-op), removes one as a tombstone (`removed: true`), and keeps 12 blocks per reply, 250 KB per call and 2 MB per thread. Accepted blocks are saved under the reply lease (`ResponseJob#write_blocks`) so the panel shows them while the reply is generating. `MegaAssistant::BlockPayload` upgrades blocks saved before the contract on read (`schema_version` 0, `legacy-<message_id>-<index>`, revision 0; never written back) and adds `blocks_revision` and `blocks_attempt` to each message, so the store keeps the newest blocks of the newest attempt. Table, chart, metric, timeline and kanban blocks open in the canvas through the same allowlisted components; `html_artifact` stays in its sandboxed iframe.

Interactions detail: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/mega_assistant/threads/:thread_id/interactions` takes `{ interaction: { intent, block_id, revision, payload }, time_zone, screen_context }` with intents `form.submit` (`values` validated against the stored form block: declared fields only, typed per input, required fields), `action.select` (`index` of a prompt item of the stored actions block) and `proposal.review` (`action_id` of a pending proposal; nothing changes on it). `action.modify` is the revise endpoint above. The block is looked up in this thread at its current revision (409 `stale_revision`, 404 `block_not_found`, 422 `invalid_values`/`unknown_intent`/`payload_too_large`). The turn is a user message with a short summary as `content` and the typed record in `mega_assistant_messages.interaction` (jsonb, migration `20261109140000`); it goes through the same path as a typed message (`MegaAssistantTurnConcern`: AI Actions, busy lock, enqueue with time zone and screen context, recovery) and the model reads it as bounded, labeled data. Blocks saved before the contract keep sending free text.

The full-page sidebar separates Pinned from Recents: each section collapses independently, and pinned chats never repeat in Recents. Pinned is shown only when it contains chats; both groups retain newest-activity-first ordering.

Each full-page history row offers Rename, Pin/Unpin and Delete through its three-dot menu, available on hover, keyboard focus and touch. Titles (1–100 trimmed characters) and pins persist. Pinned chats come first, then recent activity descending within each group, with at most 50 chats returned. Only the current user's private assistant chats in the current account can be managed, and the Mega Assistant feature must be enabled. Deletion requires confirmation and permanently removes the chat, messages, actions and attachments. Chats generating a response or executing an action cannot be deleted until finished. Deleting the selected chat starts a new chat and clears its draft and files; managing another chat preserves the current chat and draft.

The history endpoint returns up to 50 conversations across pinned and recent chats, applying pinned-first ordering before the limit; this section is not a paginated archive of all conversations.

The assistant history sidebar starts with New chat, followed by a collapsible Recents section with pinned chats first and activity descending within each group. Opening or renaming an older conversation does not change its activity order; new messages update its position.

Source: `globalConfig/get.brandName` (Custom Branding `BRAND_NAME`), trimmed before display. Empty or whitespace-only values fall back to `Mega`. Vue i18n interpolates `{brandName}` as text; feature and route identifiers remain unchanged.

Assistant-facing UI uses the Custom Branding Brand Name followed by “Assistant” (for example, “Acme Assistant”), including the sidebar, titles, reply placeholder and keyboard help. Empty Brand Name falls back to “Mega Assistant”; internal feature and route identifiers are unchanged.

The pet reads `MEGA_ASSISTANT_PET` from Custom Branding, preserving its proportions and drag controls. It defaults to `/brand-assets/mega_asisstant.png` when the setting is empty and is independent from `LOGO_THUMBNAIL`; a failed image uses the inline pet icon.

The floating Copilot launcher is hidden on the full Mega Assistant page and remains available elsewhere in the dashboard.

The keyboard-help entry is only shown when the current account has the Mega Assistant feature enabled, matching the sidebar. The shortcut is inert when the feature is disabled; opening also requires assistant availability.

Alt + Shift + M toggles the floating assistant: when its chat is open, it closes the chat and hides the pet; otherwise it restores and opens it. In the full page it focuses the existing editor without leaving the conversation.

Alt + Shift + M recalls a hidden pet and opens its chat on dashboard pages where the assistant is available. On the assistant page it focuses the existing editor. The close control has a larger, high-contrast X; the shortcut is listed in keyboard help.

The pet can be hidden with its own X and enabled again from the assistant page header. The floating chat’s expand button opens that page with the same conversation, draft and selected attachments. Visibility is saved in this browser; chat transfer stays in memory and does not survive a reload.

When starting an empty chat, the page centers the welcome and composer. The shared replybox uses the conversation editor’s solid surface and border, with subtle elevation and a visible focus state; once messages appear, it stays at the bottom.

Route: `/app/accounts/:accountId/assistant`, gated by `mega_ai_assistant`. The page checks assistant availability before loading private history; unavailable configuration displays an explanatory state, and failed requests offer retry. Using Expand transfers the conversation, draft and attachments once in memory, checking account and user. Ordinary navigation or a reload does not preserve drafts.

The primary sidebar includes a full assistant page with history and a centered conversation. It shares the rounded attachment composer with the floating panel; the pet is hidden while this page is open.

The initial SDK request contains named contact, conversation, count, inbox and Captain read tools alongside the generic compatibility tools. Discovery filters shared definitions by installed route, account features and current administrator status, then activates an explicit page while retaining no more than 40 named tools. Each named adapter returns the original shared JSON schema and delegates to the existing native controller dispatcher and confirmation lifecycle. Empty or partial searches are not absence proofs. Captain is the platform AI module; read outcomes and proposed changes remain in the private conversation action history.

The conversation stays beside the pet while it moves, flips to available space near screen edges, and keeps both within the viewport. A compact header exposes history and new conversation controls; suggestions populate the composer without sending automatically.

Mega Assistant is a private account assistant with a movable, resizable keyboard-accessible pet and a Copilot-based conversation panel. It discovers account operations, answers using authenticated API results, and proposes changes for explicit confirmation. Counts use API totals rather than page sizes. No MCP server, token or public API entitlement is required.

For OpenAI chat routes, each private thread sends a stable `prompt_cache_key`. The system instructions omit the changing time and active tool list; those values are appended after conversation history. The thread records eligible named tools in call order for the next response. At the 40-tool limit, core tools remain and the oldest added tools are removed. The automatic confirmation outcome is appended as a user message, preserving the shared prompt prefix.

The timeline holds actions created during an executing assistant reply until that reply has completed its text reveal. It then renders the actions after the message; if generation fails, the action remains available.

Providers independently opt into Captain and Mega Assistant. Super Admin account model cards select chat, image/PDF and audio transcription routes per account. Upload up to five images, PDFs, text documents or audio files (20 MB each) with the + button, by dragging and dropping them onto the composer, or by pasting them (for example, a screenshot). Files and histories remain private to their owner. Confirmed actions display their real outcome and cannot be executed twice. Dictation and realtime voice are included (see Voice); telephony is not.

Mega Assistant sends lean base instructions and adds after the conversation only the guides a request needs (`MegaAssistant::Guides`: conversations, messaging, scheduling ("agendar"/"programar" picked by what happens at that time: a scheduled message for one contact (`scheduled_messages_create`, `scheduled_outbound_messages_create` for a phone or email, the conversation one without `inbox_id`), a campaign for an audience, a task of the Tasks module, a calendar event, or snoozing a conversation; when the request fits more than one and the page does not settle it, the assistant asks), files, reports, Captain), also available through the `assistant_guide` tool. Single-call composite tools: `conversations_awaiting_reply`, `contact_overview`, `period_report` and `captain_overview` (plus `captain_assistant_stats_overview` in the catalog). `task_plan` records the parts of a request and raises the lookup allowance (12 + 4 per extra part, up to 28). Thread history and the tool results piled up within a reply are capped by size. Steps store `{name, detail}` (`MegaAssistant::StepDetail`) and billing reads them through `MegaAssistantMessage#step_names`. The `complex` model capability (plans and accounts, the provider's chat models) handles complex requests and is the fallback when a provider rate-limits or fails, after retries of 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 s. The browser time zone travels with each message and confirmation; `PATCH threads/:id/actions/batch` resolves several proposals with a single follow-up. Tool search maps Spanish and Portuguese vocabulary to the catalog's English (`MegaAssistant::Vocabulary`).

Bulk changes record by record: a confirmed `conversations_bulk_update` (or the generic `POST /bulk_actions` with type `Conversation`) no longer goes through that endpoint, which answers 200 and changes the conversations later in `BulkActionsJob` without reporting them one by one. `MegaAssistantAction#resolve!` leaves it `executing` and `MegaAssistant::BulkChangeJob` (2-minute lease, batches of 50) applies `Conversations::BulkChange`, now shared with `BulkActionsJob`, one conversation at a time through `Conversations::PermissionFilterService`, after checking access, the actions plan and the team again. Each outcome is saved in `mega_assistant_action_items` (migration `20261024180200`: `applied` with `changes_made`, `skipped` when nothing changed, `failed` with `error`, unique per record) in the same transaction as the change, so a resumed job never applies a record twice. `result` keeps `total`, `applied`, `skipped`, `failed` and the first 20 failures; the thread payload adds `progress` and the first 50 `items`. The follow-up reply is enqueued when the change finishes and covers every action decided with it (`follow_up_action_ids`); if the thread is busy, the reply in progress reads the recorded outcome. `RecoverRunsJob` resumes an expired bulk change until 3 workers died on it, then fails it keeping its counts. The panel keeps refreshing while an action runs without blocking the composer, and `AssistantActionCard.vue` shows the progress, the summary and each conversation. The MCP/REST contract of `conversations_bulk_update` is unchanged.

Typed stored selections: `select_records` (`MegaAssistant::RecordSelector`) selects conversations (status, inboxes, teams, assignee, priority, labels, creation and last activity dates), contacts (name/email/phone, labels, creation date) or messages (text through the trigram index, incoming/outgoing/note, inboxes, dates), plus `newest` when the user asks for a number of records, through `Conversations::PermissionFilterService` (messages only in visible conversations; contacts behind `ContactPolicy#search?`), and saves up to 5,000 ids newest first in `mega_assistant_selections` (migration `20261024180300`) with the exact total. `from_selection_id` derives the linked records of another type (messages → conversations → contacts, contacts → conversations → messages) and its filters narrow them. `selection_rows` reads a selection 50 per page through the same visibility checks, and `selection_bulk_update` proposes one `conversations_bulk_update` for every conversation of a selection (beyond the 500 ids a direct call takes), validated against its schema; it counts as a proposal and is only supplied when the plan allows actions. Each reply lists the last 10 selections of the thread after the history so later turns can chain from them.

Background reviews with findings: `conversations_review` creates a `MegaAssistantReview` (migration `20261024180400`, with `mega_assistant_review_findings`) over the newest 100 conversations of a conversations selection (`PER_RUN`; calling it again with `review_id` adds the next 100 once the review finished) and returns at once. `MegaAssistant::ReviewJob` works under the review lease (3 minutes) in batches of 10, enqueuing itself for the next batch; before each batch it checks the assistant access and the AI Actions balance (`MegaAssistantReview.credits_left?`, stops as `ai_actions_exhausted`), and reads conversations through `Conversations::PermissionFilterService`. `MegaAssistant::ConversationReviewer` makes one structured call per conversation (`Llm::CapabilityClient#chat_with_attachment` with a JSON schema, on the account Mega Assistant chat route) over the opening 3 and newest 12 messages within 12,000 characters, and keeps only evidence ids of messages it was shown; its calls run in `Llm::UsageTracker.track` with the starting reply as reference, so they add to that turn cost; each batch that reviewed at least one conversation charges one AI Action inside the same usage context (`mega_assistant_review`, linked to its model calls), a batch the model could not review costs nothing, and the follow-up turn is not charged. Findings (`reviewed` with matched/category/reason/evidence, or `failed` with the error) are unique per conversation, so a resumed job never reviews one twice; `RecoverRunsJob` resumes an expired review until 3 workers died on the same batch, then fails it as `interrupted`. When it finishes, `ResponseJob` runs with `trigger_review_id` and a directive to answer from `review_findings` (coverage: selected, reviewed, matched, failed, remaining) and offer to continue. `select_records` takes `from_review_id` (+ `matched_only`) to chain from the conversations found. The thread payload lists the last 20 `reviews` with their progress; `AssistantReviewCard.vue` shows them and the panel keeps refreshing while one runs.

Cost per turn and status guide: for account administrators the thread payload adds `cost` (`usd`, `calls`) to each assistant message, the sum of the `llm_usage_events` whose `reference` is that reply (the reply itself, its fallback and any review it started), read in one grouped query bounded by account and date; the panel shows it next to the reply time and members never receive it. `MegaAssistant::Guides` gains the `records` topic (also in `MegaAssistant::RequestRouter::GUIDE_TOPICS` so the Decision Router can pick it): what open, pending, snoozed and resolved mean, that priority, labels, assignee and team are set by people or automations and do not show what a customer needs, and the method for many records (select_records with only the filters the user stated, exact totals, chaining, conversations_review to judge content instead of status or priority, selection_bulk_update for one change).

The shared `AccountActions::Definitions` preserves curated schemas; `AccountActions::Catalog` discovers exact v1/v2/account/profile routes. Native execution uses Warden identity and real controller/Enterprise callbacks, restores Current, recursively redacts secrets and validates attachment ownership. Assistant self-management routes are excluded to prevent recursion; unavailable domain features remain real errors. Conversation/outbound/scheduled media adapters support private uploads, safe HTTPS ingestion and base64. Non-JSON exports become private attachments. Each write proposal expires after 15 minutes and is claimed before execution; interrupted executions remain auditable and are never retried automatically. Account settings store `mega_assistant_models`; migration preserves the former global chat selection. RubyLLM content attachments share the tool-enabled chat, text is bounded and audio transcripts are cached. Historical context is limited to 30 messages and 20 recorded action states. No live-provider verification is implied.

Version: Enterprise

Twilio Voice recordings remain attached to `Call`; an explicit account setting enables their idempotent transcription, message broadcast, and search indexing.
Last updated: September 1, 2026
Language: English

## Captain provider capabilities

AI provider cards summarize model count, verified capabilities and credit warnings. Models and tests opens a dialog with each model's assigned uses, check status and saved errors, without expanding the grid. Test, edit and protected deletion actions remain available.

Reasoning and tools work together on every provider: each model gets its reasoning in the form its API accepts (a level, a thinking budget, or nothing when it does not reason), and OpenAI models are called through the Responses API. OpenAI-compatible providers take a preset: OpenRouter, xAI, DeepSeek, Mistral, Perplexity, Ollama and GPUStack run on their own integration, while Groq, Together, Fireworks, DeepInfra, Cerebras, SambaNova, Qwen, Z.ai, vLLM, LM Studio or any custom gateway connect as generic gateways whose API (Responses or Chat Completions) the capability test detects per model.

Provider forms are centered. A visible bulk-add button opens a modal to paste model names and select capabilities; Cancel or Escape closes it without adding rows.

Add/edit provider pages use a two-column connection section and a compact model table. Bulk entry accepts newline- or comma-separated names and shared capabilities; existing names are merged without duplicating rows.

Legacy catalog selections remain available for their original features after editing an imported provider. New provider-qualified selections still require assigned, verified capabilities. Legacy PDF/audio clients resolve current native OpenAI credentials, falling back to installation settings when no active native provider exists. Audio prefers the account OpenAI integration; Captain fallback requires its feature and available quota. Call recordings retain their specialized path.

A one-time upgrade repair restores missing `whisper-1`, `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` and `text-embedding-3-small` entries on the native provider with slug `openai`. It preserves credentials, custom models, existing assignments, test results and account selections. Restored entries are configured, not automatically verified; provider-qualified selection still requires testing. The repair can restore a previously removed base entry, but does not re-add it on ordinary saves. Models marked coming soon are not added.

Models OpenAI is retiring (the GPT-4.1 series, `gpt-4o`, `whisper-1`, `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` and `gpt-4o-transcribe`) are replaced by GPT-6 Luna for text and GPT Transcribe for audio. Captain features default to Luna (Copilot uses GPT-5.1) and run it without reasoning unless AI Providers sets another effort for the model. A migration moves the choices saved on accounts, plans and installation settings to Luna or GPT Transcribe; a provider choice only moves to the same provider when that provider already validated the replacement, and Mega Assistant keeps its choice until it is validated. OpenAI providers get both models priced (GPT Transcribe US$0.0045 per minute) and unvalidated: test them in AI Providers. Diarized call transcription stays on `gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize` because OpenAI has not published a replacement. The Captain and AI feature lists in `config/llm.yml` also offer `gpt-6-sol` (GPT-6 Sol) and `gpt-6.1-terra` (GPT-6.1 Terra), both with `reasoning_effort: none` by default.

FAQ regeneration preserves existing responses on errors or empty results. It replaces only unedited responses when valid results are available, in a transaction that rolls back replacement if any insertion fails. Legacy PDF generation checks the remote file and, only on a 404, uploads the retained original again using the same credentials as generation. Permission, credit, or connection errors do not trigger another upload; if the original is missing, previous knowledge is retained.

Existing OpenAI file IDs belong to the original provider project. A credential rotation to a different project does not transfer those files; this change does not rewrite stored account selections or migrate files.

Provider forms list each model once, with Chat, PDF, Audio and Embeddings checkboxes. Tests run only for selected capabilities. Existing selections and verified results are preserved; models without a selected capability remain visible with a prompt to select one. Changing credentials or the endpoint requires revalidation.

Capability checks use a valid one-page PDF and a one-second PCM WAV. The silent audio sample checks file acceptance, not transcription accuracy.

The results dialog retains sanitized provider errors, with API keys and bearer tokens filtered; older generic errors remain unchanged. Not tested, failed checks and verified capabilities are distinct states.

Tests persist a sanitized `failure_reason` for provider-reported insufficient credit or quota exhaustion, displayed in Super Admin. Raw provider messages are not rendered. Failed checks no longer produce a success notice.

`LlmProvider.capabilities` stores explicitly declared model lists for `chat`, `embeddings`, `transcription`, and `documents`. Feature routing only accepts provider-qualified selections that declare the needed capability. Registered non-chat requests use an isolated RubyLLM credential context; legacy OpenAI file IDs and clients retain their existing path. The `decisions` capability marks System One API models (`POST {api_base}/systemone`, OpenAI-compatible providers only); its test sends one `noul` question and requires a probability back. A feature of capability `decisions` accepts models with `decisions` or `chat` (`Llm::Models::ROUTE_CAPABILITIES`, `LlmProvider#supports_route_capability?`), and the installation global model applies too.

## 1. Purpose

This document explains how core MEGA features are implemented from a technical perspective.
It complements [features.en.md](features.en.md), which is focused on commercial and presentation use.

## 2. Base stack and architecture

- Main backend: Ruby on Rails.
- Dashboard frontend: Vue 3.
- Enterprise overlay: extensions and overrides under enterprise.
- Async processing: Sidekiq background jobs.
- Optional Sidekiq-to-CloudWatch queue metrics: `ENABLE_SIDEKIQ_CLOUDWATCH` starts reporting only in the Sidekiq server process. It uses an instance role by default (or dedicated `SIDEKIQ_CLOUDWATCH_AWS_*` credentials), and publishes QueueLatency, QueueSize, EnqueuedJobs, and Utilization on a validated positive interval.
- Queues without a consumer: `Internal::SidekiqOrphanQueuesService` lists queues that hold jobs but are missing from every live `Sidekiq::ProcessSet` entry (for example, a queue new in this release that the `-q` lists of a split Sidekiq do not include). The Super Admin dashboard shows them with size and latency (`sidekiq_orphan_queues` in `GET /super_admin/stats/performance`), and `Internal::CheckSidekiqOrphanQueuesJob` (sidekiq-cron, every 5 min, `critical` queue) reports those waiting 5 minutes or more to the log and Sentry.
- Realtime layer: Action Cable for conversations, rooms, and boards.
- Persistence: PostgreSQL as primary database.
- LLM layer: `ruby_llm` 2.1.1 and `ai-agents` 0.14.0 from our fork, pinned to a commit, with our improvements (`llm_context`, caching and extra handoff arguments). Every tool passed to `with_tools` is a `RubyLLM::Tool` subclass (none deferred). Mega stores no thinking signatures (Captain and Mega Assistant rebuild history from messages), so there is nothing to migrate. RubyLLM 2.1 reclassifies some 400/403 responses as `UnauthorizedError`, `RateLimitError`, `OverloadedError` or `PaymentRequiredError` and raises `ContentFilterError` when Gemini blocks an audio file: audio transcription treats `ContentFilterError` as a rejected file, Super Admin capability probes (`SuperAdmin::LlmProvidersController`) take `ContentFilterError` and `OverloadedError` as expected results (500 before), and the Mega Assistant fallback also fires on the reclassified errors, which are no longer reported as failures.
- Development file watching: the `listen` and `spring-watcher-listen` gems are removed; development uses the Rails default `ActiveSupport::FileUpdateChecker` instead of `EventedFileUpdateChecker`.
- API security: permission policies and role-based controls.
- Settings side panels: agents, teams, labels, macros, SLAs, custom roles, assignment policies, webhooks, dashboard apps and integration hooks use one `SidePanel` per screen (view / form modes, plus members for teams), mounted through `settings/useSettingsPanel.js` with the classes in `settings/helpers/sidePanelClasses.js`, like chat rooms, custom attributes and MCP servers. Deep links `?create=1` and `?<item>=ID`, with the old create/edit routes kept as redirects; each host ignores the query unless its own route is active, and list routes set `meta.reuseOnQueryChange` because `SettingsWrapper` keeps pages alive. `LabelDialog` stays available for `LabelDropdown` and shares `LabelForm` with the panel; integration hooks have no edit (the API has none).

## 3. Functional domains

### 3.1 Messaging and voice channels

- Widget and public pages: the widget's email request is sent only once (only for a contact's incoming message, with a lock and re-check in `MessageTemplates::Template::EmailCollect`); the widget `DirectUploadsController` skips CSRF, so the widget, the help center portal and the CSAT survey no longer emit `csrf_meta_tags` or set a session cookie; the widget context loads with `eager_load` (inbox, account, contact) and agent availability without one query per member. The dashboard, the widget, the Super Admin login and layout, and onboarding carry `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` (`robots.txt` still excludes `/widget`). OpenSearch message search loads results inside the `rescue` (Searchkick relations are lazy) and on any Faraday, Searchkick or OpenSearch error falls back to SQL and reports it.
- Email (reliability): `Imap::ImapMailbox` takes an advisory lock per inbox and Message-ID inside the transaction and re-checks for the email without the query cache, so two overlapping fetches (our job lock can expire on large batches) no longer save the same email twice; an empty Reply-To (`<"">`) is ignored and From is used. In professional mode the sender is built with `Mail::Address` (`format_email_with_name`), which quotes a display name with commas or other special characters instead of splitting the header. SMTP can be configured while IMAP is off (inboxes that receive through forwarding); turning IMAP off no longer turns SMTP off. Conversation search also matches the subject (`additional_attributes.mail_subject`, email inboxes only) and the conversation header shows the subject for email conversations.
- New inbox wizard (`/settings/inboxes/new`, `InboxChannels.vue`): three steps (Channel · Configure · Agents) with `WizardSteps` and the sticky `WizardFormFooter`. `useInboxChannels.js` groups channels (messaging, web and API, email and phone) with search and reuses `useChannelConfig().isConfigured` for the Facebook, Instagram and TikTok credentials. The email provider lives in `?provider=` and is only accepted when configured. The Website form sends `reply_time` and stores bubble position, type and launcher title in `widgetBubble_<id>`; `FinishSetup.vue` adds them to the script as `window.chatwootSettings` when they differ from the defaults. The agents step uses the same `TagInput` picker as the Collaborators tab. `MigrateInboxModal.vue` uses `Dialog` with searchable selects, an irreversibility warning and contact, conversation and message counters; the migration and polling logic is unchanged.
- Public inbox identifiers are resolved in the frontend by channel type and shown beside the name in the sidebar, list, and settings header. Facebook page IDs and X profile IDs are opaque routing values and are therefore excluded from display, while remaining in the inbox payload for provider operations. Search combines name/type matches with displayed normalized identifiers without duplicating results, while `bdi` preserves mixed text direction.
- Inbox conversations link: `InboxRow` (Settings › Inboxes, desktop, for everyone who sees the list; settings and delete stay for managers) and `InboxSettingsHeader` add **View conversations**, which opens the `inbox_dashboard` route of that inbox.
- Cloud and Twilio WhatsApp look up the exact phone first, then Brazilian ninth-digit candidates only for legacy eight-digit subscribers starting with 6–9. Lookup is account-scoped and does not change the stored phone. Names containing an equivalent phone can be enriched from the profile. Cloud calls use the same builder; historical source_id variants and Evolution/WAHA/Uazapi rules are preserved.
- WhatsApp Cloud API: official channel with templates and delivery events; outside the 24-hour customer service window, the service locally rejects automation free-form messages without template parameters before contacting the provider.
- WhatsApp Cloud Direct Send (Meta beta): with `provider_config.enable_direct_send` on (`Channel::Whatsapp#direct_send_enabled?`, switch in the inbox Configuration page), ReplyBox and the new conversation form (`NewConversation/components/ActionButtons.vue`, which sends `message.direct_send` without attachments) turn the WhatsApp button into a "Template / Direct Send" picker (`ReplyBottomPanel.vue` with `DropdownMenu`, `WhatsAppOptions.vue` with a first choice step); Direct Send opens `WhatsAppMessageDialog.vue`, which only sends utility messages: buttons (none, up to 3 replies, one link, or "Call on WhatsApp" when the inbox has calling on, `voiceCallsEnabled` prop) and an optional header side by side, a text, image, video or document header (`MediaUrlField`), a body up to 1024 characters, a 60-character footer (not with the call button), a delivery time limit (`ttl_seconds`: Meta's default, 12 h, 6 h, 1 h or 30 min) and a live preview; the template name (`template_name`) and authentication are API-only. The rules live in `Whatsapp::DirectSendConfig`, used by `Messages::MessageBuilder` when the message is created and by `OutboundMessages::CreateService` when validating: `category` `utility` (default) or `authentication` (plain text only, no `template_name`), `ttl_seconds` 30 to 43200 (utility) or 30 to 900 (authentication), `template_name` `^[a-z0-9_]+$` up to 512, a 60-character `header` or `media` {`type` image/video/document, public `url`, `filename`} only with buttons, `buttons` (1 to 3 different titles of 20), `link` {`text`, `url`} or `voice_call` {`text`, `ttl_minutes` 1 to 43200} (only one of the three; the call button takes a text header and no footer, and needs `provider_config.enable_voice_calls`); anything off answers 422 `{ error, code }` (`CustomExceptions::Whatsapp::DirectSendInvalid`; codes such as `invalid_ttl`, `invalid_template_name`, `conflicting_buttons`, `header_needs_buttons`, `authentication_text_only`, `voice_call_not_enabled`). It is stored in `additional_attributes.direct_send` and `Whatsapp::SendOnWhatsappService` sends it through `WhatsappCloudService#send_direct_send_message` (`category`, `ttl_seconds`, `direct_send_config.template_name`; text, `button`, `cta_url` or `voice_call` through `Whatsapp::InteractivePayloadBuilder`) inside or outside the window; authentication goes with `to` only (a contact with only a BSUID fails with `errors.whatsapp.direct_send_authentication_phone_required`), and if Direct Send is off for the inbox it fails with `errors.whatsapp.direct_send_disabled`. The Meta error catalog adds 132021 (template name taken), 138000 (calling off) and 139200 (Direct Send blocked). The rest of the flow is unchanged: free text, automations, API and scheduled messages still need a template outside the window. Over the API: `direct_send` on `POST /conversations/:id/messages`, `message.direct_send` on `POST /conversations`, and `message.type: "direct_send"` with `message.direct_send` on `POST /outbound_messages`, which answers `direct_send_not_enabled` when the inbox does not have it on (the controller now permits `message.direct_send` and `message.interactive`, which it used to drop); authentication messages are API-only. It is documented in Swagger (`direct_send_payload` and examples for buttons with a document, call, authentication and image with a link), in the Postman collection and in the Super Admin API docs, and in the `conversation_message_send` and `outbound_messages_create` MCP tools (`DIRECT_SEND_SCHEMA`).
