Eligibility comes from `phone_number_health.messaging_limit_tier` (`Channel::Whatsapp::DIRECT_SEND_ELIGIBLE_TIERS` = `TIER_100K`, `TIER_UNLIMITED`): `Whatsapp::HealthService` returns `direct_send_eligible` and Account Health shows the Direct Send row; the inbox exposes `direct_send_eligible` to admins (the switch only renders when true) and `direct_send_enabled` to everyone; `Channel::Whatsapp#direct_send_enabled?` requires both the switch and eligibility, so an inbox that stops qualifying stops sending through Direct Send in the UI, API and MCP. In development only, `WHATSAPP_DIRECT_SEND_FORCE_ELIGIBLE=true` treats every Cloud inbox as eligible to try the UI with a sandbox number (Meta still rejects the send unless the WABA is in the beta). Template sync asks for the `source` field only on Direct Send inboxes and stores it in `metadata.source`; every template picker (reply box, new conversation, campaigns, scheduled messages and Kanban automations) hides `AUTO_GENERATED` ones, and Settings → Templates lists them with a Direct Send tag, their stats and no edit, delete or visibility switch (Meta doesn't allow editing or deleting them). The mixed 10-button format and the authentication copy-code button are not used (Meta doesn't document their format), and `ACCOUNT_RESTRICTION`, `template_correct_category_detection` webhooks and the status `template_id` are not processed.
- WhatsApp Cloud interactive messages: `content_attributes.interactive` (`type` button | list | cta_url | location_request | carousel | pix, `header`, `media` {type, url, filename}, `footer`, `buttons`, `link`, `list.button` + `list.sections[].rows[]`, `cards[]` {media, body, buttons \| link}, `pix` {code, merchant_name, key, key_type, amount, reference_id}), normalized by `Messages::MessageBuilder#interactive_params` from the `interactive` param (messages, `type: interactive` on Outbound Messages with `validate_interactive!` and the `interactive_not_supported` error outside Cloud). `WhatsappCloudService#send_interactive_message` builds Meta's payload with `Whatsapp::InteractivePayloadBuilder`, shared with Direct Send: media header by public URL (button/cta_url only), `carousel` with `card_index`, Pix as `order_details` / `review_and_pay` with `pix_dynamic_code` (`payment_type: br`, BRL in cents, `reference_id` defaults to `mega-<id>`). The Pix code comes from the business' bank/PSP; Mega doesn't confirm payments. WhatsApp Cloud only: other providers fail the message with `errors.whatsapp.interactive_unsupported` instead of sending just the body. It is a session message: without `template_params` it takes the normal path and fails like free text when the window is closed. `Liquidable#process_liquid_in_interactive` resolves variables in every field (and in Direct Send's footer and buttons too). UI: `WhatsAppMessageDialog.vue` (`direct_send` / `interactive` modes, `InputWithVariables` / `TextAreaWithVariables`), option in the `ReplyBottomPanel` WhatsApp button menu only with `can_reply` and in `NewConversation/components/WhatsAppOptions.vue` only with `can_reply_without_template` (the contact's 24h window, `message.interactive` on `POST /conversations`, no attachments); on phones the dialog sheet uses `BottomSheet` with `stacked` so it sits above the form; bubble `components-next/message/bubbles/Interactive.vue`. Schemas `interactive_payload` (Swagger) and `INTERACTIVE_SCHEMA` (MCP). Files are uploaded through `/upload` (`whatsappMessage/MediaUrlField.vue`); `CarouselEditor.vue` and `PixFields.vue` complete the dialog. Out of scope: Flows, catalog/products, address and Pix order status updates.
- WAHA interactive messages: `Whatsapp::WahaInteractiveGate` (same pattern as `WahaVoiceCallsGate`) shows the `waha_interactive_messages` account feature when `WAHA_INTERACTIVE_MESSAGES=true` or an active WAHA server has `extra_config.interactive_messages` (switch in Super Admin → WhatsApp Providers, pill on the index and status dot on the detail page); per inbox it requires the channel's server to support it and the account to have the feature, and the inbox exposes `waha_interactive_enabled`. Types in `content_attributes.interactive`: `button` (titles), `cta` (`actions[]` {type url/call/copy, text, url/phone_number/copy_code}, max 5), `list`, `carousel` (`cards[]` with `media`, `header`, `body`, `footer` and `buttons`, `link` or `actions`), `pix` {merchant_name, key, key_type}, `order` {items[{name, amount, quantity}], discount, total, currency, payment_type, pix, instructions, reference_id}, `poll` {options, multiple_answers}, `location` {latitude, longitude}, `event` {start_time, end_time, location, description, extra_guests_allowed} and `button_reply` {id, reply_to}; `content` is the body, list description, poll question, place title, event name or selected button text (optional for `pix` and `order`, where it is the note). `Messages::MessageBuilder#interactive_params` normalizes the new fields (`INTERACTIVE_NESTED_FIELDS`, `interactive_actions`, `interactive_cards`) and `WahaService#send_interactive_message` builds the request with `Whatsapp::WahaInteractivePayloadBuilder`: `/sendButtons` (header, body and footer always strings; `headerImage` with the real image, video or document mimetype), `/sendList`, `/sendCarousel`, `/sendPix`, `/sendReviewAndPay` (amounts in cents, `referenceId` defaults to `mega-<id>`), `/sendPoll`, `/sendLocation`, `/{session}/events` (unix seconds) and `/send/buttons/reply`, with the replied message as `reply_to`. They are not offered in groups (`ReplyBox#isGroupConversation`, `source_id` ending in `@g.us`) and through the API they fail with `errors.whatsapp.interactive_group_unsupported`, because WAHA answers 422 "Buttons message can only be sent to a direct message chat". WAHA errors (NestJS shape `{ message, error, statusCode }`) now show their `message` instead of the HTTP reason (`WahaService#parsed_error`). Without the gate `Whatsapp::SendOnWhatsappService` marks them with `errors.whatsapp.interactive_unsupported`. Outbound Messages validates through `OutboundMessages::WahaInteractiveValidator` (codes `interactive_not_enabled`, `invalid_interactive_type`, `interactive_action_required`). UI: `WhatsAppMessageDialog.vue` takes `provider` (`waha` from `ReplyBox` and from new conversation `ActionButtons`, which adds a WhatsApp icon) and reuses the same layout with `CtaButtonsEditor`, `PollFields`, `LocationFields`, `EventFields`, `OrderFields` (`orderTotals.js`), `PixFields` (`dynamic: false`) and `CarouselEditor` (`minCards`, `imageOnly`); GOWS engine notice for carousel, Pix, order and event. The `Interactive.vue` bubble renders CTA (link, `tel:`, copy), poll, location (open map), event, order and Pix without amount. Swagger: `interactive_payload` + `interactive_action`, 10 `waha_interactive_*` examples on messages and Outbound Messages (Postman and the Super Admin API docs are regenerated by `rake swagger:build`); The account MCP and Mega Assistant use `INTERACTIVE_SCHEMA` / `INTERACTIVE_ACTIONS_SCHEMA` on `conversation_message_send` and `outbound_messages_create`; `conversation_message_send` accepts an `interactive` without text (WAHA Pix or order). Video and document headers go through `headerImage` with their mimetype (not verified live). Incoming answers: `IncomingMessageWahaService` turns taps from `buttonsResponseMessage`, `templateButtonReplyMessage`, `listResponseMessage` (title and description) and `interactiveResponseMessage` (body or `nativeFlowResponseMessage.paramsJSON`) into text, which used to be dropped as `unsupported`; the `poll.vote` (chosen options) and `event.response` (`conversations.whatsapp_interactive.event_response.*`) events are always subscribed (`WahaService::INTERACTIVE_WEBHOOK_EVENTS`, also when the inbox keeps its own event list; existing sessions pick them up when the inbox is saved) and come in as replies to the poll or event; button and list taps quote the original message through `contextInfo.stanzaID`.
- UazAPI interactive messages: `Whatsapp::UazapiInteractiveGate` (same triad as WAHA: `UAZAPI_INTERACTIVE_MESSAGES`, `extra_config.interactive_messages` on UazAPI servers via `WhatsappProviderServer.interactive_messages_enabled('uazapi')` and the `INTERACTIVE_PROVIDER_TYPES` switch, account feature `uazapi_interactive_messages`); the inbox exposes `uazapi_interactive_enabled`. Types in `content_attributes.interactive`: `button`, `cta`, `list`, `poll` (+ `selectable_count`), `carousel` (cards mix `buttons`, `actions` and `link`; media image/video/document optional), `pix` {key, key_type, merchant_name optional}, `payment_request` (`payment` {amount in BRL, item_name, invoice_number, pix, boleto_code, payment_link, order_message_id}; `header` = title, `media` = attached document), `event` (+ `join_link`, `schedule_call`, `has_reminder`, `reminder_offset_sec`, `location` string or {name, address, latitude, longitude}) and `location_request`. `UazapiService#send_interactive_message` uses `Whatsapp::UazapiInteractivePayloadBuilder`: `/send/menu` (button/cta/list/poll as `choices`, `|` and line breaks stripped; header media in `file` + `mimetype` + `docName`), `/send/carousel`, `/send/pix-button`, `/send/request-payment`, `/send/event`, `/send/location-button`, with `replyid` and `delay`; only type and endpoint are logged. Groups are allowed (UazAPI documents `@g.us` on every endpoint). Outbound Messages validates through the provider table `CreateService::PROVIDER_INTERACTIVE` and `OutboundMessages::UazapiInteractiveValidator`. Order updates: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/conversations/:cid/messages/:mid/payment_status` (`Messages::PaymentStatusesController` → `Whatsapp::UazapiPaymentStatusService` → UazAPI `/message/payment/status`), stored in `content_attributes.payment_status`, MCP action `message_payment_status_update` (write tool, confirmed in Mega Assistant). Incoming answers: `IncomingMessageUazapiService#uazapi_interactive_reply_text` turns `vote` (poll and `EventResponseMessage`, translated with `conversations.whatsapp_interactive.event_response.*`) and `buttonOrListid` into text when `text` is empty, and links poll votes through `pollCreationMessageKey`; the vote format is not verified live. UI: `WhatsAppMessageDialog` with `provider: 'uazapi'` (media-only header for buttons, `PaymentRequestFields.vue`, `EventFields` join link), `ReplyBox` / `ActionButtons` `uazapi_interactive_enabled`; `Interactive.vue` renders the payment request, its order status and update form, and document cards. `MessageList` now keeps `content_attributes.interactive` out of the deep camelCase, which also fixes WAHA/Cloud bubble fields such as `pix.merchant_name` and `event.start_time`. Turning an account feature (`Account#refresh_inbox_cache`) or a server switch (`WhatsappProviderServer#refresh_inbox_caches`) on or off renews the `inbox` cache key, so the dashboard refetches inboxes and shows or hides the option without a reload (WAHA had the same gap). Per-inbox sale: `waha_interactive_messages` and `uazapi_interactive_messages` are in `Billing::Addon::PER_INBOX_FEATURES`; the inbox switch lives in `provider_config['interactive_messages']` (`Whatsapp::InteractiveInboxSwitch`, extended by both gates; Enterprise `Enterprise::Whatsapp::InteractiveInboxSwitch` requires it and counts the inboxes switched on when units are bought per inbox, `Billing::AddonGrants.in_use`), changed through `GET/PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:id/inboxes/:inbox_id/interactive_messages` (`addon_quota_exhausted` without a free unit) from `WhatsappInteractiveMessagesSetting.vue` in WAHA and UazAPI inbox settings. An add-on is not offered when the account already has the feature on another way. Poll votes (WAHA `poll.vote` and UazAPI `PollUpdateMessage`): when Mega sent the poll, `Whatsapp::PollVoteRecorder` keeps each voter's latest choice in the poll's `content_attributes.poll_votes` (name, contact and picture; an empty vote removes it) instead of adding a reply, and the bubble shows it with `ChatRoomPollBubble` in `readonly` mode (bars, percentages and "View votes"); votes on polls Mega did not send still come in as replies. The agent can also vote from the bubble with the inbox number (`POST .../messages/:id/poll_vote`, `Whatsapp::PollVoteService`): WAHA `POST /api/sendPollVote` (several options, an empty list takes the vote back) and UazAPI `POST /send/text` with `replyid` and `>N` (one option per request, no taking it back); our vote, and our own votes made from the phone, are kept as voter `me`.
- Mark chats as read on UazAPI: `provider_config['mark_as_read']` (off by default) is saved with `PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/inboxes/:inbox_id/whatsapp_uazapi/read_receipts`, which writes only that column, without the credentials check or the webhook reconfiguration; a missing or non-boolean `mark_as_read` gets a 422. `Whatsapp::MarkChatReadJob` (`low` queue, one call every 10 s per conversation through a Redis lock) is enqueued from `update_last_seen` when there are unread messages and from `Whatsapp::SendOnWhatsappService` when the reply answers an incoming message (the last earlier public message is incoming). `Channel::Whatsapp#read_messages` calls `POST /chat/read` (`number` = chat JID, `read: true`) with the same recipient as sending (`SendOnWhatsappService.recipient_identifier_for`: current phone, `@g.us` group or `@lid`). WAHA does not use this option: it still sends seen before every message.
- Interactive reply ids (Cloud API, WAHA, UazAPI): a contact's tap on a list row or reply button is stored on the incoming message as `content_attributes.interactive_reply` {`type` list \| button, `id`, `title`}, so the `message_created` webhook and the messages API tell the option apart by the id it was sent with (rows and buttons without an `id` come back with their title as `id`); `content` keeps the title. Shared builder `Whatsapp::InteractiveReply.attributes`; sources: Cloud `interactive.list_reply` / `interactive.button_reply` and template quick replies (`button.payload`) in `IncomingMessageBaseService#cloud_interactive_reply`, WAHA `listResponseMessage.singleSelectReply.selectedRowID`, `buttonsResponseMessage.selectedButtonID` / `templateButtonReplyMessage.selectedId` and `nativeFlowResponseMessage.paramsJSON.id` (`IncomingMessageWahaService#waha_interactive_reply`), UazAPI `buttonOrListid` (`#uazapi_interactive_reply`, not for poll or event votes). `IncomingMessageBaseService#apply_content_attributes` keeps the quoted message id when content attributes are assigned. Evolution and NotificaMe are not covered yet. WAHA lists without header now go out with the list button as `message.title` (WAHA rejected an empty title). Documented in Swagger (`message` / `message_detailed` schemas, Outbound Messages description and examples), Postman and the Super Admin API docs. Reply buttons (`buttons` of type button and of carousel cards) take a title or `{ id, title }` on all three providers: `Whatsapp::InteractivePayloadBuilder.reply_option` / `.reply_options` normalize them for the Cloud, WAHA and UazAPI builders (UazAPI swaps an id that looks like an action prefix for `option_N`), Outbound Messages requires a title on each button and WAHA/UazAPI also reject repeated ids, and the bubble shows the title (OpenAPI schema `interactive_reply_button`). Direct Send buttons are still titles only.
- Cloud registration recovery: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/whatsapp/phone_registrations/request_code` requests SMS/VOICE; `/verify_code` validates the `code` field; `POST /phone_registrations` sends only `pin` to `/register`. Inbox update permission is required and Meta errors are preserved. Received codes are never stored as PINs. Without a stored PIN, setup configures webhooks and leaves registration awaiting user action. PINs are saved after successful registration; `133005` directs PIN recovery in Meta and attempt limits are never treated as success. Coexistence skips this registration. Explicit connection refresh reads the actual `status` without reusing cache, and business enrichment queries `waba_id` with a legacy-ID fallback. References: [registration](https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/business-messaging/whatsapp/business-phone-numbers/registration/), [verification and registration](https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/business-messaging/whatsapp/solution-providers/registering-phone-numbers/), and [Meta errors](https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/business-messaging/whatsapp/support/error-codes/). If registration is accepted but webhook setup fails, the response is `422 webhook_setup_failed`, the accepted PIN is retained, and `webhook_pending` remains; a subsequent attempt with a connected number repairs only the webhook.
- Mega Hub for Meta: optional Super Admin mode to connect WhatsApp, Messenger, and Instagram using shared Hub apps; the Hub credentials block is configured in Super Admin → Mega Hub, and created inboxes still send through native services and receive relayed webhook events. WhatsApp account health validates the Hub forward rather than comparing Meta's Hub callback with MEGA's direct callback, and only reports a webhook configuration failure when the forward is confirmed invalid. The finish screen treats Mega Hub like embedded signup and does not expose manual webhook URL or verification-token instructions. Hub credentials are shared by the whole installation, so each connection sends the dashboard origin and a reference that identifies the installation and account (`MegaHub::ConnectReference`); the sync only creates inboxes for the channels the popup reports or that carry this account's reference, refuses a channel another account connected (unless this account already has its inbox, so a `FRONTEND_URL` host change does not lock it out), and refreshes only the account's own inboxes otherwise. Deleting an inbox releases its Hub channel (`MegaHub::ReleaseChannelJob`) only when it carries this account's reference and no other inbox of the installation uses the same Meta id; Hubs without references leave it connected.
- WhatsApp Cloud connection health: manual-token failures are surfaced without blocking inbound webhook processing, while embedded signup still uses the reauthorization flow.
- WhatsApp Cloud phone health persists its available phone-level data when optional WABA business enrichment fails, retains the last business metadata, and records the enrichment error separately.
- Account Health fetches the WhatsApp business profile on demand for Cloud API inboxes and returns it without persisting it. A profile lookup failure is logged and does not hide core phone or business health data.
- Embedded-signup WhatsApp Cloud inboxes can use a feature-flagged guided manual migration to a customer-owned Meta app; the flow validates WABA, phone-number, and token credentials before updating the connection, while the reauthorization alert remains visible when required.
- Guided manual WhatsApp setup creates a new Cloud inbox from a validated WABA ID, Phone Number ID, and permanent access token. It stores the WABA under both compatibility keys, explicitly configures the phone-level callback, exposes callback/subscription checks, and avoids the generic post-create webhook callback.
- On Cloud, `whatsapp_embedded_signup_inbox_creation` gates only new embedded-signup inbox creation. Administrators can reconfigure or reauthorize existing embedded-signup WhatsApp Cloud inboxes without a feature flag; `whatsapp_reconfigure` remains deprecated to preserve stored flag positions.
- Embedded signup accepts WhatsApp Business App coexistence completions without a `phone_number_id`; it forwards `is_coexistence` and `is_business_app_onboarding` so the authorization service configures the channel and webhooks for that flow.
- WhatsApp Evolution, WAHA, and Uazapi: alternative providers with multimedia and group support.
- WAHA group-message edits reuse the group-member mention composer. Stored mention Markdown, or edited `@phone` text resolved from loaded group members, restores the member name in the editor; the timeline uses the same resolution for display. A valid selected member is sent through the native WAHA edit request as normalized text plus `mentions` JIDs. Direct chats, unsupported providers, and malformed mention markup are rejected.
- Incoming group edits from Evolution, WAHA, and Uazapi preserve an existing bold `number - name` participant header beneath the localized edit marker; repeated edits and provider-prefixed bodies remain deduplicated. Incoming text edits from WhatsApp Cloud and Notificame update the stored message by its provider ID, preserve its original content, and use the same localized marker without creating a duplicate.
- Unsupported WhatsApp placeholders retain `unsupported_reason`, code, and subtype when Meta provides them. Only `131060` is classified as coexistence unavailability; `131051`, other types, and legacy rows use neutral guidance. Outgoing messages without sendable content or a WAHA response are marked failed rather than unsupported inbound content.
- Provider-specific connection status: WAHA, Evolution, and Uazapi use their own APIs and webhooks; Meta signature validation is reserved for WhatsApp Cloud.
- WAHA session proxy: configuration is applied through `config.proxy` when updating the session, with a `server` plus optional username and password; it can be removed with `proxy: null`.
- WAHA session apps: `GET /whatsapp_waha/apps` lists the session apps (`GET /api/apps?session=`) and `PATCH /whatsapp_waha/apps` (`app`, `enabled`) enables or disables `brazilian-phone-numbers`, `argentine-phone-numbers` or `mexican-phone-numbers`. An app already in the requested state is left unchanged; an existing app has `enabled` changed with `PUT /api/apps/{id}`, keeping its config and cache; a missing app is created when enabling with WAHA's default config (`strict: false`, `lookup: true`, persistent cache). WAHA restarts the session when these apps change and requires `WAHA_APPS_ENABLED=True`.
- WAHA voice calls (`voice-calls` app): `Whatsapp::WahaVoiceCallsGate` shows the `waha_voice_calls` account feature when `WAHA_VOICE_CALL=true` or an active WAHA server has `extra_config.voice_calls`; per inbox it requires the channel's server to support it and the account to have the feature. `PATCH /whatsapp_waha/voice_calls` adds or removes the `call.received`, `call.accepted`, `call.active`, `call.ended`, `call.rejected` and `call.recording` events on Mega's webhook only, enables or disables the app (dm: let it ring, groups: reject, `recording.enabled`) and stores the state in `channel_whatsapp.voice_calls_enabled`. Incoming webhooks are translated to `Whatsapp::ProcessCallEventService`'s format and reuse the conversation, `voice_call` message, routing and `whatsapp_call` event; `call.rejected` only marks a call rejected when an agent rejected it. When the phone or another linked device answers, `call.ended` carries `reason: accepted_elsewhere` before `call.accepted` and no later end follows: the call becomes `completed` with no duration, with `answered_elsewhere` on the message, and the bubble shows "Answered on another device". Declining it there (`reason: rejected_elsewhere`) marks it rejected. Neither case sends the contact the unanswered-call message. `whatsapp_calls#accept` answers with the admin token (`/api/apps/voice-calls/{session}/{id}/answer`) and the browser opens the audio with its own `RTCPeerConnection` (no ICE servers: gows is ICE-lite): it sends a non-trickle offer to `whatsapp_calls#webrtc`, which forwards it with the admin token to `/api/apps/voice-calls/{session}/{id}/webrtc` and returns the SDP answer. Only the agent holding the call (`whatsapp_active_call:agent:{id}` in Redis) can use it; the browser never receives a WAHA credential, because WAHA session keys need the `send` permission for audio and could then send messages. `whatsapp_calls#initiate` places outgoing calls, counted as answered on `call.accepted` or `call.active`; the agent is told through `whatsapp_call` with `event: waha_call_answered` so the browser opens the audio. The `call.*` events of one call are processed one at a time (per-call-id lock in `Webhooks::WhatsappEventsJob`). An unanswered outgoing call is `failed` only when the contact declined it (`reason: rejected` on `call.ended`, or the `reject` tag on `call.rejected`); otherwise it is `no-answer`, because WAHA also sends `call.rejected` (tag `terminate`) when nobody answers. Reject and hangup go through the backend; the browser only releases the audio. Audio needs WebRTC enabled on WAHA (`WAHA_CALLS_WEBRTC_PUBLIC_IP`, open UDP port and `mode: host` on Swarm). `call.recording` arrives after `call.ended`; `Whatsapp::WahaCallRecordingJob` downloads the file (the URL may expire in about an hour) and attaches it through `Whatsapp::AttachCallRecordingService` as a private `.ogg` note, once per call (`recording_attached` on the call message). An outgoing call WAHA never reports (no `call.accepted`, `call.ended` or `call.rejected`) is ended by `Whatsapp::WahaUnansweredCallJob` after `RING_TIMEOUT` (2 minutes): hangup in WAHA, `no-answer` on the message, and the agent and conversation are freed (`Whatsapp::WahaOutboundCallEndService`, also used by the WAHA end events). `Internal::RemoveStaleCallStateService` also frees the agent of the call it clears.
- MEGA Voice Engine (PoC, beta, per account from Super Admin): Go service `voice-engine/` on Pion that answers incoming WhatsApp Cloud calls on the server. With `VOICE_ENGINE_URL` and `VOICE_ENGINE_SECRET` set and the `voice_engine` account feature (`Whatsapp::VoiceEngineClient::ACCOUNT_FEATURE`, turned on from Super Admin; `SuperAdmin::AccountFeaturesHelper` hides it while the engine is not configured; the inbox exposes `voice_engine_enabled` and Configuration → WhatsApp Calling only shows the hold music, with no switch or engine copy), `Whatsapp::AcceptCallService` sends Meta's offer to the engine (`Whatsapp::VoiceEngineClient#create_call`) and sends the same answer in `pre_accept` and `accept`; it marks `whatsapp_call.media = 'server'`. The agent's browser joins through the `whatsapp_calls#webrtc` relay (WAHA pattern: `WahaCallClient`) and reconnects after a reload. With no agent, the engine sends Opus silence so Meta does not hang up (20 s) and, after `MEGA_VOICE_ORPHAN_TIMEOUT`, ends the call and Rails hangs up at Meta (`POST /webhooks/voice_engine`). Rails↔engine traffic is signed with HMAC-SHA256 over `timestamp.METHOD path.body` (5-minute window). No keypad in this mode (recording, outbound calls, hold and transfers: see below). Details and deployment: `voice-engine/README.md`. The dead Node server path was removed (`lib/webrtc_server.js`, `Whatsapp::WebrtcSignalingService`, Redis `webrtc:*` publishes, `@roamhq/wrtc` and `redis` npm dependencies).
- TURN for the agent's leg on voice engine calls: `WahaCallClient#prepare`/`#connect` take the call's `inboxId` and open the browser's connection with the inbox's ICE servers (`GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/inboxes/:inbox_id/whatsapp_call_ice_config`, `Mega::Calls::IceConfig`: STUN/TURN from `MEGA_CALL_STUN_URLS`, `MEGA_CALL_TURN_URLS`, `MEGA_CALL_TURN_USERNAME` and `MEGA_CALL_TURN_CREDENTIAL` with the `premium_call_connectivity` feature), so an agent behind a symmetric NAT or a corporate firewall reaches the engine. That leg used to open without ICE servers. If the lookup fails, it opens without servers as before.
- Calls the voice engine loses: when the engine restarts or dies no `call_ended` comes. `Whatsapp::VoiceEngineClient` records each call it hands over (`create_call`, `create_outbound_call`, and `rename_call` moves the key) in a Redis sorted set (`Whatsapp::VoiceEngineCalls`, `voice_engine:calls`), and `Whatsapp::VoiceEngineReconcileJob` (every minute, `config/schedule.yml`) checks the ones older than a minute: one that already ended (the conversation no longer holds it live with `media = 'server'`) is forgotten; one the engine no longer knows (`404` on `POST /v1/calls/status`, `#call_known?`) is hung up at Meta or WAHA (`FlowBuilder::CallSettlement.hang_up_at_provider`) with its flow cancelled (`engine_lost`), and the provider's terminate closes it like any hang-up. Nothing is hung up while the engine does not answer. Per-engine cap: `MEGA_VOICE_MAX_CALLS` (503 `at capacity`; the call goes on without the engine: browser or agents).
- Voice engine hold music (per WhatsApp Cloud inbox, shown with the `voice_engine` feature): `Whatsapp::HoldMusic` keeps the choice in `provider_config.hold_music` (a preset key or `custom`) and the uploaded file in `Inbox#hold_music` (ActiveStorage). Presets are Ogg/Opus loops in `public/audio/hold-music/` (`warm-lounge` and `soft-lounge`, made with ElevenLabs Music, plus the synthesized `calm`, `bright` and `classic-tone`; default `warm-lounge`, also used when `custom` has no file). Uploads go through `Voice::HoldMusicConverter`: ffprobe checks there is an audio stream, size ≤ 10 MB and duration ≤ 10 minutes, then ffmpeg converts to Ogg/Opus 48 kHz mono 32 kbit/s with 20 ms frames (`-application audio`), the format the engine plays without transcoding; refused files return 422 with a translated message. `Whatsapp::HoldMusic.url_for(inbox)` gives the engine an absolute URL on `FRONTEND_URL` (the preset path or the blob redirect URL). API (admins): `GET`/`PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:id/inboxes/:inbox_id/hold_music` (`selection`), `POST`/`DELETE .../hold_music/file` (multipart `file`).
- Voice engine call transfer (direct, agent to agent, engine calls only): `Whatsapp::CallTransfer` keeps the pending transfer in `additional_attributes.whatsapp_call.transfer` (`agent_id`, `from_agent_id`, `expires_at`) and changes it only under the conversation lock. `GET whatsapp_calls/transfer_targets` lists the inbox agents online, taking calls and on no other call (`CallAssignmentService#transfer_targets`); `POST whatsapp_calls/transfer` (agent on the call) holds the caller with the inbox hold music and rings the target by ActionCable (`status: ringing` with `transfer`) and web push; `POST transfer_accept` (target, with its relay offer in `agent_sdp`) attaches the target on the engine, which closes the previous agent leg, resumes the call and moves the active-call Redis key, `answered_by_id`, `initiated_by` (outbound) and the assignee (`CallHandover#assign(..., reason: :transfer, restore: false)`); `POST transfer_decline`, `POST transfer_cancel` and `Whatsapp::CallTransferTimeoutJob` (30 s) give the caller back to the first agent. Check and cleanup run in one lock, so only one of accept, decline, cancel, timeout or the caller's hang-up wins. Every `whatsapp_call` broadcast carries `account_id` (the dashboard drops events of other accounts); the accept sends the target `transfer_withdrawn` and an `answered` push, so its other tabs stop ringing while the tab that took the call keeps it. Hold returns 409 while a transfer is pending. A target without a microphone declines automatically. The pending transfer stores both agents' names, and a reload rings the target again while it is still pending (`whatsapp_transfer_ring` in sessionStorage, `restoreTransferRing`); the transfer picker filters agents by name. On phones the call screen has Transfer at the top, the picker as a bottom sheet and Cancel under the name. Consultative transfer: `POST transfer` with `mode: 'consult'`; B's accept attaches B through the engine's `POST /v1/calls/consult` (a second agent leg bridged to the current agent while the caller is on hold; every agent-bound stream rewrites sequence and timestamps when its source changes) and returns `status: 'consulting'`; the pending transfer gets `connected_at` and loses `expires_at`; A completes with `POST transfer_complete` (engine `consult/end complete: true`, then the direct hand-over) or returns with `transfer_cancel`; B leaves with `transfer_decline`; the engine's `consult_left` webhook recovers the call. While consulting B is marked `consult:whatsapp_<call_id>` in the active-call Redis key (busy, but not the call's handler) and a reload of B leaves the consultation. ActionCable: `consult_connected`, `consult_completed`, `consult_ended`, `transfer_failed` with `consult_left`.
- Voice engine call recording: Rails creates engine calls with `record: true`; the engine writes two Ogg/Opus files without transcoding (caller track: every non-DTMF packet from Meta, hold included; agent track: every packet `outStream` sends, so agent audio, hold music, silence and the agent after a transfer) in `MEGA_VOICE_RECORDINGS_DIR` (default `$TMPDIR/mega-voice-recordings`), named by the final call id, and reports `call_ended` with `data.recorded`. `Webhooks::VoiceEngineController` enqueues `Whatsapp::VoiceEngineRecordingJob` for answered calls: it claims the call message once (`recording_attached`), fetches both tracks (`POST /v1/recordings/info` and `/fetch`), mixes them with `Voice::RecordingMixer` (ffmpeg `adelay` by the tracks' `started_at_ms`, `amix`, mono 48 kHz Ogg/Opus 32 kbit/s), attaches the file with `Whatsapp::AttachCallRecordingService` and deletes it on the engine (`/delete`); leftovers go after `MEGA_VOICE_RECORDING_TTL` (1 h). The agents' consultation is not recorded.
- Voice engine on WAHA inboxes: the same account feature (`voice_engine_enabled` is exposed for `whatsapp_cloud` and `waha`, `Whatsapp::VoiceEngineClient::PROVIDERS`); the WAHA voice calls app shows the hold music (`HoldMusicSettings`, with hold music; `hold_music` accepts both providers). WAHA's `/webrtc` takes an offer and returns the answer, so `Whatsapp::WahaVoiceEngineBridge` puts the engine where the agent's browser was: engine offer (`create_outbound_call(call_id, record: false)`), WAHA `/webrtc`, engine `set_remote_answer`; any failure ends the engine call and leaves the browser flow (an engine that does not answer has no call to end, so the call still reaches the agent's browser). Inbound: `whatsapp_calls#accept` answers at WAHA, then connects the bridge and marks `media: 'server'`. Outbound: on `call.accepted`/`call.active` the bridge runs before `answered_at` is saved and `waha_call_answered` (with `media`) is broadcast, so the browser always opens its audio on the engine. The agent joins through the same relay (`attach_agent`), so reloads, hold and transfers work as on Cloud (`isEngineCall` in `useCallSession`). Ends: WAHA's `call.ended` ends the engine leg (shared terminate path, `WahaOutboundCallEndService` for outbound calls) and an engine orphan or shutdown hangs up at WAHA (`Webhooks::VoiceEngineController#hang_up_at_provider`). WAHA keeps recording the call itself (one recording per call). WAHA serves every call from one IP:port, so the engine listens on `MEGA_VOICE_UDP_PORTS` consecutive ports (production compose: 10000-10009) and gives each call the least busy one: on a single port two WAHA calls share one 5-tuple and the ICE mux mixes their packets. WAHA has no DTMF.
- Call menu (IVR, WhatsApp Cloud and WAHA with the voice engine): a published event flow whose trigger is `call_received` (one per inbox) takes the inbox's incoming calls. `Whatsapp::ProcessCallEventService` holds agent assignment back while such a flow matches; `Whatsapp::CallIvrService` answers on the engine (`create_call` with `orphan_timeout_s: 600`, Meta `pre_accept` + `accept`), marks `whatsapp_call.media = 'server'` and `whatsapp_call.ivr`, and starts the run (`FlowBuilder::EventTriggerJob`, event `call_received`, payload with `call_id`); any failure before Meta's accept rings agents as before. Call blocks (`FlowBuilder::CallNodeExecutor`, only in `call_received` runs while `ivr` is set): `call_play_audio` (engine `/v1/calls/play`, waits `call_playback` until `playback_finished`, with the prompt length + 10 s as a safety timer), `call_collect_digit` (plays the prompt, waits `call_input` for a `dtmf` event; a digit during the prompt stops it; unlisted digit → `invalid_prompt_id` and retry, silence after `timeout_seconds` → retry, then `invalid` / `no_input`; each option has an optional name that labels its output on the canvas as `1 · Sales`), `call_transfer` (clears `ivr`, sets `ring_team_id` for team targets and rings through `ring_held_call`: assignment, escalation, broadcast, push; `no_agents` when nobody can take it; the accepting agent only attaches to the engine leg) and `call_hangup` (engine end + Meta terminate). `Webhooks::VoiceEngineController` resumes the waiting call session on `dtmf` and `playback_finished`; a call end cancels it. A run that ends without transferring hangs up; a run that fails rings agents instead of stranding the caller. Prompts: `FlowBuilder::VoicePrompt` (`/api/v1/accounts/:id/flow_builder/voice_prompts`, administrators, converted to Ogg/Opus by `Voice::HoldMusicConverter`); `source: tts` prompts keep `text`, `model` (`slug::model`) and `voice` and are synthesized by `FlowBuilder::VoicePromptSynthesizer` on save and when those change (never during a call): the `speech` capability on AI Providers models (probed with `RubyLLM.speak`), feature `voice_prompt_generation` (internal; default `gpt-4o-mini-tts`), `Llm::CapabilityClient#speak`, metered as `kind: speech` events (provider-reported tokens, zero when none); Gemini PCM is wrapped as WAV before the conversion; `GET .../voice_prompts/speech_models` lists the speech models with the voices of `speech_voices` in `config/llm.yml`. Engine: `POST /v1/calls/play` plays an Ogg/Opus URL once (hold music wins; agent audio dropped meanwhile), `/v1/calls/play/stop`, event `playback_finished {url}`, per-call `orphan_timeout_s`. On WAHA (`CallIvrService#perform_on_waha`) the call is answered at WAHA and bridged to the engine (`waha_bridged?`; a bridge failure falls back to ringing agents, marked answered), the widget does not ring while `ivr` is set (`mark_answered_elsewhere` and `accept_waha_call` 409 `in_ivr`), a transferred call that nobody answers is recorded as no-answer (`leave_ivr` drops `answered_at`), and `call_collect_digit` fails because WAHA carries no DTMF (inbox gate `call_dtmf`): menus there use `call_collect_voice`.
- Call menu on Twilio (Twilio voice inboxes, no voice engine): a published flow with `call_received` on a voice-enabled `Channel::TwilioSms` inbox takes its incoming calls. `Voice::InboundCallBuilder#initial_state` (after the out-of-hours check) creates the call with no ring recipients, stores `Call.meta['ivr']` and enqueues `FlowBuilder::EventTriggerJob` with `call_id` = CallSid; `Twilio::VoiceController#call_twiml` holds the caller with `<Pause>` + `<Redirect>`. The blocks go through `FlowBuilder::CallProvider` (`CallProviders::Twilio`; WhatsApp stays in `CallProviders::Whatsapp`, unchanged) and `Voice::Provider::Twilio::FlowCall`, which redirects the live call with TwiML: `call_play_audio` = `<Play>` + `<Redirect>` to `POST /twilio/voice/flow/:phone` (`playback_finished`), `call_collect_digit` = `<Gather input=dtmf>` (`dtmf` / `input_timeout`, with retries), `call_transfer` = `Voice::RingRecipients` (with `team_id` for teams, no escalation) + `Voice::CallRinging` and the regular conference (a `meta.ivr_transfer` token keeps the hold loop from joining before the redirect), `call_hangup` = call `completed`. Twilio callbacks are signed and resume through `FlowBuilder::CallSessions.resume`; the call end (`Voice::StatusUpdateService`) cancels the sessions. Prompts (Ogg/Opus) are served to Twilio as 8 kHz μ-law WAV (`Voice::Provider::Twilio::PromptAudio`, ffmpeg, cached by checksum) at `GET /twilio/voice/prompt/:phone/:token` with a signed token (`MessageVerifier`). No queue on Twilio (`no_agents`), no AI on Twilio (`call_ai` and `call_collect_voice` need the engine; `call_ai` inbox gate) and no agent-to-agent transfer on Twilio.
  - Menu pacing: on answering, `Whatsapp::CallIvrService` sends the engine (`POST /v1/prompts/preload`) every prompt of the flow's published version plus the inbox hold music, so no prompt waits for its download (a failure is only logged). The first prompt of the call starts with `delay_ms: 800` so Meta's media path already carries audio. `call_collect_digit` asks the engine for `input_timeout_ms` (`timeout_seconds` × 1000) on its last prompt: with no digit in that time the engine sends `input_timeout` and `Webhooks::VoiceEngineController` resumes the `call_input` wait as no input (only when the prompt URL matches and that prompt already ended); the Sidekiq timer stays 10 s later as a safety net, and a block with no prompt keeps its own timer. While `call_transfer` rings agents the caller hears the inbox hold music (`/v1/calls/hold`, `whatsapp_call.on_hold`); on accept `Whatsapp::AcceptCallService` takes it off so both hear each other. The dashboard keeps the transfer ring card even when an `assignee.changed` built before `ivr` was cleared arrives after it.
  - Queue: a `call_transfer` with `queue` on and nobody free waits with the inbox hold music (`FlowBuilder::CallQueue`, session wait kind `call_queue`) and looks for a free agent every 5 s; calls of the same inbox and team ring in arrival order, and after `max_wait_seconds` (30-300, default 120) the music stops and the run leaves by `no_agents`. An agent another transferred call of the inbox is ringing for is not offered a second one (`Whatsapp::CallAssignmentService#assign_held_call`).
  - AI on calls: `call_ai` ("Answer with AI", wait kind `call_ai`) opens an AI WebRTC leg on the engine (`/v1/calls/ai/offer|answer|send|end`, events `ai_connected`, `ai_ended`, `ai_event` over the `oai-events` data channel, deltas not relayed) and connects it to OpenAI Realtime with the SDP exchanged by Rails (`MegaAssistant::Voice::OpenaiRealtime`, the account's realtime voice model). `FlowBuilder::CallAiInstructions` builds the session: with `source: assistant` the inbox's Captain assistant (description, guidelines, guardrails) answers through `ask_assistant` (one Captain turn, `FlowBuilder::CallAiToolJob`); with `source: instructions` the block's prompt (up to 4000 characters) answers with no Captain turn. Both get `transfer_to_human` and `end_call` and are told to act as a front-line phone agent that resolves the call and transfers only when the caller asks for a person or it cannot help. The greeting is sent on `session.created` and, unless `announce_ai` is `false`, says it is a virtual assistant (the prompt also forbids claiming to be a person); a transfer or goodbye waits for `output_audio_buffer.stopped` so the last sentence is heard. Talk time is charged in AI Actions per 5-minute block (`MegaAssistant::Voice::SessionService.charge_block!`, feature `flow_builder_call_ai`), up to `max_minutes` (1-30); the transcript is posted once as a private note (`FlowBuilder::CallAiTranscript`). The step output carries `latency_ms` (`turns`, `avg`, `max`): the time from the caller's `input_audio_buffer.speech_stopped` to the assistant's `output_audio_buffer.started`, as Rails receives both (`FlowBuilder::CallAiLatency`). Call blocks start in the account's language but set no transcription language, so a caller who speaks another one is heard and the model switches to it at once; "Ask by voice" reports that language in `choose_option` (`language`, a two-letter code) and the run keeps it as `caller_language`, so the next call block starts in it. They answer as soon as the caller finished their sentence, with semantic turn detection (`semantic_vad`, `PHONE_EAGERNESS` = `high`, `eagerness` in the session config; measured on calls, about 0.6 s against 1 s with a fixed 500 ms pause; the Mega Assistant keeps its 700 ms pause or the installation's), a handoff or goodbye already spoken in an earlier response is not announced twice, and in "Ask by voice" a greeting such as "hello?" or "how are you?" is returned in a few words and repeats the question instead of counting as an answer; when it picks an option, the model confirms it in one short sentence ("Of course, one moment while I connect you with sales") and the block leaves only once that was heard (`output_audio_buffer.stopped`, `CONFIRM_GRACE` of 8 s at the latest); a choice made without words asks for that sentence first. The time to answer stops when the caller starts speaking (`input_audio_buffer.speech_started`) and starts again after the model's next words, so it never says "I did not hear you" over the caller; when no words follow (a noise the model does not answer), it asks again `SPEECH_ALLOWANCE` (20 s) after the time to answer, as with no answer. The engine reports `ai_ended` with `reason: closed` when the provider hangs up its side (the block leaves by `ended`) and `failed` only on an ICE/DTLS failure (`error`). A WAHA call the IVR answered at WAHA that nobody then takes is hung up, not rejected (`Whatsapp::AutoRejectCallJob`), and every call run carries the call's own message (`message_id`). `call_collect_voice` ("Ask by voice", wait kind `call_voice`, `FlowBuilder::CallVoice`) asks `question`, listens and maps the answer to an option with `choose_option` / `no_match` (`options` 1-9 of `{ id, label, examples }`, `timeout_seconds` 5-30, `retries` 0-2), one block of AI Actions per use; the caller's words are not stored. Provider events are filtered from Rails logs (`provider_event`).
- Call back from the chat: an unanswered or failed `voice_call` bubble (`components-next/message/bubbles/VoiceCall.vue`) shows the inbox and number the call went through. When it is the conversation's latest call, the contact has a phone number and `useContactCallMethods` finds an available method (WaVoIP, Twilio Voice, WhatsApp Cloud or WAHA, the same rule as the contact panel call button), it shows *Call back* (incoming) or *Call again* (outgoing). The button stores the request in `useCallsStore().contactCallRequestConversationId` and opens the contact panel; `ContactInfo.vue` handles it with `handleCallClick`, so the method picker and the WhatsApp Cloud call permission still apply.
- Uazapi proxy: inbox settings support an internal regional proxy with a Brazilian city validated from the provider catalog, a custom proxy without fallback, or no proxy; changing or removing a proxy applies the corresponding provider mode, and removal clears the stored regional preference.
- WAHA, Evolution and Uazapi session settings: a unified screen built from shared components in `components/WhatsappSession/`: a connection card with a status badge normalized by tone (working, connecting, action required, disconnected, unknown), QR or pairing-code linking inside the card (no modals), guided passkey steps for WAHA, a sync card with progress, counts (imported, skipped, conversations, new) and the last run's result while it remains in Redis, positively worded switches (`ignore_*` inverted in the UI) that save automatically with a saving indicator, and a danger zone with a confirmation dialog before recreating or disconnecting.
- WAHA passkey linking: proactive extension detection via `WAHA_PASSKEY_CHROME_EXTENSION_ID`, `PASSKEY_REQUIRED` and `PASSKEY_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` states inside `session.status`, challenge retrieval through `/auth/passkey/challenge`, temporary assertion handoff, browser-extension signing on `web.whatsapp.com`, and manual code confirmation; GET requests return `422` when no data is pending.
- On-demand global sync for WAHA and Uazapi: Sidekiq jobs with Redis progress, 24h/7d windows, provider-id deduplication, open-conversation reuse, asynchronous historical media downloads, account-level concurrency locks, and an optional dedicated `whatsapp_history_sync` worker for high-volume installations.
- Per-conversation sync for WAHA and Uazapi: manual action from the conversation menu, with 24h/7d windows and provider-id deduplication.
- WAHA group lazy media download: an inbox can enable `lazy_media_download` to persist only incoming group media as a pending attachment (only provider identifiers in `meta`); an agent downloads it on demand through the conversation `download_attachment` endpoint, which reuses the proven two-step metadata-then-binary flow and attaches the file idempotently. Direct conversations retain immediate downloads.
- WhatsApp reactions: dashboard and API-token actors can add, replace, and remove one reaction per message while preserving WhatsApp Device echoes.
- Notificame: official-oriented variant for LATAM operations. `MESSAGE` webhooks with `direction: OUT` (replies from the phone or WhatsApp Web) are stored as outgoing `external_echo` messages in the conversation of the `message.to` contact, like WhatsApp Cloud echoes: no sender, `status: sent` and no `SendReplyJob`. They are skipped when their ids already exist; when one arrives before the send response stores the ids of a Mega message (same content, last minute, no `source_id`), the ids go to that message. Groups are ignored.
- Instagram, Facebook, TikTok, Telegram, X, SMS, and Email exposed as inbox channels.
- Facebook Messenger contact creation prefers a nonblank Graph profile `name`, otherwise joins nonblank `first_name` and `last_name`; the existing unidentified-profile placeholder remains when Graph returns no profile data.
- Facebook, Instagram, and TikTok channels persist `provider_name` on creation or reauthorization and expose it in the inbox payload; reauthorization preserves a custom inbox name. The `social_channels:backfill_provider_names` task starts in dry-run mode and supports resumable provider-scoped batches for existing channels.
- Facebook and Instagram comments (Mega): comments on posts, reels and ads come in as incoming messages in the commenter's conversation, next to their DMs. Gated by the `social_comments` account feature (appended last in `config/features.yml`, off by default) and, per inbox, by `inboxes.comments_enabled` (default `false`, "Receive comments from posts and reels") and `inboxes.comments_top_level_only` (default `true`, "Top-level comments only (no replies)") in the "Post comments" section of the inbox settings; `Inbox#social_comments_enabled?` requires both the toggle and the feature.
- Comments: connection and Meta setup. Three paths: Facebook Page (`feed` field of the Page object; scopes `pages_read_user_content` and `pages_manage_engagement` in `facebookScopes.js`), Instagram through Facebook Login (`comments`/`live_comments` fields of the Instagram object; scope `instagram_manage_comments`) and Instagram Login (`comments`/`live_comments`; scope `instagram_business_manage_comments` in `Instagram::IntegrationHelper::REQUIRED_SCOPES`). Because `subscribed_apps` replaces the whole field list, `subscribe` sends the DM fields alone first, exactly as before, and only for inboxes with comments enabled makes a second call with DM + comment fields (`COMMENT_SUBSCRIBED_FIELDS`); if that call fails it is logged and the DM subscription stays intact (`rake facebook:resubscribe_pages` is unchanged). Turning the toggle on re-subscribes the channel (`Inbox` `after_update_commit`), and reauthorizing the Facebook Page or Instagram does too. The webhook fields in the Meta App Dashboard are set per app; the comment permissions need Advanced Access through App Review and business verification. Channels connected before must reconnect to grant the new permissions and then run `bundle exec rake social_comments:resubscribe` (walks `Channel::Instagram` and `Channel::FacebookPage` and prints OK/FAILED per channel). If channels are connected through Mega Hub, the Hub app and its n8n relay must forward the `changes` entries.
- Comments: webhook reception. Instagram: `Webhooks::InstagramEventsJob` routes entries with `changes` (or Instagram Login's flat `field`/`value`) of field `comments`/`live_comments` before Meta's test-event check, locking on the comment author, to `SocialComments::Ingestion::InstagramNormalizer`. Facebook: the `facebook-messenger` gem only dispatches `messaging` entries, so `/bot` mounts `Integrations::Facebook::FeedRelay`, which lets the gem handle the request (verify token, `X-Hub-Signature` check, Messenger events) and, only when it answered a POST with 200, queues the `feed` entries of the same body to `Webhooks::FacebookFeedEventsJob` (`item: comment`, `FacebookNormalizer`, one builder per `Channel::FacebookPage` with that `page_id`). `Webhooks::MegaHubController` queues the same `feed` entries arriving through the Hub relay.
- Comments: ingestion (`SocialComments::Ingestion::CommentBuilder`, built on `Messages::Messenger::MessageBuilder`). Skips channels awaiting reauthorization, inboxes without comments enabled, comments without an id and the account's own comments (author equal to `instagram_id`/`page_id`); with `comments_top_level_only` it drops those with a `parent_id`; it dedupes by comment id (Meta retries for 36 h and may notify a boosted post's comment twice). Contact and conversation follow the DM rules: one thread per contact next to their DMs (Instagram through a Page uses the `instagram_direct_message` conversations). Facebook verbs `edited`, `remove`/`delete` and `hide`/`unhide` update the text and the `deleted`/`hidden` flags of the existing message. No webhook carries the post picture: the card (Instagram: photo or thumbnail, text and type; Facebook: `full_picture`, `message` and `permalink_url`) is read once from Graph with `MediaDetailsService`, the picture is copied into storage with `SocialComments::Ingestion::PostThumbnailStore` (permanent `/storage/file/:key` URL, because Meta CDN links expire; if the copy fails the Meta link is kept) and the result is cached for 30 days (`social_comments:media:<channel class>:<media id>`).
- Comments: conversation status. `CommentBuilder#build_conversation` creates the conversation with `skip_bot_logic` (`Conversation` opens it instead of leaving it pending) unless the inbox's active published flow is in AgentBot mode and its start has `include_comments` (`flow_bot_answers_comments?`); then it stays pending for the bot, like a DM.
- Comments: message contract. `message_type: incoming`, `source_id` = comment id, `additional_attributes.social_comment = true` (jsonb marker for SQL filters; `Message#social_comment?`), `content_attributes.social_comment` with `platform`, `comment_id`, `parent_id`, `post_id`, `media_product_type`, `permalink`, `caption`, `thumbnail_url`, `ad_id`, `ad_title`, `from_username`, `hidden`, `deleted` and `private_reply_sent_at`, and `content_attributes.external_created_at` (comment time on Meta). The dashboard renders it with `ExternalAdReplyCard`, like Click-to-WhatsApp ads (compact variant: 64 px thumbnail with ▶ on reels, Post/Reel/Ad type, two lines of text and "View on {platform}"; a later comment on the same post shows "On the same post" instead of the card; Replied in public, Private message sent, Hidden and Deleted badges), and the conversation list prefixes it with "Comment:".
- Comments: reply and moderation. The main composer always sends a DM. A comment uses the same message context menu (`MessageContextMenu`): "Reply to comment" replaces "Reply to this message" and opens `SocialCommentReplyPanel` ("Send DM" and "Publish reply"), and the menu adds "Open post", "Hide comment"/"Show comment" and "Delete comment". Public reply (`SocialComments::PublicReplyService`): an outgoing message with `content_attributes.in_reply_to` = the comment; `SocialComments::ReplyDelivery`, included in the Facebook and Instagram send services, publishes it at `/{comment_id}/replies` (Instagram; a reply to a reply goes under the top-level comment) or `/{comment_id}/comments` (Facebook), and stores the reply id as `source_id` and in `content_attributes.social_comment_reply`; attachments fail (`attachments_not_supported`). Private reply (`SocialComments::PrivateReplyService`): one per comment, within 7 days of `external_created_at` (or `created_at`); sent right away to `/{page_id | instagram_id}/messages` with `recipient.comment_id`, saved already `sent` (`skip_send_reply`, `content_attributes.private_reply_to`, `additional_attributes.social_comment_private_reply`), and stamps `private_reply_sent_at`. Hide/show (`hide` on Instagram, `is_hidden` on Facebook) and delete (`SocialComments::ModerationService`) change the comment on Meta, update the message flag and leave an activity in the conversation. Endpoints: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/conversations/:conversation_id/messages/:message_id/social_comment/reply` (`content`), `POST .../social_comment/private_reply` (`content`), `POST .../social_comment/hide` (`hidden`) and `DELETE .../social_comment`; what the rules or Meta refuse returns 422 with `{ error }` (`CustomExceptions::SocialComment`).
- Comments: safety. A comment never opens the 24 h DM window (`Conversations::MessageWindowService::NOT_SOCIAL_COMMENT_SQL`, also used by the Facebook HUMAN_AGENT tag) and never triggers the greeting, out-of-office, email collect or Captain reply (`MessageTemplates::HookExecutionService`), Agent Bots (`AgentBotListener`), automation rules (`AutomationRuleListener`), or Flow Builder resumes (`FlowBuilderListener`); "Message received" and AgentBot flows handle it only when their start has `include_comments` (see Flow Builder).
- Comments: API and tools. `POST .../messages/:message_id/social_comment/reply`, `.../private_reply`, `.../hide` (`hidden`) and `DELETE .../social_comment` are in Swagger under the "Social Comments" tag ("Conversations & Messaging" group), along with the inbox `comments_enabled`/`comments_top_level_only` and the message `content_attributes.social_comment`/`in_reply_to`/`private_reply_to`/`social_comment_reply`. `POST /outbound_messages` accepts `message.type: comment_reply` with `message.comment_reply.comment_id` (Meta id, the message `source_id`) and `visibility` `public` (default) or `private`: requires the `social_comments` feature (`comment_reply_not_enabled`), no `recipient` (`recipient_not_allowed`), text only, looks the comment up among the inbox incoming messages (`comment_not_found`) and calls `SocialComments::PublicReplyService`/`PrivateReplyService`; comment errors return their key as `code` (`CustomExceptions::SocialComment#code`, `meta_error` when Meta refuses). It runs outside a transaction because the private reply reaches Meta before it is saved: the idempotency record is saved first and dropped only when nothing was sent; one left without a message makes the same key answer `409 request_in_progress`, never sending twice. The `social_comments_reply`, `social_comments_private_reply`, `social_comments_hide` and `social_comments_delete` tools (`COMMON_TOOL_SPECS`, feature `social_comments`) are on the account MCP server and in Mega Assistant, and `outbound_messages_create` gains `comment_reply`.
- TikTok authorization is enforced by the `channel_tiktok` account feature; unavailable hosted accounts see the support-request entry point and onboarding keeps TikTok out of detected suggestions.
- IMAP email processing with a dedicated timeout to avoid stuck mailbox jobs.
- Gmail inbox connection diagnostics with live IMAP/SMTP XOAUTH2 authentication, safe error categories, recent incoming/outgoing activity timestamps, and administrator-only OAuth reconnection.
- When messages or conversations are deleted from Gmail inboxes, an asynchronous job searches the stored RFC `Message-ID`, resolves Gmail's opaque identifiers, and permanently deletes the message or thread without blocking the local deletion.
- Email provider inference from signup-domain MX records to suggest Gmail or Outlook integrations during onboarding.
- Attachment uploads with explicit `.pfx` recognition alongside common media and document formats.
- API Channel: generic gateway for proprietary platforms via API/webhooks.
- Widget pre-chat form: checkboxes marked required use the form acceptance rule, so submission remains blocked until they are selected; the localized required-field message is retained.
- Twilio voice and WhatsApp Cloud calls: WebRTC flow with a unified timeline; Cloud calls from profiles without a conversation safely resolve or create the contact thread while honoring inbox continuity and agent visibility. Before negotiating a Cloud call, the dashboard requests the inbox ICE configuration: accounts with `premium_call_connectivity` receive STUN/TURN from `Mega::Calls::IceConfig`, while other accounts retain default STUN; if the configuration cannot be fetched, the client uses its local fallback. Call permission can use an approved template selected from ReplyBox or configured as the inbox default, retains the WAMID to correlate the reply, and records the outgoing message in the conversation without sending it to the provider twice. Meta queries are the source of truth: they normalize and persist no-permission, temporary, and permanent states, and honor the `start_call` action before starting a call; every change is recorded as activity, with Meta's expiry date for temporary permissions. Client and server prevent a second active call per agent, including across tabs. Inbox candidates are determined by the standard assignment rules —capacity, team, and overflow— and agents already on a call are excluded; an online administrator who enabled call notifications enters only as a fallback when no eligible agent remains. A call with no assignee (for example the first call from an unknown number) rings one online inbox agent at a time and escalates to the next even when the inbox auto-assignment is off; then the candidates are all inbox members. "Online" is live presence (`OnlineStatusTracker`), also when picking the fallback administrator; when nobody is online, the call is announced, without assigning it, to every administrator who enabled call notifications. While a Cloud call is accepting, connecting, or active, both the UI and model reject agent and team changes; a ringing call remains reassignable and reassignment is available again after termination. Calling controls, permission requests, call initiation, and call webhooks are disabled for Cloud channels marked as WhatsApp Business App coexistence, because calling remains in the WhatsApp Business app. Twilio reports normalize data from the Call model, optional native recordings require storage-cost acceptance, and recordings are exposed in conversations and call reports. When the conversation assignee is already on another call, `provider_config.call_busy_assignee_behavior` decides (Cloud and WAHA): `reassign` (default) uses `Whatsapp::CallAssignmentService#reassign_unavailable_assignee` to hand the conversation to a free agent and schedules escalation; `wait` keeps the assignee. When the assignee is away or offline (live presence; the busy status still hears the call), the call always goes to a free online agent, whatever that setting says, escalation is scheduled and the activity says they were offline; when nobody else is online, it keeps ringing the assignee. The UI for these settings (escalation, busy assignee, auto-reject and messages) is `WhatsappCallHandlingSettings.vue`, shared by the Cloud settings and the WAHA Apps collapsible. If the same contact calls again while their previous call is still live, the conversation keeps `whatsapp_call`, `call_answered` and the agent, and the new call is ignored. When call routing (round-robin, a busy assignee or escalation after no answer) moves a conversation that had an assignee, `Whatsapp::CallHandover` keeps `additional_attributes.call_reassignment` (call id, previous assignee, agent the call went to) and writes an activity with the reason; when the call ends (terminate webhook or the agent hanging up), the conversation goes back to the previous assignee, unless an agent reassigned it during the call or that agent lost access to the inbox. A conversation that had no assignee stays with the agent who took the call.
- WhatsApp Cloud call hours (opt-in, Cloud only): `provider_config.apply_business_hours_to_calls` (switch in the inbox call settings). `Whatsapp::CallHoursBuilder` turns the inbox business hours and timezone into Meta's `call_hours`: `status` is ENABLED only while the switch and the inbox business hours are on, open days go in `weekly_operating_hours`, a lunch break becomes two ranges, and Meta's existing `holiday_schedule` is kept. `inboxes#update` enqueues `Whatsapp::SyncCallHoursJob` when the switch, the hours, their on/off or the timezone change (also once when opting out), and the calling status update enqueues it for opted-in inboxes. The job calls `FacebookApiClient#update_call_hours` in a request separate from the calling status, so a Meta refusal never undoes the saved status; the refusal (or hours closed every day) is stored in `provider_config.call_hours_sync_error` and shown under the switch, and a successful sync clears it.
- Withheld Twilio callers: `Voice::Provider::Twilio::InboundCaller` treats a `From` that is not E.164, or is one of the Twilio withheld caller-ID placeholders (`266696687`, `86282452253`, `8656696`, `2562533`, `7378742833`, `464`), as anonymous: source id `anonymous:<CallSid>`, a new "Unknown caller" contact per call and its own conversation even with `lock_to_single_conversation`. `ContactInbox#anonymous_caller?` (Enterprise) adds `anonymous_caller` and `can_reply: false` to the conversation REST, Action Cable and webhook payloads; public outgoing and template messages are refused there (private notes, call records and incoming messages are allowed). `MessagesView` shows a banner, `ConversationHeaderControls`, `ConversationHeader` and the `VoiceCall` bubble hide the call buttons, and the conversations store keeps `can_reply` off when a message arrives in such a conversation.
- Call errors: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/contacts/:id/call` answers 422 (`errors.whatsapp.calls.contact_phone_required`) for a contact without a phone number instead of failing; `VoiceCallButton` shows the server reason carried by `ExceptionWithMessage#data` (it read `error.message`, which is only `ERROR_WITH_MESSAGE`).
- Audio transcription controls: GPT-4o Mini Transcribe defaults for voice notes with Whisper available as an account override; call recordings keep account-level flags for master enablement and per-provider behavior (WhatsApp Cloud and WaVoIP), audio normalization, turn-based diarization, higher-fidelity GPT-4o Transcribe text, contact/assigned-agent name labels, and manual retry from the context menu for audio messages without text. Stored transcriptions participate in OpenSearch, GIN/SQL fallback, global conversation results, and in-conversation message search while retaining the existing permission scopes and filters.
- Rejected voice notes in Captain: `Captain::OpenAiMessageBuilderService#audio_transcription` drops a `Faraday::BadRequestError` (the provider rejected the file) and Captain answers without that transcript instead of retrying the same audio; `Messages::AudioTranscriptionService` turns `RubyLLM::BadRequestError` and `RubyLLM::ContentFilterError` into `Faraday::BadRequestError` (no retry, `transcription_failed`) and anything else into `Faraday::ClientError` (retried). With RubyLLM 2.1, a 400 the provider classifies as rate limit, overload or bad key is no longer a `BadRequestError`, so it is retried.
- Long call recordings (stack#776): every transcription request may take `AUDIO_TRANSCRIPTION_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 600; ruby-openai's default of 120 s cut off recordings of about 4.5 minutes or more), for audio messages and Twilio calls. `gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize` rejects audio longer than 1400 s, so above 1350 s `Messages::CallRecordingChunkedDiarization` diarizes the recording in equal 16 kHz WAV pieces of up to 600 s, shifts each segment by its piece's start and sends every later piece a 2–10 s sample of each first-piece speaker (`known_speaker_names[]`/`known_speaker_references[]`, up to 4) so speakers keep their names; the plain GPT Transcribe text still comes from the whole file. When the recording cannot be split (no ffmpeg or an unreadable piece), it is still diarized whole up to the model's 1400 s limit; above it, it keeps the plain transcript without speakers instead of sending a file the model would reject. `Messages::AudioTranscriptionJob` retries a timeout once instead of three times; when the provider rejects the audio (400), the timeout repeats or the rate-limit retries run out, it sets `meta.transcription_failed` on the attachment (merged into the existing meta) and the audio shows that the transcription could not be completed, with manual retry from the message menu. A successful transcription clears the flag.
- WaVoIP with per-inbox session persistence and role-aware credential reuse. During initialization, it requests the inbox ICE configuration and supplies the STUN/TURN servers from `Mega::Calls::IceConfig` to the WebRTC client when the account has `premium_call_connectivity`; without that configuration, it retains the provider's default behavior.
- WhatsApp contact cards (Mega): `file_type: contact` attachments (phone in `fallback_title`, name in `meta.firstName`/`lastName` or the first line of the content) are sent as native contacts: Cloud with `type: contacts` (`formatted_name`, `phone` and `wa_id`), WAHA with `POST /api/sendContactVcard` and Uazapi with `POST /send/contact` (one request per card; the first id is stored), NotificaMe with a `contacts` content (`formattedName`, `phone`, `waId`) and Twilio with a `.vcf` vCard (`text/x-vcard`) stored on the same attachment and sent as `MediaUrl` without text. `Attachment#contact_card` builds the shared card; without valid digits the message goes out as text. The message text and the agent signature are not sent with the card. In ReplyBox, the address-book button (Cloud, WAHA, Uazapi, NotificaMe and Twilio WhatsApp) searches `contacts/search` and sends `contact_card: { contact_id }`; `Messages::MessageBuilder` reads the contact phone on the server and rejects contacts without one. `Conversations::ForwardMessageJob` also copies `meta`. Evolution and 360dialog still send the name as text.
- Widget (contact details): `POST /api/v1/widget/config` returns `contact` = `{id, has_email, has_name, has_phone_number, pubsub_token}` (`Contact#widget_presence_data`); `PATCH/PUT /widget/contact`, `contact/set_user` and `contact/destroy_custom_attributes` return `id` and the same flags (`set_user` adds `widget_auth_token` when it switches contacts) and `GET /widget/contact` keeps `identifier` for the pre-chat form. `GET/POST /widget/conversations` use `Conversation#widget_push_event_data` (`id`, `inbox_id`, `contact_last_seen_at`, `status`); `create` does not return `contact` and only lists chat messages (`messages.chat`). The `sender` of a contact message is `{type: 'contact'}` (`Message#widget_sender_data`) in REST and realtime (`Message#widget_push_event_data`, `ActionCableBroadcastJob` with `widget_members`/`widget_data`), and widget sessions do not get `conversation.updated`. `PATCH/PUT /widget/messages/:id` returns `{contact: {has_email, has_name, has_phone_number}}` and `conversations/destroy_custom_attributes` returns 204 with no body.
- Widget (identification): `ContactIdentifyAction` never merges into a contact that already has an `identifier` unless the request carries that same identifier, even when the request identifier is blank; the matching detail is not saved and is kept in `unverified_matches`. In `widget/conversations#create` (pre-chat) and `widget/messages#update` (email request), `Contacts::UnverifiedContactNoteService` adds, after commit, a private note in the account locale with the detail and a link to the matching contact, stripping `` ` ``, `()`, `[]` and `{}` so visitors cannot add Markdown links, mentions or Liquid. Webhook agent bots receive it like any sender-less private note. Merges are logged (`[CONTACT_IDENTIFY]`, `[CONTACT_MERGE]`).
- Widget (other): the article viewer only loads `/hc/` URLs from the widget's own origin (`getArticleViewerURL`); `widget/integrations/dyte/add_participant_to_meeting` only looks the message up in the visitor's conversations (404 otherwise) and adds the session's contact.
- Twilio SMS/WhatsApp (signature): `Twilio::CallbackController` and `Twilio::DeliveryStatusController` include `TwilioWebhookVerification::Messaging`, which validates `X-Twilio-Signature` against the request URL or `FRONTEND_URL` + path. Each channel starts in log-only mode; the first valid signature per endpoint stores `<endpoint>_signature_enforced` in `channel_twilio_sms.provider_config` (new jsonb, migration `20261112120000`) and from then on an invalid signature returns 403. API Key channels without the account auth token are not verified (log only).
- Twilio WhatsApp health: `Api::V1::Accounts::Concerns::WhatsappHealthManagement` accepts any `twilio_channel?` (SMS and WhatsApp) on `GET .../inboxes/:id/health` and `POST .../inboxes/:id/register_webhook` (400 for Twilio WhatsApp before; the error messages say "Twilio channels"). `Twilio::HealthService` adds `medium` (`sms`/`whatsapp`) and, for a WhatsApp number, `sender.type: whatsapp_sender` with its Twilio `status` (only `ONLINE` is healthy), `friendly_name`, `quality_rating` and `messaging_limit`; a Twilio error (credentials, missing sender) answers 422. UI: `TwilioHealth.vue`.
- Bandwidth (SMS): OAuth with `client_id`/`client_secret` (`Sms::BandwidthTokenService`, cached token; `SendReplyJob` retries 3 times when the token cannot be fetched, then marks the message failed). `Sms::BandwidthConfigurationService` merges the sent config onto the saved one (an API-user inbox migrates by sending only the client ID and secret), drops server-managed keys and verifies the credentials with Bandwidth before the inbox is created or updated: 422 when they are missing or rejected, 503 when Bandwidth does not answer. New inboxes must use OAuth; the API user stops working on 2026-12-02.
- Bandwidth (webhook): the URL is `/webhooks/sms/:phone_number?token=<webhook_token>`; the token lives in `channel_sms.provider_config.webhook_token` (generated on create or, for existing channels, the first time it is needed, with `update_columns`; no migration). `Webhooks::SmsController` accepts callbacks without a valid token (with a warning) until Bandwidth first calls with the token for that event kind (`inbound`, `status`); from then on it returns 401. A token only routes events for its own number or another number of the same Bandwidth application and account. `callback_webhook_url` (administrators only) includes the token, and the inbox Configuration tab (`BandwidthConfiguration.vue`) shows the URL, the OAuth state and whether the token is enforced, and lets administrators save the Client ID and Client Secret.
- X/Twitter: `POST /webhooks/twitter` requires `X-Twitter-Webhooks-Signature` (base64 HMAC-SHA256 of the body with `TWITTER_CONSUMER_SECRET`) and returns 401 when the secret is missing or the signature does not match; `GET` (CRC) returns 400 when `crc_token` has an invalid format. The payload is read from the request body only.
- Meta (Messenger and Instagram): `/bot` mounts `Integrations::Facebook::WebhookServer` (behind `FeedRelay`), which rejects every Messenger event with 400 when there is no app secret (`FB_APP_SECRET`) instead of letting the gem skip the `X-Hub-Signature` check, so a direct Meta connection needs `FB_APP_SECRET`; events relayed by Mega Hub arrive at `/webhooks/mega_hub/*` with their own token and are not affected. The subscription check (`GET /bot`, `GET /webhooks/instagram`) returns the challenge only when `hub.verify_token` matches `FB_VERIFY_TOKEN`, `IG_VERIFY_TOKEN` or `INSTAGRAM_VERIFY_TOKEN` (constant-time comparison); a missing token or an unset variable never matches.
- Hanging up calls: `POST .../conversations/:id/whatsapp_calls/terminate` (Cloud and WAHA) and `DELETE .../inboxes/:inbox_id/conference` (Twilio) return 403 (`errors.voice.cannot_end_call`) when the caller is not the call's agent: on WhatsApp the one who placed it (`initiated_by`) or answered it (`answered_by_id`); on Twilio `accepted_by_agent_id`. With no owner (an incoming call nobody answered), on WhatsApp anyone who can see the conversation can end it, and on Twilio only an incoming call that is still ringing can be declined.
- Call conversations open on private note: `ReplyBox` checks `isVoiceCallConversation` (last message `content_type` `voice_call`, any provider) and sets Private Note when the conversation opens (mount and id change), then keeps the agent's choice for that visit.
- In-call keypad (DTMF): `components-next/call/CallKeypad.vue` in `FloatingCallWidget` (button beside mute) and `MobileCallScreen` (Keypad button). `useCallSession` exposes `canSendDigits`/`sendDigits` for Twilio (`TwilioVoiceClient.sendDigits`, only while the device connection is open) and WhatsApp Cloud (`NativeWebRTCClient.sendDigits`, `RTCDTMFSender.insertDTMF`); WAHA is left out (WAHA does not support DTMF). The typed line shows only the tones the client accepted.
- Twilio TwiML lives in `Voice::Provider::Twilio::TwimlBuilder` (conference, hang-up, announcement `<Say>`+`<Hangup>`, wait `<Say>`+`<Redirect>` to Twilio's default playlist). `<Say>` sends only `language` from the account locale (en→en-US, es→es-MX, pt→pt-PT, pt_BR→pt-BR, fr/de/it, otherwise en-US), so Twilio uses its default voice for that language.
- Twilio call settings (`Enterprise::Channel::TwilioCallSettings`, prepended to `Channel::TwilioSms`, store accessors on `channel_twilio_sms.provider_config`, permitted as channel attributes of a Twilio SMS inbox and returned in the inbox JSON): `call_greeting_enabled`/`call_greeting_message`, `call_business_hours_enabled`/`call_out_of_hours_message`, `call_ring_timeout` (10–600 s, blank = no limit)/`call_unanswered_message` (messages up to 1000 characters), `call_routing_enabled` and the WhatsApp routing keys `call_escalation_enabled` (blank reads as on), `call_escalation_timeout` (10–60, default 15), `call_max_escalation_attempts` (1–10, default 3), `call_auto_reject_timeout` (10–120, default 30), `call_busy_assignee_behavior` (`reassign` | `wait`); out of range = 422. With no key set an inbox behaves as before (ring everyone, no timeout). UI: Voice tab (`VoiceConfigurationPage`), routing reuses `WhatsappCallHandlingSettings.vue` with flat channel keys.
- Twilio greeting and business hours: the greeting is the conference `waitUrl` of the inbound contact leg only, `GET /twilio/voice/wait/:phone`, signed like the other voice webhooks (unsigned → 403). With `call_business_hours_enabled` and `inbox.out_of_office?`, `Voice::InboundCallBuilder` creates the call `no_answer` with `end_reason: outside_working_hours` and the call webhook answers the out-of-hours `<Say>` + `<Hangup>`; nobody rings. Business-hours exceptions (`out_of_office_skipped_for?`) are not applied to calls. `Voice::CallStatus::Manager` checks status and `end_reason` under the row lock and never moves a terminal call that has an `end_reason`, so Twilio's later `completed` callback keeps it missed. `Call#push_event_data` carries `end_reason` (bubble labels for out of hours and no agents available).
- Twilio ring time limit: `Voice::RingTimeoutJob`, scheduled after commit only for a ringing call on an inbox with `call_ring_timeout`, ends the call under the row lock when it still rings unclaimed (`no_answer`, `end_reason: ring_timeout`), broadcasts `voice_call.ended` and hangs up the caller leg with the unanswered message through `Voice::HangUpUnansweredCallerJob` (REST `update_call`, retried on `Twilio::REST::TwilioError`, 5 attempts in all; error 21220 or 404 = caller already gone). Accepting a call that already ended returns 409 (`errors.voice.call_already_ended`).
- Twilio ring list: `calls.ring_state` (jsonb, default `{}`) records `ring_recipient_ids` at creation (`Voice::RingRecipients`: inbox members, or the restricted team's assignee/members, plus administrators, narrowed to the assignee) and `dismissed_user_ids`.
