The call payload carries `ring_user_ids` (recipients minus dismissed; null when the call has no list, then the dashboard keeps its assignee rule) and `ring_routed`. `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/inboxes/:inbox_id/conference/dismiss` (`call_sid`, `conversation_id`; same inbox and conversation access as hanging up) adds the agent under the row lock and touches the message so other tabs stop ringing; a call already claimed is left alone. `GET /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/ringing_calls` returns `{ payload: [message] }` with the incoming Twilio calls still ringing for the user (unaccepted, last hour, conversation still openable via `Conversations::PermissionFilterService`); `Dashboard.vue` restores them on page load.
- Twilio call routing (`call_routing_enabled`, opt-in): a routed call rings one agent from `Whatsapp::CallAssignmentService#find_available_agent` (assignee first, online inbox agents round robin, opted-in administrators; it assigns nothing), recorded in `ring_state.tried_agent_ids`; an assignee set mid-ring rings only when free. `Voice::EscalateTwilioCallJob` mirrors `Whatsapp::EscalateCallJob` under the row lock with an attempt counter (a job delivered twice runs once); with nobody left the last agent rings `call_auto_reject_timeout` seconds more and the call ends `no_answer` / `no_agents_available` with the unanswered message (also at creation when nobody is available). No hand-back after the call: the conversation keeps its assignee. With routing on and escalation off nothing schedules an end; the ring time limit is then the only cap.
- Shared busy state: a Twilio claim marks the agent's busy key (`whatsapp_active_call:agent:*`, value `twilio_<CallSid>`) inside both claim locks, taking over a key held by the agent's previous Twilio call but never a WhatsApp one, and releases it after a terminal update only while it still holds that call. WhatsApp routing therefore sees agents on Twilio calls as busy and vice versa; Redis errors on mark or release are logged and do not roll back the call.
- Work preferences: `account_users.accepts_conversations` / `accepts_calls` (default true, at least one on, null → 422), set with `POST /api/v1/profile/work_preferences` (`profile: { account_id, accepts_conversations, accepts_calls }`, the current user's membership only, 404 for another account, 422 translated) and returned in each `accounts` entry of the user JSON. Conversations off: legacy round robin (`AgentAssignmentService`) and assignment v2 (OSS and Enterprise, through `Inbox#available_conversation_agents`) skip the agent; manual assignment and the creator's own assignment stay. Calls off: `Whatsapp::CallAssignmentService` (an assignee with calls off hands the ring over, `:calls_off` activity), `ProcessCallEventService#determine_call_recipients`, `Voice::RingRecipients` (an unrouted list skips the agent; `ring_state.assignee_skipped` makes the list decide alone; when every agent the call reaches has calls off the call ends `no_agents_available`) and the WaVoIP store. UI: switches in `SidebarProfileMenuStatus` and `SidebarMobileStatus` (`useAvailabilityStatus`), shown when an inbox of the account takes incoming calls; the last switch on is locked. Chat room calls ignore `accepts_calls`.
- Twilio hold: every conference webhook stores Twilio's conference SID once in `meta.provider_conference_sid` under the lock (`meta.conference_sid` stays the friendly name). `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/inboxes/:inbox_id/conference/hold` (`call_sid`, `conversation_id`, boolean `hold`) → `ConferenceService#hold` under one lock: only the agent who accepted the call (also after handing the conversation over), only `in_progress`, conference SID known; then the `contact` participant is updated with `hold`, `hold_url` = Twilio's default playlist (GET), `meta.on_hold` is saved and the message touched. Refusals (`CustomExceptions::CallHoldRefused`, `errors.voice.hold.*`): 422 for a non-boolean `hold`, another agent or a Twilio error; 409 for a call that is not connected or whose conference SID is not known yet. The call payload carries `on_hold` (true only while in progress); widget Hold/Resume (desktop between Keypad and Mute, phone in the control row) and an "On hold" bubble state. Not verified against live Twilio.
- Facebook and WhatsApp connection: `Api::V1::Accounts::CallbacksController` (register or reauthorize pages) and `Whatsapp::AuthorizationsController` (embedded signup, create or reconfigure), `Waha::AuthorizationsController` and `Evolution::AuthorizationsController` require an administrator; an agent gets 401.

### 3.2 Conversation core

- Sorting and labels in filtered lists: advanced filters and folders accept the same sort order as the regular list (`sort_by` on `POST /conversations/filter`, whitelist shared in `Conversations::SortService`; an unknown value falls back to `last_activity_at_desc`; `unread` also keeps only unread conversations, like the regular list); with filters or folders the sort menu stays visible and only the status selector is hidden (`ConversationBasicFilter` `showStatusFilter`), and changing the order reloads the filtered list from the first page. A day marked "open all day" counts as open until 23:59:59 (`WorkingHour#open_at?`). Labels added by another instance (automations, Captain) are no longer lost when an unrelated save with the old list loaded persists the record: `Labelable::Persistence` only writes the list and its cache when it changed on that instance (patch over acts-as-taggable-on 12; `Migration::ConversationBatchCacheLabelJob` builds the cache from the taggings under a lock).
- Number custom attributes in filters: `Filters::CustomAttributeFilterHelper::NUMERIC_FILTER_OPERATORS` (`equal_to`, `not_equal_to`, `is_present`, `is_not_present`, `is_greater_than`, `is_less_than`) limits `number` attributes in contact and conversation filters; `contains`/`does_not_contain` answer 422 (500 before). The editor (`useFilterCondition`) marks those operators invalid.
- Copying a date custom attribute (`CustomAttribute.vue`) copies `displayValue`, the date as shown, instead of the stored value.
- Conversation without a contact: `_conversation.json.jbuilder` and `Conversations::EventDataPresenter#push_meta` send `meta.sender: {}` when the contact no longer exists, and the contacts store skips senders without an `id`, so the list and events do not break.
- Timezone-aware dates and per-view counts: date conditions on `created_at` and `last_activity_at` in advanced filters, folders and contact segments store the browser timezone (`timezone` on each condition; `filterQueryGenerator`/`withTimestampTimezone`) and the server turns the local day into UTC boundaries before querying (`Filters::DateFilterHelper`: `<` local midnight for "less than", `>=` next day's midnight for "greater than", `days_before` with today's date in that zone and only 1 to 998), so PostgreSQL compares the column directly with its indexes; an invalid timezone answers 422 (also when saving folders through `custom_filters`). Existing folders and segments keep UTC until they are opened and saved once; automations and Captain audiences are unchanged. The list applies the same boundary in the browser (`createFiltersMatcher`, once per filter set). Tab counts follow the active view (`conversationStats` `onListRequestStarted`): an in-flight `/meta` from another view no longer overwrites a filter's totals, and deleting a conversation refreshes the totals of the view on screen (filters and folders included).
- `ReplyBox` registers shortcuts through `useKeyboardEvents`, whose `AbortController` removes the listener on unmount, including components with multiple Vue roots. Send validation, closing mentions with Escape, and the editor reference used to restore focus after resizing are preserved; `FullEditor` no longer includes the obsolete keyboard mixin.
- Collapsible reply box: `MessagesView` wraps the editor in `ReplyBoxFold`, which hides it with `v-show` (the `ReplyBox` instance stays mounted, so draft text, attachments, reply mode and Copilot state survive) and shows `CollapsedReplyBar` (Reply / Private note with `Alt+L`/`Alt+P`, Shift added on QWERTZ, "Unsent draft" marker from `hasMeaningfulEditorContent` or `attachedFiles`). `useReplyBoxCollapse` stores the choice in the user UI settings as `is_reply_box_collapsed` and folds only when `useViewport().isMobile` is false, so the phone composer is never folded. Collapse comes from the `ReplyTopPanel` button (keyboard reachable) or from dragging the `ResizableEditorWrapper` handle 48 px below its minimum (`collapsible` prop, off by default so Captain scenario editors are unchanged). Revealing only calls `setReplyMode` when the picked mode differs from the current one, because switching modes clears attachments; `TOGGLE_REPLY_TO_MESSAGE`, `INSERT_INTO_RICH_EDITOR`, `CMD_AI_ASSIST` and pasted files also reveal it. A resize observer keeps a list resting at its end pinned when the editor comes back. `useKbd` now prints `Alt` instead of `⌥` outside macOS. Analytics: `Collapsed the reply box` / `Expanded the reply box`.
- Widget email-transcript requests disable their button while in flight and for 15 seconds after a successful send, preventing repeated delivery requests while retaining immediate retry after a failed request.
- Widget visitor messages: `UserMessageBubble.vue` adds the `message-content` class, so lists in the visitor's bubble show their bullets and numbers.
- CSAT feedback visibility is stored per inbox in `csat_config.hide_feedback_from_agents`. Dashboard message serializers and Action Cable redact only `feedback_message` for non-administrator account users, while ratings, persisted data, administrator payloads, and public customer payloads remain unchanged.
- Deleted messages may retain their original text and attachments for agents through `inboxes.show_deleted_message_placeholder`; the public API, widget payloads, and contact-facing Action Cable broadcasts replace `content` and `processed_message_content` with the deletion notice, omit `content_attributes.original_content` and `translations`, and expose an empty attachment list without changing the persisted record.
- The widget message endpoint returns `403 Forbidden` for text and attachment submissions to a resolved conversation when `inboxes.allow_messages_after_resolved` is false. This server-side guard also covers retried failed messages and leaves the resolved conversation unchanged.
- Status model: open, pending, resolved, snoozed.
- Reply window countdown (stack#746): `Conversations::MessageWindowService#window_ends_at` (last incoming message, comments excluded, + the channel window; nil without a window or before the first incoming message) and `can_reply?` derive from the same lookup, and `Conversation#reply_window` picks the own window or the open window of a sibling conversation (same contact and inbox, template-only inboxes) exactly as `can_reply?` does. The conversation JSON and `conversation.*` events expose `messaging_window_ends_at` (unix seconds) next to `can_reply` from one `reply_window` call; `Message#conversation_push_event_data` adds it for incoming direct messages (created_at + window, no query) so `message.created` moves the countdown (`SET_CONVERSATION_CAN_REPLY` never moves it back). UI: `helper/messagingWindow.js` (none/open/closing under 2 h/closed), `useMessagingWindow` (one shared 30 s clock; when the end passes it commits `can_reply: false`, so `ReplyBox` and the banner switch to templates), `components-next/Conversation/MessagingWindowIndicator.vue` in the desktop, phone and expanded cards (hidden for resolved conversations with a closed window), the header subtitle and the phone header chips. Reactions count as they already did for `can_reply`; the `last_incoming_message_at` column is not used because it differs (reactions, social comments).
- One active conversation per contact and inbox: besides creation, `toggle_status` to `open`/`pending` and public agent replies on a resolved conversation return 422 `errors.conversations.active_conversation_exists` with the `conversation_id` of the existing open or pending conversation (`Conversation#other_active_conversation`, `ActiveConversationConflict`); the reply is rejected before it is sent. Private notes are saved without reopening. Incoming contact messages, automations, macros and bots still reopen as before. Conversation search exposes `status` and shows it as a colored badge.
- Relative dashboard timestamps use `exactTimestamp` for a delayed hover tooltip with the full date and time; `TimeAgo` renders creation and last-activity values on separate lines.
- Priority model: urgency handling per conversation.
- Participants: multi-agent collaboration in the same thread.
- Custom attributes API: `POST .../custom_attributes` keeps replacement as the default and accepts `merge=true` to update only supplied keys; `POST .../destroy_custom_attributes` removes specific keys and returns the remaining attributes.
- Macro execution and required attributes: the reply editor provides a `#` picker, while the command bar exposes **Execute a macro** only on active conversation routes with the macro flag enabled; both use the same persisted sidebar order. All three surfaces share execution, detect `resolve_conversation` and resolved `change_status` actions, prompt for and persist missing values before execution, and can continue without resolving when the dialog is dismissed. The `Macros::ExecutionService` overlay also blocks direct resolution while values for current required definitions are missing; checkbox `false` counts as filled and nullish values as missing.
- Bulk macro execution: the desktop selection bar (`BulkMacroActions`, behind the `macros` flag) and the phone selection bar (**More** → macro sheet → confirmation sheet) call `useBulkActions#onExecuteMacro`, which sends every selected id in one `POST /macros/:id/execute` (`conversation_ids` array; `MacrosExecutionJob` checks access per conversation). It does not open the required-attributes dialog: when a resolving macro (`resolvesConversation`, exported from `useMacroExecution`) targets a conversation with missing required attributes, the toast warns that it may stay open. The `macros_execute` account action is unchanged.
- Drafts and pinned: per-agent workflow continuity.
- Advanced filters and custom views: high-volume operational segmentation.
- Dedicated unread sort mode in the conversation list.
- Email attachment indicator: `Conversations::AttachmentSummaryService` aggregates, per page, attachments on incoming messages of `Channel::Email` inboxes only and exposes `has_attachments`, `attachments_count` and `attachment_extensions` on the conversation payload (list, show, `push_data` and webhooks); other channels omit the fields. Incoming messages with attachments carry the summary in `message.conversation` so the list updates in real time. Inline images referenced by CID are embedded in the HTML and create no `Attachment`. The `attachments` filter (`is_present`/`is_not_present`) uses the same rule through `EXISTS` and is mirrored in client-side filtering.
- Large attachments in email replies: `MAXIMUM_EMAIL_ATTACHMENT_SIZE` (Super Admin → Email, in MB; empty uses 40) sets the ReplyBox limit on `Channel::Email` inboxes through `fileUploadMixin#maxSizeFor`, private notes included; it is separate from `MAXIMUM_FILE_UPLOAD_SIZE`, which still caps URL downloads. The mailer attaches files up to 20 MB in total based on `blob.byte_size` and sends the rest as links without downloading them; the editor warns when the total passes 20 MB. Videos over 40 MB (`Attachment::MAX_VIDEO_PREVIEW_SIZE`) skip the ffmpeg preview and come with an empty `thumb_url`.
- New email conversation (stack#730): `useFileUpload` takes `inbox` as an object, ref or getter, uses `MAXIMUM_EMAIL_ATTACHMENT_SIZE` for email inboxes (like `fileUploadMixin`) and with `uploadToInbox` uploads to `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/inboxes/:inbox_id/direct_uploads` (`Inboxes::DirectUploadsController`, authorized by `InboxPolicy#show?`; the blob logic lives in the `DirectUploadBlob` concern, shared with the conversation endpoint). `ActionButtons` turns direct upload on only for email inboxes, the only channel whose create payload sends `signed_id`s (`composeConversationHelper`). `ComposeNewConversationForm` shows `CONVERSATION.EMAIL_ATTACHMENTS_AS_LINKS` when attachments exceed `EMAIL_INLINE_ATTACHMENTS_LIMIT`. The form accepts drag and drop of attachments (`ActionButtons` enables `drop` on the file upload and shows the `CONVERSATION.REPLYBOX.DRAG_DROP` overlay); without a conversation or the inbox endpoint, `useFileUpload` sends the file with the message instead of a direct upload.
- Inline images in `email_reply` (stack#748): `ConversationReplyMailerInlineImages` finds `/storage/file/<key>` in `content_attributes.email.html_content.reply` and `outgoing_content`, registers each `image/*` blob as `attachments.inline` before `mail()` and then points the `html_part` `src` attributes at the `cid:` URL. Limits: 2 MB per image, 8 MB in total and 10 images; the rest and any error stay as links without stopping the send. Without attachments the message is `multipart/related`; with attachments, `multipart/mixed`. Regular attachments use `attachments[]` instead of `mail.attachments[]`, which rendered the body one extra time and left a duplicate copy. Summary and transcript keep their links.
- Quoted email replies (`quoted_email_reply` account flag, off by default, gates the whole feature): inside the flag every public reply in an email inbox appends the last non-private email (`extractQuotedEmailText`, `full` before `reply`, HTML turned into text with line breaks, links and nested quotes kept) as a Markdown blockquote with its header; the per-user `<channel>_quoted_reply_enabled` ui_settings toggle and its bottom-panel/phone-tools button are gone. `QuotedEmailPreview.vue` sits in the editor `footer` slot as a collapsed "…" button; expanding it asks `ResizableEditorWrapper` for room (`requestEditorHeight`) and on desktop the editor and the preview share the resizable height (Tailwind split classes on `WootMessageEditor`, `.editor-mount` on the ProseMirror mount). Remove drops the quote for that message (`isQuoteRemoved`), Cmd/Ctrl+Z inside the composer restores it, and it comes back after sending or switching conversation. `appendQuotedTextToMessage` escapes `{{`/`{%` in the quote as `{{ '{{' }}`/`{{ '{%' }}` so Liquid (`Liquidable`, `Messages::MessageBuilder#process_liquid_in_email_body`) prints them literally, and keeps whole lines from the top so the reply stays within the 150,000-character content limit (a quote cut down to its header is dropped).
- Tables in email replies: `FORMATTING['Channel::Email']` enables the `table` node and the `insertTable` menu item (`@chatwoot/prosemirror-schema` 1.4.6, column resizing off for messages; the editor reloads its state when the channel changes). `ChatwootMarkdownRenderer#render_message(tables: true)` turns on CommonMarker's `:table` extension and `BaseMarkdownRenderer#table`/`#table_cell` add inline styles (`TABLE_STYLE`, `TABLE_CELL_STYLE`, `TABLE_HEADER_STYLE`) and an `&nbsp;` in empty cells; only the email reply asks for it (`render_email_html` and the `email_reply` view), so the Liquid drops (`MessageDrop#text_content`, `ConversationDrop#recent_messages`, agent notification emails) render exactly as before. In the dashboard `MessageFormatter` fills empty cells too, and channels without tables flatten a pasted table to one `a | b` line per row (`MARKDOWN_PATTERNS` type `table`). `FullEditor.vue` no longer caps every `.ProseMirror-woot-style` editor at 7.5rem, which kept the reply editor from growing after opening a help center article.
- Forward by email (backend): `POST .../conversations/:id/messages` with `content_attributes.forwarded_message_id` (a message of the same conversation, else 422), `to_emails` (required), optional `cc_emails`/`bcc_emails`, `email_html_content` (the edited email, always stored as `content_attributes.email.html_content` even with `quoted_email_reply` off) and `forwarded_attachment_ids` (attachments of the forwarded message reused by blob; ids of other messages are ignored). `Messages::MessageBuilder#validate_forward` runs before any other step (also before the split-attachment path) and rejects forwards outside email inboxes, private forwards, forwards without a recipient and forwards of a private note; `private` arriving as a multipart string is cast to boolean. Liquid and spintax never run on a forward (`Liquidable`, `#should_process_liquid?`): the text carries the customer's email and the dashboard resolves the note's variables before sending. `Message#forwarded?` / `.not_forwarded` (json column read back as jsonb, always scoped to one conversation; legacy rows holding `\u0000` are kept without decoding, as in `.hide_removed_reactions`) keep forwards out of first-reply/waiting-since (`human_response?`, `valid_first_reply?`), the conversation transcript email, `Captain::ConversationTranscript` and `LlmFormatter::ConversationLlmFormatter`; auto-translation already sends them as written. `ConversationReplyMailer` sends a forward as a new thread: `Fwd: <subject of the forwarded email or the conversation>`, Message-ID `<forward/<id>@<domain>>`, no In-Reply-To/References, Reply-To the inbox address (never `reply+uuid`). Replies to a forward start a conversation with each recipient: `InReplyToStrategy`, `ReferencesStrategy` and `Imap::ImapMailbox` skip forwarded messages, and the `in_reply_to` lookup is scoped to the sender only when it points at a forward (`NewConversationStrategy#reply_to_forward?`, IMAP `find_conversation_by_in_reply_to`), so a Cc'd third party replying to an unknown parent still joins the conversation sharing it. Deleting a forward keeps the marker. The generic Forward (contacts, `Conversations::ForwardMessageJob`) never sets `forwarded_message_id`. Captain never reads a forward as an agent reply either: `MessageHistoryBuilderService`, `ConversationFaqContentService` and `ConversationCompletionService` use `.not_forwarded`, and `ConversationOutcomeTracker` does not count it as the human reply.
- Forward by email (dashboard): `Message.vue` adds the `forwardEmail` context-menu option for public, delivered, not deleted messages of email inboxes and mounts `components-next/message/forward/ForwardEmailPanel.vue` on the menu's `forwardEmail` event (`MessageContextMenu` lists "Forward by email" right after Forward, in the desktop menu and the phone sheet); the generic Forward (contact picker) stays. The panel is a `Dialog` (sheet on phones) with `ReplyEmailHead` (`show-to`, values committed on blur), a note `Editor` with the email signature and variables resolved before sending (`resolveVariablesInMessage`), the original email rebuilt by `forwardEmailHelper.buildForwardedEmailHtml` (Gmail-style header, escaped values) in a contenteditable `EditableEmailBody` (vue-letter sanitized), and removable chips for the original attachments. It sends `createPendingMessageAndSend` with `emailHtmlContent` (note html + edited body), `forwardedAttachmentIds` and `contentAttributes.forwarded_message_id` (`api/inbox/message.js` posts `email_html_content` and `forwarded_attachment_ids[]`). The email bubble shows a forward whole (no quote folding) under `ForwardedEmailBanner` (recipients, and that the contact cannot see it unless they are a recipient); `getLastEmailInSelectedChat` skips forwards so replies keep quoting and addressing the contact's thread. Not ported from upstream: new attachments in the panel and the NewConversation `RecipientsInput` contact search.
- Email reply CC: with `@chatwoot/utils` 0.0.58 the reply box keeps the other To recipients of the incoming email in CC.
- Custom subject on email replies: `ReplyEmailHead.vue` shows a Subject row when the parent binds `v-model:subject` (both ReplyBox mounts, not the forward panel), prefilled by `getDefaultSubject` (last outgoing non-forward `content_attributes.email.subject`, else `Re: <mail_subject>`) and following that default until edited; ReplyBox resets it when the conversation changes and sends `subject` only when it differs from the `Re: <mail_subject>` the mailer builds itself (`getCustomSubject`, never on private notes), so an untouched reply keeps the exact subject as before. `Messages::MessageBuilder#process_emails` stores a present `subject` as `content_attributes.email.subject` (not on private messages), and `process_email_content` carries it through every branch (with `quoted_email_reply` off it used to replace `attrs[:email]`). `ConversationReplyMailerHelper#prepare_mail` uses that subject as is, else `mail_subject` (`Re:` / `Fwd:` / `[#id]`).
- Selected conversations can be marked read or unread through an account-scoped batch endpoint. The dashboard splits large selections into sequential requests of at most 100 IDs, reconciles each returned read-state row, and refreshes unread indicators after the operation completes. The endpoint filters display IDs with `Conversations::PermissionFilterService` and preserves per-user notification state.
- Dedicated sidebar filters: unread, mentions, participating, groups, and unattended conversation views.
- Reactive sidebar counters: unread per conversation type and mentions via notification_type=conversation_mention.
- Assigning a human to a pending conversation creates the same `conversation_assignment` notification as an open conversation when the change is notifiable. The participant listener suppresses its duplicate participant-added notification, and the recipient's push preference still controls delivery.
- The legacy notification-history UI and `/notifications` route are removed while `inbox_view` remains disabled. Persisted notifications still feed realtime popups, sidebar counters, and reconnect reconciliation. Calendar uses its specialized Push payload; `kanban_note_agent_assigned`, `kanban_stage_automation`, `kanban_checklist_due_date`, and `account_task_due` use a separate operational payload with direct module URLs and no email delivery.
- Assignment V2: smart distribution with capacity and policy rules. The per-inbox job is queued after the transaction that opens the conversation commits, again when an agent becomes available (availability changes to online, auto offline is turned off, or their presence returns after expiring), and once more when a trigger arrives while a run for that inbox is already in progress; the 30-minute `periodic_assignment_job` remains only as a fallback.
- Inboxes expose `auto_assign_on_agent_reply` to keep unassigned conversations unassigned when an agent sends an outgoing message.
- Multi-account users keep one avatar selector, but its upload and delete operations target the active `AccountUser` membership. Account-scoped agent and message payloads prefer that membership avatar and fall back to the global `User` avatar; single-account behavior remains global and no permission or account policy toggle is introduced.
- Teams: `icon` and `icon_color` are persisted on `teams`, exposed through API/model JSON, and included in realtime payloads for lists and assignment pickers.
- Enterprise SLA: `AppliedSla` exposes backend-computed FRT/NRT/RT deadlines; when the policy uses business hours, `Sla::BusinessHoursService` consumes the inbox working-hours configuration and the JSON response returns `sla_*_due_at` values to the dashboard. Resolving a conversation records `sla_completed_at`, freezing the displayed duration of FRT/NRT/RT misses; historical SLAs without that timestamp remain visible as static misses. Conversations with blocked contacts reject new SLA assignment, are excluded from processing/reports, and clear `sla_policy_id`, `applied_sla`, and `sla_events` from payloads while blocked.
- SLA to automation: after an SLA is created, `SlaPanel` shows a created step with **Later** and **Create automation** (only with the automations feature); `useAutomationRoute` opens the automation list with `?sla_policy_id`, which starts a new rule with the `add_sla` action preset to that policy.
- V2 report drilldown: `/api/v2/accounts/:account_id/reports/drilldown` returns the conversations, messages, or reporting events behind a chart bar; `V2::Reports::DrilldownBuilder` validates the metric, bucket, administrator permission, pagination, account/inbox/agent/team/label filters, business hours, and last-message serialization, with a dedicated Rack::Attack rate limit. For non-hourly `conversations_count` buckets, the drawer can open the conversation list with Created-at date conditions and the active inbox, agent, team, or label scope; the URL conditions remain visible and savable in the filter popover. Date filtering uses UTC, so counts near a local midnight may differ.
- Bot report "total responses": `V2::Reports::BotMetricsBuilder#bot_messages` counts only outgoing, non-private messages of bot conversations whose `sender_type` is in `Message::BOT_SENDER_TYPES` (`AgentBot`, `Captain::Assistant`); agent replies and notes no longer count, so numbers may drop compared to before. Flow Builder messages in AgentBot mode are sent by the flow's bot (`AgentBot`) and count as its bot; other Flow Builder and automation messages do not.
- Call report recordings: `Api::V1::Accounts::CallReportsController#find_waba_recording` matches the recording blob by its base name `call_recording_<id>` with any extension, so WAHA Ogg recordings (`Whatsapp::AttachCallRecordingService`) are found besides Cloud API `.webm`; each row adds `recording_extension` (`mp3` for Twilio). `CallReports/CallRecordingPlayer.vue`, in the desktop cell and the phone card, is a compact pill with play/pause, range seek, elapsed time and length, speed 1x/1.5x/2x and download: one recording plays at a time, WebM/Opus without a length header gets its real length from one seek to the end (that far time is never shown), the call's `call_duration` is the fallback, and Ogg is converted to WAV on Safari/iOS. Calls without a recording show "No recording". `ReportsWrapper` gives `call_reports` and `activities_reports` the full width (`w-full`); the other reports keep `max-w-5xl`.
- Queue metrics in the call report: `CallReports::QueueMetrics` (called from `CallReportsController#metrics`, same filters) adds `abandoned_calls`, `self_service_calls`, `average_wait`, `sla_answered_within`, `sla_considered`, `sla_rate` and `transfers` by target agent, over incoming calls. WhatsApp (Cloud and WAHA) keeps on the `voice_call` message: `data.meta.answered_at` (an agent's first answer, `WhatsappCallsController#record_call_agent`), `data.outcome` at the end (`answered`, `rejected`, `missed`, `self_service`, `abandoned`; read from the call state before `ProcessCallEventService#handle_call_terminate` clears it) and `data.transfers` (`Whatsapp::CallTransfer#record_transfer`: from, to, mode and outcome `accepted`/`declined`/`cancelled`/`timeout`/`consult_left`/`failed`/`call_ended`). A WhatsApp call that has a call session (`call_sessions`, recorded since issue #2419 step 2) is read from it instead: its `outcome`, the wait from `started_at` to `answered_at`, the ring from `started_at` to `ended_at`, and the `transfer_<outcome>` transitions that end an attempt (`transfer_started` and `transfer_consult_connected` do not count); calls without a session fall back to the message. `bin/rails call_sessions:backfill` (`CallSessionBackfillService`; `DRY_RUN=true` only counts, `ACCOUNT_ID=<id>` limits it) opens ended sessions for past WhatsApp calls from their message, so the report gives the same numbers; it skips live calls and calls that already have a session, and can run again. Twilio calls record their session too (`Voice::CallSessionSync`, next to the `Call` record: created by the call builders, the agent who joins the conference, holds, the call menu handing the call to agents, escalations, hang-ups and the end in `Voice::CallStatus::Manager`), and the backfill fills past Twilio calls from `Call`. The report reads a call's session once it ended; a live call is read as before. The outcome and times come from `Call#report_outcome`, `#report_started_at`, `#report_answered_at` and `#report_ended_at`, shared by the report and the session; they read the unix seconds Twilio writes in `meta.initiated_at` and `meta.ended_at`, which the report used to fail on. The session also covers two calls the message leaves out: a terminate that arrived before its connect, and a WAHA call answered on another device, whose wait is now known. Twilio is read from the `Call` model: `accepted_by_agent_id` = answered, `outside_working_hours` left out, any other `end_reason` = missed, `completed` without an agent = solved in the call menu, `no_answer`/`failed` without a reason = abandoned; the wait runs from `meta.initiated_at` to `meta.accepted_broadcast_at`. Formula: SLA = answered within the inbox's SLA ÷ (answered + rejected + missed + abandoned after the SLA); calls abandoned within the SLA and answered calls with no known wait do not count; `nil` when none counts. The SLA is `call_sla_seconds` (5-600, default 20): inside `provider_config` on WhatsApp (`WhatsappCallHandlingSettings.vue`), a validated `store_accessor` edited in `VoiceConfigurationPage.vue` on Twilio. Calls from before this change have no outcome and are left out. The page is `CallReports/CallReportsQueue.vue`.
- Canned responses with reusable attachments also available in new conversation flows.
- The canned response picker is teleported and caret-anchored, loads the account list through the hardened IndexedDB cache, filters shortcuts/content locally, reconciles realtime invalidations, previews channel-compatible rendered content, and preserves unresolved Liquid placeholders for backend evaluation.
- `CaretAnchoredPicker` (canned responses and macros) resets the highlighted option only when the item ids change, so a background refresh of the same list keeps it. `SearchInput` takes "/" as its focus shortcut only when no typeable element has focus (`isActiveElementTypeable`), so "/" typed in a reply composer or another text field on the search page stays there.
- Reply editor inline image upload for Email and Web Widget, ProseMirror resizing, and safe `cw_image_width`/`cw_image_height` rendering.
- WhatsApp `reply_to` resolution honors `conversation_history` by matching quoted message identifiers in previous conversations for the same contact inbox, without expanding to account-wide message search. In coexistence, it also links phone-scoped and BSUID-scoped WAMIDs sharing one decoded token; malformed or ambiguous identifiers remain unlinked.
- WhatsApp Cloud and Meta (reliability): outgoing Cloud attachments are uploaded to `/{phone_number_id}/media` first (`Whatsapp::MediaUploadService`) and sent by `id`, so Meta does not download them through its fwdproxy (rate limited per ASN, error 131053); if the upload fails the link is used as before, and `WHATSAPP_MEDIA_UPLOAD_STRATEGY=link` restores link sending. Cloud webhooks that only carry `tracking_events` are dropped before Sidekiq. Outbound calls and call permission requests use the conversation's identity (`contact_inbox.source_id`): a BSUID goes in `recipient` and a phone number in `to` (`FacebookApiClient#recipient_params`), so a BSUID-only contact can be called from their conversation (without a conversation the phone number is still used). Messenger handles postbacks (Get Started and buttons) as incoming messages with their title or payload; pages connected earlier need `rake facebook:resubscribe_pages` to receive them. The `HUMAN_AGENT` tag (Messenger and Instagram) only applies to human agent replies outside the 24-hour window (`Facebook::HumanAgentTagHelpers`); inside the window or from bots and Captain it is sent as `RESPONSE`.
- Agent offboarding flow with assigned-conversation review and bulk unassign/reassign options constrained by inbox/team access.
- Agent invitations atomically reserve daily email capacity in Redis before queueing mail; an exhausted limit rolls back only that invitation and returns HTTP 429, while bulk onboarding continues adding existing users.
- Message search looks back 90 days by default; Super Admin → Settings → General sets the window (`SEARCH_TIME_RANGE_LIMIT_DAYS`, read by `Limits.search_time_range_limit_days`, 90 when empty or invalid, at most 3650). The Postgres search, the `since` cap of the search filters and the dashboard date picker (min date and presets) follow it; the OpenSearch path keeps no default lookback when no `since` is sent.

### 3.3 Internal communication and rooms

- Chat Rooms remains a dedicated domain on the existing base: `chat_rooms`, `chat_room_members`, and `chat_room_messages`.
- Room names preserve their submitted casing; `chat_rooms` enforces account-scoped case-insensitive uniqueness with a unique `account_id, LOWER(name)` index.
- Internal parity is extended with `chat_room_categories`, `chat_room_drafts`, `chat_room_reactions`, `chat_room_polls`, `chat_room_poll_options`, `chat_room_poll_votes`, and `chat_room_teams`.
- Room types: `public_channel`, `private_channel`, and `direct_message`, with optional names for DMs and reuse of existing DMs by member combination.
- Account-scoped API for rooms, members, categories, drafts, reactions, polls, search, read/unread state, archive state, and typing status.
- Search uses `f_unaccent` across channels, DMs, and accessible messages.
- Vuex `chatRooms` centralizes rooms, messages, thread replies, categories, drafts, and search results; the UI exposes filters, sections, quick creation, DMs, drafts, polls, the thread side panel, and channel editing from the header action menu.
- Realtime delivery through Action Cable and `CHAT_ROOM_*` events for message, room, reaction, poll, read state, and typing changes.
- WebRTC audio/video calls persist lifecycle state in `chat_room_calls`, track attendees in `chat_room_call_participants`, relay ephemeral SDP/ICE through `RoomChannel`, and reuse `ring.mp3`/`calling.mp3` tones.
- Accounts with `chat_room_calls` receive only Google's `DEFAULT_STUN_URL`; enabling `premium_call_connectivity` makes `Mega::Calls::IceConfig` load `MEGA_CALL_STUN_URLS`, `MEGA_CALL_TURN_URLS`, `MEGA_CALL_TURN_USERNAME`, and `MEGA_CALL_TURN_CREDENTIAL` through `GlobalConfigService`. Saved Super Admin > Call ICE values take priority, existing environment variables are migrated, and TURN is published only when all three TURN fields are complete.
- Native video normalizes camera and display as independent streams, renegotiates an additional display sender without interrupting camera publication, and renders a bounded or floating workspace with a presentation stage and participant rail; Rails authorizes group mute against the call initiator, and the P2P topology remains intended for controlled small groups.
- Live calls require both account features, `chat_rooms` and the disabled-by-default `chat_room_calls`; `premium_call_connectivity` only selects the ICE transport. The Calls API returns `403 feature_disabled` and `RoomChannel` relays no SDP/ICE when either required feature is off, while stored call messages remain readable.
- Calls with three or more members track each invitee as `pending`/`joined`/`declined`; ringing continues until every invitee declines or the initiator ends the call.
- The audience comes from `OnlineStatusTracker`: a 1:1 call is not created when its recipient is offline, and group calls invite only members with `online` presence.
- Each call creates one `voice_call` `chat_room_message`; that record transitions through `ringing`, `in-progress`, `no-answer`, and `completed`, feeding room history and list previews in real time.
- The audio flow reuses the `FloatingCallWidget` visual pattern: logical RTL/LTR positioning, responsive width, `n-call-widget-*` tokens, status and identity hierarchy, and circular controls; it retains its own WebRTC state and keeps video isolated.
- Webhooks can emit chat room events without mixing the customer conversation contract.

### 3.4 Automation and bots

Supported events:

- Conversation created and updated.
- Message received and created.

- Delayed rules persist one claimed pending execution per rule, conversation, and state episode. The editor keeps the wait's structural conditions and supports additional conditions joined with `AND`; a scheduled worker revalidates the feature flag, rule, and every condition before firing. Status changes and replies invalidate the matching episode.

Conditions:

- Status, inbox, labels, language, attributes, content.
- Private note condition for internal-only workflows.

Actions:

- Assign agent or team.
- Assign last responding agent.
- Remove agent or team assignment.
- Labeling, status/priority update, webhook send, mute.

Flow Builder (ManyChat style):

- Persistence: `flow_builder_flows` (draft in `definition`), `flow_builder_flow_versions` (published snapshots), `flow_builder_flow_inboxes` (AgentBot-style start per inbox), `flow_builder_executions` and `flow_builder_sessions` (the step each conversation is waiting at). Execution FKs are `on_delete: :cascade` to conversations and `:nullify` to messages, and `Conversation` declares `has_many :flow_builder_executions`/`:flow_builder_sessions` with `dependent: :delete_all`, so deleting an inbox or conversation that ran a flow no longer fails with `PG::ForeignKeyViolation`.
- AgentBot-style start with several inboxes (S75): the trigger stores `inbox_ids` (the legacy `inbox_id` is still read through `FlowBuilder::Flow.trigger_inbox_ids` and `triggerInboxIds` in the editor); the Inicio editor picks them with `WebhookInboxMultiselectDropdown` (the webhook form inbox picker). Publishing (`FlowBuilder::BotBindingSyncService`) needs at least one inbox, rejects any inbox with another published flow, an active AgentBot or Dialogflow, naming the inbox and the provider (`errors.flow_builder.inbox_bot_provider_conflict`, through `Inbox#conflicting_bot_provider(:flow_builder)`); a Captain assistant on the inbox does not block publishing and leaves exactly one active `flow_builder_flow_inboxes` row per selected inbox, removing deselected ones (their pending conversations reopen). Blocks that depend on the channel (interactive providers, templates, availability, reply window) use every selected inbox and offer what any of them offers (`flowInboxes`, `useInteractiveProviders().triggerInboxes`); "Template" mode picks from the start inboxes with templates, asks for the inbox only when more than one has them and stores `inbox_id` when the template is picked. Event start with inboxes: the same picker, optional (empty = every inbox); `FlowBuilder::EventTriggerService#event_inbox_matches?` requires `context['inbox_id']` among `trigger_inbox_ids` when there are any, and `FlowBuilder::ManualTestService` takes the test conversation from those inboxes in both modes. Switching to events still clears the inboxes. The card shows one or two inbox names, more as "N inboxes".
- AgentBot mode acts as an AgentBot (S76): publishing an AgentBot-mode flow creates or renames its `AgentBot` (`bot_type: flow_builder`, name = flow name, no access token) linked by `flow_builder_flows.agent_bot_id` (FK `on_delete: :nullify`; the flow points to the bot so the bot outlives the flow as attribution). `AgentBot.accessible_to` excludes `flow_builder` bots, so they stay out of Settings → Bots, `inboxes#set_agent_bot`, assignable bots and manual assignment. `Conversation#apply_bot_logic` resolves the bot as the active `AgentBotInbox` bot or `Inbox#flow_builder_agent_bot` and sets `ai_assignee` the same way (human assignee and `should_ignore_for_bot?` rules kept). `AgentBotListener` skips `flow_builder` bots. Removing a binding (deselect, pause, unpublish, event mode, delete) enqueues `ReopenPendingConversationsJob` with the flow bot id, which clears its ownership in that inbox and reopens the pending conversations; re-publishing reuses the bot. Flows published in AgentBot mode before this change get their bot on the next publish.
- AgentBot-mode messages and handoff (S76): when the published start is in AgentBot mode (`FlowBuilder::Flow#agent_bot_mode?`), `FlowBuilder::MessageSender` builds the flow's messages with the flow bot as sender (`Messages::MessageBuilder.new(bot, …)`, as AgentBot API messages do), so they are `bot_response?` (they clear `waiting_since`, keep the conversation pending and AgentBot webhooks skip them); event starts keep sending without a sender. The "Transfer to an agent" block (`FlowBuilder::HandoffNodeExecutor`) calls `Conversation#bot_handoff!` on a pending conversation: bot owner cleared, status open and the `conversation.bot_handoff` event (reported as `conversation_bot_handoff`).
- Account-scoped API: `/api/v1/accounts/:account_id/flow_builder/flows` with CRUD, `validate`, `publish`, `pause` and `test`; `/flows/:flow_id/executions` lists and shows executions. Permissions: Pundit admin-only and the `flow_builder` feature flag.
- Definition: `{ nodes, edges, metadata }`. Default output `right`; condition `true`/`false`; `message.data.buttons` `[{ id: "btn_…", title }]` (text mode options: max 10, title ≤ 20 up to 3 options and ≤ 24 from 4, each `id` is an output handle); `message.data.options_style` `{ bullet, number_format: dash|dot|paren, delivery: text|interactive }` (missing = `🔷`, `dash`, `text`); with `delivery: "interactive"`, `message.data.interactive` is the interactive composer config (`button` with `buttons: [{ id, title }]`, `list` with `list: { button, sections: [{ title?, rows: [{ id, title, description? }] }] }`, `cta_url` with `link: { text, url }`, `cta` with `actions: [{ type: url|call|copy, text, url|phone_number|copy_code }]` or `location_request`; `header`/`media`/`footer` per provider). Each button or row is a handle; `location_request` has the `location` handle; `save_reply_to` `{ model: "contact", key }`; `no_reply` `{ amount, unit: minutes|hours|days }` with the `no_reply` handle; `random_split.data.branches` `[{ id: "split_…", label, percent }]` (sum 100, handle = `id`); `captain.data` `{}` with no outputs; `trigger.data.once_per_conversation` (true on new flows, missing = false); `action.data.actions` ordered list of actions (old nodes with `action`/`action_params` keep working); `metadata.layout: "cards"` marks a flow already re-arranged for 260px cards.
- Runtime: `FlowBuilderListener` enqueues `FlowBuilder::EventTriggerJob`; `EventTriggerService` runs the published flows whose start matches and skips the ones with `once_per_conversation` when the conversation already has a non-test execution. `ExecutorService` runs trigger, message, question, condition, switch, action, handoff, wait, random_split, captain, end and the batch 1 blocks (below) (set and webhook are skipped as `unsupported_node`). Three kinds of start (`trigger.data.mode`); see "Start kinds".
- Load (high-load review): the listener queues `EventTriggerJob` only when a published flow of the account starts on the event (`FlowBuilder::PublishedStarts`, read from the published versions, cached per account and dropped on every flow commit; it also gates `message_sent`); resuming a waiting flow and cancelling sessions do not depend on it. Each flow of an event runs isolated (a failure while running stays as an error execution and is not retried; a failure before running is retried alone). `EventTriggerJob` and `SessionResumeJob` run on the `flow_builder` queue: the main Sidekiq consumes it unless `FLOW_BUILDER_IN_MAIN_SIDEKIQ=false`, and `config/sidekiq_flow_builder.yml` (`SIDEKIQ_FLOW_BUILDER_CONCURRENCY`, default 5) is the dedicated worker, a service of its own in `docker-compose.production.yaml`. AI blocks (ai_condition, ai_router, ai_classify, ai_summary, ai_translate, ai_extract) call the model within `Llm::Config.with_request_timeout` (`AI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` 30 s, `AI_MAX_RETRIES` 2). Execution events go only to who has the flow's executions open (`FlowBuilderExecutionsChannel`, administrators of the account, subscribed by `FlowExecutionsPanel`). A session that leaves waiting before its timer deletes its scheduled `SessionResumeJob` (found by score in Sidekiq's scheduled set). `ScheduleTickJob` runs on `medium`. The Code block runs `FLOW_BUILDER_CODE_CONCURRENCY` children per process (default 2). The async event listeners run isolated (`IsolatedListenerBroadcaster`): one that raises is logged and reported, and the next ones still get the event.
- Waiting for the customer: a message with buttons or `save_reply_to` is sent and pauses: it creates a `FlowBuilder::Session` (`wait_kind: reply`, partial unique index: one waiting session per conversation), leaves the execution `waiting` and schedules `FlowBuilder::SessionResumeJob` at `resume_at` (now + `no_reply`, or a 24h expiry with no route). The `wait` block pauses with `wait_kind: delay` and `WaitConfig.resume_at`. Resuming uses the session's flow version and keeps logging steps on the same execution.
- Retries and going back to a block: `message.data.retry` `{ message, max (1-5), resend_menu }`. A reply that matches no option (only with options or a location request) resumes by the `retry` route (`SessionResumeService#retry?`, context `retry`/`retries`) while `retries < max`: the executor runs the message again (`ExecutorService#ask_again`), sends `retry.message`, sends the menu again when `resend_menu` and pauses again with `retries + 1`; the invalid reply is not saved (`save_reply_to`). Once the tries run out it leaves by `right`. The `go_to` block (`data.target_node_id`) is a virtual edge (`FlowGraph.go_to_edges`, also `graph.js goToEdges`, the simulator and validation); a disabled one does not jump. `MAX_STEPS` (300) counts per run segment, since the last resume, within a cap of `MAX_EXECUTION_STEPS` (1000) per execution.
- Replies: an incoming message with a waiting `reply` session enqueues the resume and starts no other flows; a `delay` session also starts no flows. Matching: `content_attributes.interactive_reply.id` (Cloud, WAHA and UazAPI through `Whatsapp::InteractiveReply`), normalized title (NFKC, lowercase, no spaces) or 1-based number; anything else leaves through `right`. With `save_reply_to` it writes the button title or the text to the contact custom attribute.
- Cancellation: a non-private outgoing agent reply (`sender_type: User`), `conversation_resolved` (unless the flow resolved it), pausing the flow or a new pause in the same conversation cancels the session; the execution closes with `run_data.stopped_reason`. `SessionResumeJob` takes the session `with_lock` and only acts if it is still waiting and due, so a stale or duplicated job does nothing.
- Options per channel (`FlowBuilder::OptionsFormat`): by default `MessageSender` sends the text, a blank line and one line per option (`"🔷 1 - Title"`, with the block's bullet and numbering). With `delivery: "interactive"` it sends `data.interactive` as is as `content_attributes.interactive` (built by `Whatsapp::InteractivePayloadBuilder`, `WahaInteractivePayloadBuilder` and `UazapiInteractivePayloadBuilder`) when the inbox is WhatsApp Cloud or WAHA/UazAPI with their gate on (same rules as `Whatsapp::SendOnWhatsappService#unsupported_interactive?`) and the provider sends that type (`OptionsFormat::PROVIDER_TYPES`); otherwise it falls back to the same numbered text (options, or "text: link" for links and action buttons) and the step records `interactive_fallback: "text"`. `location_request` waits: a message with a `location` attachment leaves by `location` with `{ latitude, longitude, address }` in the step output (`{{ input.location.latitude }}` in the next block). List replies come back with the row id in `interactive_reply.id`; numbers are accepted as "4", "4 -", "4." or "4)".
- Interactive, every type: `BLOCK_TYPES` adds `carousel` (`cards: [{ media: { type, url }, body?, buttons: [{ id, title }] | link }]`, each quick reply is a handle), `poll` (`poll: { options: [titles], multiple_answers }`; output ids live in `message.data.poll_option_ids`, aligned by index, since the WAHA/UazAPI builders expect titles) and the types that expect no answer: `pix`, `order` (WAHA), `payment_request` (UazAPI), `event`, `location` (WAHA) and `voice_call`, sent as the composer does, through Direct Send (`direct_send: { header?, voice_call: { text, ttl_minutes } }`) and only with Direct Send and calling on for the inbox. The editor embeds the composer's fields (`CarouselEditor`, `PixFields`, `OrderFields`, `PaymentRequestFields`, `EventFields`, `LocationFields`, `CtaButtonsEditor`, `MediaUrlField`) with converters in `utils/interactiveFields.js`. Shared checks in `FlowBuilder::InteractiveTypeConfig` and `SendOnlyTypeConfig` (per-provider rules, like Cloud's Pix code and amount or WAHA's image-only cards, are applied by the editor with the flow's providers).
- Interactive availability (`utils/messageOptions.js#inboxInteractiveProvider`): the reply box rule (`ReplyBox.vue` `isWahaInteractiveInbox`/`isUazapiInteractiveInbox`/`interactiveProvider`, `ActionButtons.vue` `providerInteractive`): Cloud always; WAHA/UazAPI with the inbox JSON `waha_interactive_enabled`/`uazapi_interactive_enabled` (`_inbox.json.jbuilder`, i.e. `Whatsapp::WahaInteractiveGate`/`UazapiInteractiveGate.enabled_for?`: server or env, account feature and inbox switch with the Enterprise add-on). Without a provider the interactive mode is not offered; a block saved that way shows a Banner with "Switch to text" and the card reads "Will be sent as text"; the validator does not reject it.
- Text fallback (`OptionsFormat.fallback_text`, `FallbackMedia`): text header in bold, body, options (or the carousel's "card: button" lines) and footer in italics; the header file goes as an attachment of the same message (a file uploaded from the composer, `/storage/file/<key>`, is attached as is; any other public URL is downloaded with `SafeFetch`).
- Poll: sending it stores `poll_message_id` in the session context. A vote updates the message's `content_attributes.poll_votes` (`Whatsapp::PollVoteRecorder`); `FlowBuilderListener#message_updated` enqueues the resume when that poll's `content_attributes` changed, and `SessionResumeService` takes the first vote that is not the inbox's (`PollVoteService::OWN_VOTER`), leaves by the first selected option and saves all of them to `save_reply_to`; a vote taken back keeps waiting and later votes do nothing.
- Template (`message_type: template`): the same `template_params` as the reply box (`name`, `category`, `language`, `namespace`, `processed_params` with `header.media_url`, `body`, `buttons[].parameter`; Twilio: `name` = `friendly_name`, numbered `processed_params`) and `message.data.template_buttons` `[{ id, title }]` with the template's quick replies as handles. Offered where the reply box offers templates (`ReplyBox.vue` `showWhatsappTemplates`: the inbox has `message_templates`; `showContentTemplates`: Twilio WhatsApp). The reply leaves by the payload Cloud/360dialog echo (`interactive_reply.id`, incoming_message_base_service.rb `cloud_template_button_reply`) or by the button text (NotificaMe, Twilio). Older blocks with a template picked from the message box open in "Template" without being rewritten. It is the only mode that goes out outside the 24-hour window (existing window handling).
- Platform variables: the message picker inserts `{{contact.first_name}}`, `{{contact.phone}}`, `{{agent.name}}`, `{{inbox.name}}`, `{{contact.custom_attribute.x}}`… as campaigns and the reply box do (`MESSAGE_VARIABLES`). `ExpressionResolver` (with `liquid: true` for messages) leaves those keys, and any `contact/agent/conversation/inbox/account` key the payload lacks, to `Liquidable`, which renders them on message create with the drops (`ContactDrop`, `UserDrop` of the assignee, `ConversationDrop`, `InboxDrop`, `AccountDrop`) in the content, `content_attributes.interactive`, `direct_send` and `template_params.processed_params`; the flow's own paths (`{{ contact.phone_number }}`, `{{ message.content }}`, `{{ input.* }}`) are resolved by the flow first. `ParameterSchema` enables the interactive and template fields. Length and URL limits do not reject a field holding variables (`FieldLimits`). Fixed: a text with two expressions (`{{ a }} and {{ b }}`) was taken as a single one.
- Voice note: with text or options, `MessageSender` sends the voice note on its own first and the text after it (Cloud drops audio captions, whatsapp_cloud_service.rb:346; WAHA `/sendVoice` and Evolution only carry the file); the session sits on the text message. "Record audio" uses `WootWriter/AudioRecorder.vue` with the reply box format for the inbox (`utils/audioRecordFormat.js`).
- Panel (S26): Text | Interactive | Template segmented control, pinned live preview (`MessagePreview.vue` + `InteractiveBubble.vue`, also in "Test"), type with `OptionSelect`, header, body textarea with counter and "+ Variable", footer, compact rows (`OptionRows.vue`) and a folding "Response" with the outputs; on phones each section opens its own screen (`useMessagePanel`, `PanelSection.vue`).
- New blocks: `random_split` picks a weighted branch (`RandomSplitConfig`); `captain` (`CaptainNodeExecutor`) requires `captain_integration` and an assistant on the inbox, assigns with `Conversations::AssignmentService` (`assignee_type: Captain::Assistant`) and ends; if something is missing it marks the step as an error without retrying.
- Switch and Loop (S40, S41): `switch.data` `{ left, case_sensitive, cases: [{ id, label, operator, value }] }`; each case is evaluated like one If condition (`ConditionEvaluator` with `left`, same operators, unary ones without a value), the first match wins, otherwise `fallback`; switches saved with `field`/`attribute_key` keep evaluating as before (the editor moves them to `left` on the first edit, with their `case_sensitive`). `loop.data.collection` (variable or text): `LoopNodeExecutor` builds the list (array, JSON array, or text separated by commas or new lines, max 25 per pass; the rest is recorded as `ignored`), leaves by `item` with `{{ loop.item }}`, `{{ loop.index }}` (from 1) and `{{ loop.total }}` in the payload, and by `done` when the list ends or is empty; each arrival advances one item. The state lives in `run_data.loops` (`ExecutionLogger#loops`), so it survives waits and resumes. `FlowGraph` only accepts cycles through a loop's `item` output and warns when that path does not come back. `MAX_STEPS` (100) still counts every step of the execution. In the editor the Switch is "By value" and shares the field and value picker with the Condition (see "Condition and By value"); the Loop offers the lists of the blocks before it (`{{ vars.<kanban_search>.data }}`, `stripe_lookup` `subscriptions`/`invoices`) and `{{ conversation.labels }}`.
- JavaScript Code (S43): `code.data` `{ language: "javascript", code, result_name }`. `CodeNodeExecutor` builds `input` from the payload (`contact`, `conversation`, `message`, `inbox`, `account`, `agent` = assignee, `loop`, `vars`) and `CodeRunner` runs it in an isolated V8 (`mini_racer` gem) inside a Ruby process of its own per run (`Process.spawn`, no environment, no core dump, `rlimit_data` 2 GB, killed at 2.5 s): a huge allocation makes V8 abort its whole process even under heap flags, so it never runs inside Puma or Sidekiq. Inside: deep-frozen `input`, `"use strict"`, no `require`/`process`/`fetch`/timers, V8 `timeout` 1 s and `max_memory` 32 MB, no `WebAssembly`, the parent kills the child above 128 MB resident memory (checked every 10 ms) and runs at most 2 children per process, NUL characters are removed from the result, code ≤ 10,000 characters, a plain object result ≤ 32 KB (JSON built in V8). It leaves by `right` and keeps the object in `run_data.vars[result_name]` (`ExecutionLogger#vars`, survives waits and resumes); the payload offers it as `{{ vars.<name>.<key> }}` (messages, fields, If/Switch). On failure (exception, timeout, memory, invalid_result, result_too_large, language) it leaves by `error` with the reason in the step, or ends the run as an error when `error` is not connected. `FlowValidator`: code required and ≤ 10,000, `result_name` `/\A[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9_]{0,39}\z/` and unique in the flow (`ResultName`), `javascript` only. `BlockAvailability`: `code` only when `CodeRunner.available?` (gem in the bundle and V8 starts). Test does not run code: the tester picks Success/Error and types a sample result.
- HTTP request (S45): type `http_request` (legacy `webhook` nodes are still skipped as before). `data` `{ method, connection_id, url, query: [{key,value}], headers: [{key,value}], body_type: none|json|text, body, result_name }`; url/query/headers/body take variables (`ParameterSchema`, Liquid like messages through `LiquidVariables.render`). `HttpRequestBuilder` joins the connection base URL and the path (an absolute URL or one leaving that origin is refused), encodes the query, applies default headers, the block headers (`HttpHeaders`: RFC 9110 names, no authorization/cookie/host/transport, no CR/LF once rendered, ≤ 16) and the connection auth last. `HttpClient` sends through `SsrfFilter` (SafeFetch's guard: public IPs only, every redirect checked again, credentials dropped on cross-origin redirects; `SAFE_FETCH_ALLOW_PRIVATE_NETWORK` as in SafeFetch), opens in 10 s, reads in 30 s, stops above 1 MB, keeps status, body (JSON when it parses, text otherwise, NUL removed) and a safe header subset (never Set-Cookie) in `run_data.vars[result_name]`. It leaves by `right` on 2xx; another status (the answer stays in vars), timeout, blocked, too_large, invalid_url, invalid_header, connection or request_failed leave by `error` or end the run when `error` is not connected. Steps keep method, URL, status and a body excerpt; never request headers or credentials. Flow test runs send GET; other methods are recorded as `would_send` and leave by `right` with an empty result. `POST /flow_builder/http_requests/test` (admin) sends the editor config with the sample data already in place and returns status, safe headers and body (≤ 20,000 characters). Always available (behind `flow_builder`). Phase E: the editor asks for the method and URL first, and the connection after them as "Authentication (optional): saved credential"; `response_keys` (editor only, the run ignores it) keeps the body keys "Test request" answered so later blocks can pick `{{ vars.<name>.body.<key> }}`. `redact_response`: see "Privacy and retention".
- Connections (S46): `flow_builder_connections` table (account FK cascade, `name` unique per account, `base_url`, `auth_type` none/bearer/basic/api_key, `auth_config` jsonb encrypted with Active Record encryption when keys are set, `headers` jsonb). Admin API `api/v1/accounts/:id/flow_builder/connections` (`FlowBuilder::ConnectionPolicy`): token, password and key go out as `••••••`; an update with the mask keeps the stored value; `destroy` answers 422 with the names of the flows whose draft or published version uses it. Frontend: `flowBuilderConnections` Vuex module, `ConnectionsPanel` (SidePanel list + form with the Captain custom tool `AuthConfig`/`HeadersConfig`), opened from "Connections" in the flows list, the editor ⋮ menu and the block's "New connection".
- New blocks, batch 1 (S29–S39): `business_hours` (`Inbox#working_now?`, outputs `in_hours`/`out_of_hours`), `agents_available` (`scope: inbox|team`, `team_id`; `online` status from `OnlineStatusTracker`, outputs `yes`/`no`), `kanban_stage` (`funnel_id`, optional `funnel_stage`, `yes`/`no`), `kanban` (`mode: create|move|status`, `funnel_id`, `funnel_stage`, `status: won|lost|open`; like the Automation Kanban actions; moving runs the stage automations), `ai_condition` (`description` ≤ 500; `FlowBuilder::AiCondition` inherits `Captain::AutomationConditionService` with credits, `Llm::UsageTracker` and charging, and returns `nil` when Captain cannot answer → `error` output; dry runs stop without calling the model), `update_fields` (`updates: [{ target: contact|contact_attribute|conversation_attribute, key, value }]`, checks the attribute exists, casts number and checkbox), `macro` (`macro_id`; `global` macros only, `Macros::ExecutionService` as the `created_by` account member; restores `Current` afterwards, and the listener does not take a message `performed_by` the flow as an agent reply), `task` (`activity_type_id`, `title`/`description` with variables, `due: { amount, unit }`, `priority`; `AccountTasks::UpsertService`) and `calendar_event` (`title`/`description`, `start: { amount, unit }`, `duration_minutes`, `google_meet`; `CalendarEvents::CreateService`, `source: automation`, Google sync and Meet only with an outbound connection). `responsible` (`FlowBuilder::ResponsibleResolver`): `mode: conversation_assignee|agent|rotation`, `agent_id`, `rotation: { source: team|agents, team_id, agent_ids, online_only }`, `fallback_agent_id`; the rotation turn is a `Redis::Alfred.incr` per flow and block. The texts of these blocks render the platform variables with the same drops (`LiquidVariables.render`). An error in these blocks marks the step as an error and stops the flow without retrying (`FlowBuilder::BlockError`, `run_block`). `business_hours`, `agents_available` and `kanban_stage` are no longer in the library (see "Retired blocks"); `task` and `calendar_event` gain `operation` (see "Task and Event").
- Availability (S39): `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/flow_builder/block_availability` (admin) returns `{ blocks: { type: bool }, actions: { add_sla, create_kanban_item, …: bool }, inboxes: { id: { captain } }, calendar: { google_sync } }` from `FlowBuilder::BlockAvailability` (Kanban: `kanban_board` + `ka` license; task: `activities` + `activities` integration; event: `google_calendar_integration`; macro: `macros`; AI: `captain_classifier` + credits; Captain: `captain_integration` + an assistant per inbox). The library hides what is unavailable and `ExecutorService#run_handler` checks the gate again when it runs. `block_reasons` (additive, only the unavailable blocks) says why: `reason_code` (`feature_disabled`, `license_inactive`, `integration_disabled`, `credits_exhausted`, `edition_unavailable`, `translation_service_mismatch`, `runtime_unavailable`), the missing `required_feature`, `required_license` or `required_integration`, and the `message` a run logs as the step error (`BlockAvailability#reason_detail_for`; `unavailable_reason` keeps answering that same text).
- Automation actions (S42): the `action` block adds `send_email_to_team` (`action_params: [{ team_ids, message }]`; `FlowBuilder::ActionExecutor` repeats `AutomationRules::ActionService#send_email_to_team`: `TeamNotifications::AutomationNotificationMailer#conversation_creation` per team of the account, stops at `within_email_rate_limit?` and calls `increment_email_sent_count`; the message resolves flow variables through `ParameterSchema` `action` and renders platform variables with `LiquidVariables.render`) and `add_sla` (`Enterprise::ActionService#add_sla` through `ActionService`). `ActionExecutor::ACTION_FEATURES` (`add_sla` → `sla`, the same flag as `AutomationRuleForm.vue`) feeds `block_availability` `actions: { add_sla }` so the editor hides it, and is checked again at runtime: with the flag off the action is recorded as `status: unavailable` with `error`, nothing is applied and the other actions and the flow go on. The `kanban` block adds `mode` `assign` (`agent_id`, `KanbanItem#assign_agent`), `note` (`note` with variables, `KanbanItem#add_note(text:, agent_id: Current.user&.id)`, as the automation does: no note webhook) and `start_timer`/`stop_timer` (`KanbanItem#start_timer`/`#stop_timer`, with their activity); without a card they record `skipped: no_card` and go on; test runs touch nothing. Send message already attaches files and records audio (`MessageSender#normalized_attachments`); S47 also adds `send_attachment` to Actions. The editor now offers `start_timer`/`stop_timer` only on blocks that already use them and, without a card, the `kanban` block leaves by `no_card` (see "Kanban by card").
- Every Automation action (S47): the `action` block adds `send_message` (`[text]`), `send_attachment` (`[{ attachments }]`, the Send message attachment shape, uploaded with `useAttachmentUpload`) and the 6 Kanban actions with the automation's params (`create_kanban_item`/`move_kanban_item_to_stage` `[{ funnel_id, funnel_stage }]`, `assign_agent_to_kanban_item` `[agent_id]`, `add_note_to_kanban_item` `[text]`, `start_kanban_item_timer`/`stop_kanban_item_timer` `[]`). The Kanban ones run the Kanban block's code (`ActionExecutor::KANBAN_ACTIONS` → `KanbanNodeExecutor#result` with a `mode`): without a card they record `status: skipped, reason: no_card`; a funnel or stage that no longer exists records `status: error` on that action; either way the other actions go on. Their gate is the Kanban block's (`ACTION_BLOCKS` → `BlockAvailability#unavailable_reason('kanban')`), returned in `block_availability` `actions` and checked again at runtime (`status: unavailable`). Messages go out through `FlowBuilder::MessageSender` like a Send message block (`content_attributes.flow_builder`, `Current.executed_by` = the flow, platform Liquid on create); outside the reply window (S38) they record `status: skipped, reason: outside_window` and send nothing. Flow variables in the texts through `ParameterSchema` `action` `actions.*.action_params.*` (this also reaches `add_private_note` and the webhook URL). Test runs send and touch nothing. `sleep_m` and `change_status` are not added: hint rows in the picker (`hint_wait`, `hint_status`) point to the Wait block and the status actions. Since phase D, `send_message`, `send_attachment`, the Kanban actions and the comment actions are no longer offered for new rows (see "Actions").
- Schedule message (S48): type `schedule_message`, `data` `{ content, attachments, when: { mode: after_interval|at_specified_time, amount, unit | run_at }, recurrence: { recurrence_type, recurrence_interval, recurrence_days, recurrence_end_type, recurrence_end_date, recurrence_max_occurrences }, responsible }`. `ScheduleMessageNodeExecutor` creates the conversation's `ScheduledMessage` as `Conversations::ScheduledMessagesController#create` does (the conversation inbox, `status: pending`, files as `scheduled_attachments` from the same blobs `MessageSender#attachments` reads) with `user` = the Responsible (`ResponsibleResolver`); `ScheduledMessageJob` sends it later in that agent's name and checks the 24-hour window when sending, not when scheduling. `when` follows `WaitConfig` (`run_at` takes variables; platform ones are rendered with `LiquidVariables.render` before the date is read with `Time.zone.parse`); an invalid or past date fails the step. The text resolves flow variables when scheduling and leaves platform ones to Liquidable when it is sent; the repetition is validated at publish with the rules of `ScheduledMessage#validate_recurrence_settings`. It leaves by `right`. Gate `schedule_messages` in `BlockAvailability`, checked again at run time (that flag only existed in the frontend before). Tests: records `would_schedule` and creates nothing; "Probar" shows it as a system line. Limit: the later send is done by `ScheduledMessageJob` as the agent, without the flow as actor. Phase E: `when.time_zone` (an IANA zone such as `America/Asuncion`; an unknown one is rejected) reads `run_at` in that zone; without it, it is read in the server zone (UTC), as on blocks saved before, and the editor sets the account's reports time zone when the date is picked. `error` output (a date that cannot be read or is past, nobody to send it, a refused message); unconnected, the step fails as before. The `responsible` is "Sent by (agent)".
- Cancel scheduled messages (S49): type `cancel_scheduled`, no data. `CancelScheduledNodeExecutor` moves every `pending` `ScheduledMessage` of the conversation to `cancelled` with the same `update!(status: :cancelled)` as the Cancel button (`ScheduledMessagesController#cancel`); a repeating one stops too, since its next occurrence is only created when it is sent. Outputs `cancelled` (the step keeps `cancelled` and `scheduled_message_ids`) and `none`. Gate `schedule_messages`, checked again at run time. Tests: counts the pending ones (`would_cancel`) without cancelling them and follows the output it would take; "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch. `data.scope`: `flow` (only the ones this flow scheduled, `scheduled_messages.flow_builder_flow_id`; the default for new blocks) or `all` (every pending one, the agents' included; the value when it is absent, as on blocks saved before).
- Ask for CSAT (S50): type `csat`, no data. `CsatNodeExecutor` calls `CsatSurveyService#perform` (the one `CsatSurveyListener` runs on resolve) inside the run, so the `input_csat` message is created with the flow as actor; it leaves by `sent` when a new survey was created and by `not_sent` otherwise, with `reason` (`not_resolved`, `csat_disabled`, `contact_opted_out`, `already_sent`, `outside_window`, or `survey_settings` when the label rules or a template that did not go out stopped it). No account gate: availability adds `inboxes[id].csat` (`csat_survey_enabled`) and the library offers it only where the start inbox (or some inbox, for all) has CSAT; a block in an inbox without CSAT shows its notice and leaves by `not_sent`. Tests: works out the reason without sending (`would_send`) and follows the output it would take; "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch. When a flow starts on `conversation_resolved`, the async `CsatSurveyListener` also tries to send it; `csat_already_sent?` avoids the duplicate when one finishes first. `data.resolve_first` ("Resolve first"): first resolves the conversation, as the `resolve_conversation` action does, then asks for the survey; without it, it only asks. Right after it, `{{ input.reason }}` holds the `not_sent` reason (a condition picks it by its Spanish name).
- Add participant (S51): `add_participant` action of the `action` block, `action_params: [<agent id>]`. `FlowBuilder::ActionExecutor#add_participant` does what `Conversations::ParticipantsController#create` does: `conversation.conversation_participants.find_or_create_by!(user_id:)` (one participation, `conversation_participant_added` notification); the model refuses an agent without access to the inbox and that `RecordInvalid` becomes the action `status: error` without stopping the others. No gate. Tests and "Probar": nobody is added (`dry_run`). In the editor it is a flow-only action (`FLOW_ONLY_ACTION_TYPES` in `actionCatalog.js`) with an agent picker
- Contact note (S52): `add_contact_note` action of the `action` block, `action_params: [<text>]`. `FlowBuilder::ActionExecutor#add_contact_note` creates the note as `Contacts::NotesController#create` does (`contact.notes.create!`), with no `user_id` since the flow writes it; the text resolves flow variables (`ParameterSchema` `action`) and platform variables with `FlowBuilder::LiquidVariables.render`. An empty text (also one that comes out empty once resolved) is the action `status: error` and the others go on; validation asks for the text (`params_missing?` / `actionParamsMissing`). No gate. Tests and "Probar": no note is created
- Link company (S53): type `company`, `data: { name, domain }` (flow and platform variables: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`, rendered with `FlowBuilder::LiquidVariables`). `FlowBuilder::CompanyNodeExecutor` normalizes the domain (lowercase, no scheme, `www.` or path; from an email it keeps what follows `@`), looks `account.companies` up by `domain` or, without one, by exact case-insensitive name; when none exists it creates it (`create!`, name derived from the domain as `Contacts::CompanyAssociationService` does when missing) and links the contact with `Companies::ContactMembershipService#assign` (the one `Companies::ContactsController#create` uses: `counter_cache`, `company_name`, activity; it replaces a company the contact had). Outputs `found` / `created`; an invalid domain or a name that comes out empty fails the step. Gate `companies` + the Enterprise overlay (`BlockAvailability` `enterprise: true`), checked again at run time. Tests: only looks it up (`would_create`), never creates (a new company fetches its favicon and may start the paid enrichment) nor links; "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch
- Find contact (S54): type `contact_lookup`, `data: { field: email|phone_number|identifier|custom_attribute, attribute_key, value, result_name: "contacto1" }` (`value` takes flow and platform variables). `FlowBuilder::ContactLookupNodeExecutor` runs `Contacts::FilterService` with `equal_to` (the Contacts filter rules: case-insensitive, phone with or without `+`, contact custom attributes defined in the account, `resolved_contacts` base) and keeps the oldest (`reorder(:id)`). Found: it stores in `ExecutionLogger#vars[result_name]` the same `contact_snapshot` as `{{ contact }}` (name, phone_number, email, identifier, avatar, id, custom_attributes, additional_attributes), read as `{{ vars.contacto1.name }}`, and leaves by `found`; no match or an empty value leaves by `not_found`; an unknown attribute fails the step. No gate. Test runs look it up too (read only); "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch and on Found uses the sample contact as the result. The variable picker offers `vars.<name>.name/email/phone_number/identifier/id` under "Results" to the blocks that follow
- Has an open task? (S56): type `task_condition`, `data: { status: open|overdue|pending|in_progress, activity_type_id }` (type optional). `FlowBuilder::TaskCondition` runs `AccountTasks::FilterService` (the activities list filters: `open` = pending or in progress, `overdue` = open with `ends_at` past, `pending`/`in_progress` without the overdue ones, `activity_type_id`) over `account.account_tasks.where(conversation_id:)`; outputs `yes` / `no`, the step records `count` and up to 10 `task_ids`. Gate G-ACT: `activities` + enabled `activities` hook (`BlockAvailability`), checked again at run time. Read only, so test runs look it up too; "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch. Retired from the library: see "Retired blocks".
- Update task (S55): type `task_update`, `data: { target: flow|conversation, operation: complete|cancel|reopen, outcome_summary }` (summary with flow and platform variables: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`). `flow` = the task id the latest earlier `task` step of this execution left in its output (`ExecutionLogger#last_output('task', :task_id)`, also after a wait: steps read back from `run_data`); without one, the latest task this flow created on the conversation in an earlier run (`account_tasks.flow_builder_flow_id`; open for complete or cancel, closed for reopen). `conversation` = the conversation's open tasks (`AccountTasks::FilterService` status `open`). `FlowBuilder::TaskUpdateNodeExecutor` changes each one with `AccountTasks::UpsertService` (status `completed` / `cancelled` / `pending`, `outcome_summary` when closing; as `AccountTasksController#update`: due notification reset, recurrence, Kanban history, calendar sync) in the name of the task's assignee or, without one, its author. Outputs `updated` / `not_found`; a summary that comes out empty, a task without assignee or author or a refused record fail the step. Validation: known change and target, summary to complete/cancel, `reopen` only with `flow`. Gate G-ACT, checked again at run time. Tests: nothing changes (`would_update`); "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch. Retired from the library: it is now `task` with `operation` (see "Task and Event").
- Reschedule or cancel event (S57): type `calendar_update`, `data: { operation: reschedule|cancel, start: { amount, unit } }`.
