The event = the `event_id` the latest earlier `calendar_event` step of this execution left in its output (`ExecutionLogger#last_output`, also after a wait); without one, the latest still scheduled event this flow scheduled on the conversation in an earlier run (`calendar_events.metadata.flow_builder_flow_id`), and without that it leaves by `not_found`. `FlowBuilder::CalendarUpdateNodeExecutor` calls `CalendarEvents::UpdateService` as `CalendarEventsController` does: `reschedule` = `starts_at` now + interval, `ends_at` keeping the duration, `status: scheduled`; `cancel` = `{ status: :cancelled }` (as `#destroy`). The service resets the reminder and pushes to Google only under its rules (outbound connection; scheduled events with `sync_enabled`, which S37 sets only with `google_sync?`; a cancel of an event never synced only marks it locally). Outputs `updated` / `not_found`; a refused record fails the step. Gate `google_calendar_integration` (as `calendar_event`), checked again at run time. Tests: nothing changes; "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch. Retired from the library: it is now `calendar_event` with `operation` (see "Task and Event").
- Kanban card checklist (S64): `kanban` block modes `checklist_add` / `checklist_complete` with `data.checklist_item` (flow and platform variables: `ParameterSchema` `kanban` + `LiquidVariables`), and the flow-only Acciones actions `add_kanban_checklist_item` / `complete_kanban_checklist_item` (`action_params: [<text>]`, `ActionExecutor::KANBAN_ACTIONS` → the same modes, `FLOW_ONLY_ACTION_TYPES` with `flow_text`). `FlowBuilder::KanbanNodeExecutor` uses `KanbanItems::ChecklistService` with `KanbanWebhookService` as `KanbanItemsController` does: `create_item(text:)`, or `update_item(id, completed: true)` on the first item whose text matches (stripped, case-insensitive), an open one before a completed one (due notifications, webhooks, the card's `handle_checklist_changes` automations). No card → `skipped: no_card`; no such item → `skipped: no_checklist_item`; a service error (an item before its start date) or a text that comes out empty fail the step, or the action with `status: error` while the next actions run. Gate G-KANBAN (`kanban_board` + license; the actions through `ACTION_BLOCKS`, hidden in the picker when off), checked again at run time. Tests: no card is touched. Phase D: the checklist actions are no longer offered for new Actions rows; without a card the block leaves by `no_card`, and the editor offers the funnels' `stages.<stage>.checklist_templates` items.
- Choose an option with AI (S58): type `ai_router`, `data: { options: [{ id: "route_<random>", label, criteria }] }` (2 to 10 options, `criteria` ≤ 500). `FlowBuilder::AiRouter` inherits `Captain::AutomationConditionService` (same `conversation_classification` model route, credits, `Llm::UsageTracker` and `automation_condition` charge) and asks `Captain::DecisionService` one `choice` question with one criterion per option (`option_N` → "label: criteria") plus `none`. It leaves by the picked option's `id`, by `fallback` ("None") or by `error` when Captain cannot answer or there are no credits (`credits: :output` in `BlockAvailability::GATES`: the library hides it without credits, but at run time the block leaves by `error`; with nothing connected there, the step fails and the run ends). Gate `captain_classifier`. Dry runs stop without calling the model (`would_ask`); "Probar" lets the tester pick the branch
- Suggest labels or priority with AI (S59): type `ai_classify`, `data: { target: labels|contact_labels|priority }`. `FlowBuilder::AiClassifyNodeExecutor` calls `Captain::ConversationClassifierService#labels/#contact_labels/#priority` (the one behind the sidebar suggestions) and, like the `label_suggestion` task (`TasksController#label_suggestion`), checks the credits (`BlockAvailability#captain_credits?`), wraps the call in `Llm::UsageTracker` and charges one AI Action (`label_suggestion` / `priority_suggestion`) only when it suggested something. It applies them with `FlowBuilder::ActionExecutor` (`add_label`, `add_contact_label`, `change_priority`, the Actions block actions). Leaves by `right`, or by `error` when Captain cannot answer (`Captain::DecisionService::Error`) or there are no credits. Gate `captain_tasks` + `captain_classifier` (`feature` takes several flags), `credits: :output`. Dry runs stop without calling the model (`would_classify`)
- Summarize conversation (S60): type `ai_summary`, `data: { private_note: boolean, result_name: "resumen1" }`. `FlowBuilder::AiSummaryNodeExecutor` calls `Captain::SummaryService#perform` (its Enterprise wrapper `Enterprise::Captain::BaseTaskService` checks the credits, wraps it in `Llm::UsageTracker` and charges one AI Action; it returns `{ error: }` instead of raising), keeps `{ summary }` in `ExecutionLogger#vars[result_name]` (`{{ vars.resumen1.summary }}`, picker "Results") and with `private_note` adds the private note through `FlowBuilder::ActionExecutor` (`add_private_note`). Leaves by `right`, or by `error` with Captain's message. Gate `captain_tasks`, `credits: :output`. Dry runs stop without calling the model (`would_summarize`), so the next blocks are not tested; "Probar" uses a sample summary when Done is picked
- Translate (S61): type `ai_translate`, `data: { text, target_language, result_name: "traduccion1" }` (`text` takes flow and platform variables: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`; languages from `advancedFilterItems/languages`). `FlowBuilder::AiTranslateNodeExecutor` calls `Captain::Llm::MessageTranslationService` (Enterprise; its `Enterprise::Captain::BaseTaskService` wrapper checks credits, `Llm::UsageTracker` and charging), keeps `{ translation }` in `ExecutionLogger#vars[result_name]` (`{{ vars.traduccion1.translation }}`) and leaves by `right`, or by `error` with Captain's message or without text. Gate `auto_translate` + `captain_tasks` + `enterprise: true` + `translation: 'captain'` (`Account#translation_service`, as the Translate action; new `translation_reason`, checked after the edition), `credits: :output`. Dry runs render the text and stop without calling the model (`would_translate`); "Probar" uses a sample translation when Done is picked. `target_language: conversation_language` ("The contact's language") translates into the language detected for the conversation; without one it leaves by `error`.
- Captain custom tool (S63): type `custom_tool`, `data: { tool_id, params: { <name>: <value with variables> }, result_name: "herramienta1" }` (`ParameterSchema` `params.*` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`). `FlowBuilder::CustomToolNodeExecutor` finds it in `account.captain_custom_tools`, refuses tools that are off, `requires_email_verification?` or `risk_level: high`, casts each parameter to its `param_schema` type and sends the request as Captain does: `build_request_url`/`build_request_body`, `Captain::Tools::HttpTool#execute_http_request` (auth, metadata headers with the context of `Captain::Tools::CustomHttpTool#build_tool_context`, private IPs blocked, 10/30 s, 1 MB; the tools controller test action already calls it this way) and `format_response`. It skips `Captain::Runtime::ToolExecutionService`: its answer is the same text on success and failure, and its ledger (key assistant + conversation + tool + params, no message) would replay a write tool's result on the flow's next run. It keeps `{ response }` (parsed when JSON) in `ExecutionLogger#vars[result_name]`; leaves by `right` (2xx) or `error`. No assistant in the inbox: error step. Gate `custom_tools` + `enterprise: true`; the library needs an assistant per inbox (`inboxes[id].captain`). Dry runs never call it (`would_call` with the params) and go on by `right` with an empty response; "Probar" uses the answer the tester types. In the editor it is "Captain tool": it shows each parameter's type and marks the required ones from `param_schema`; a required one left empty after its variables are rendered leaves by `error` without calling the tool.
- Extract contact data with AI (S62): type `ai_extract`, `data: { mode: attributes|notes, result_name: "datos1" }`. `FlowBuilder::AiExtractNodeExecutor` uses the Enterprise subclasses `Captain::Llm::FlowBuilderContactAttributesService` / `FlowBuilderContactNotesService` (of `ContactAttributesService` / `ContactNotesService`, with the inbox's assistant; `Captain::Llm::FlowBuilderFailures` turns a failed model call into `Failed` instead of the parents' `[]`). Attributes: `{ attribute => value }` kept in `ExecutionLogger#vars[result_name].attributes`, nothing written; notes: `generate_and_update_notes` writes contact notes (and the assistant's runtime memories) and keeps them in `.notes`. Like `ai_classify`: `BlockAvailability#captain_credits?`, `Llm::UsageTracker.track` (`contact_attributes` / `contact_notes`) and one AI Action only when something was found. Leaves by `right`, or by `error` (failed call, no credits, no assistant). Gate `captain_integration` + `enterprise: true`, `credits: :output`, per inbox like `custom_tool` (`inboxes[id].captain`). Dry runs stop without calling the model (`would_extract`); "Probar" uses sample values. Phase E: `attribute_keys` (a closed list of contact attribute keys: only those are kept, matched by key or display name) and `save_to_contact` ("Save to the contact", needs `attribute_keys`), which writes the attributes found to the contact.
- Look up customer in Stripe (S65): type `stripe_lookup`, `data: { result_name: "stripe1" }`, outputs `found` / `not_found` / `error`. `FlowBuilder::StripeLookupNodeExecutor` runs `Integrations::Stripe::CustomerSummary` with `Integrations::Stripe::Client` and the enabled `stripe` hook's key, as `StripeController#customer`: a hook with `reauthorization_required?` leaves by `error` without calling, `AuthenticationError` calls `prompt_reauthorization!`. Several customers with the email: the first one (second `perform(customer_id:)`). `vars[result_name]`: `customer_id`, `name`, `email`, `phone`, `subscription_status` (latest), `subscriptions`, `invoices`. Gate `integrations` + `stripe_integration` + hook `stripe`. Dry runs stop without calling Stripe (`would_lookup`); "Probar" fills the customer from the sample contact
- Hand over to Typebot / Dialogflow (S66): type `bot_handoff`, `data: { provider: typebot|dialogflow, agent_bot_id }`, terminal like `captain` (`TERMINAL_NODE_TYPES`, simulator `STOP_TYPES`). `FlowBuilder::BotHandoffNodeExecutor`: typebot → `Conversations::AssignmentService` with `assignee_type: 'AgentBot'` (no agent, `pending`, the `ai_assignee` `AgentBotListener` sends the next messages to) and the incoming message to `AgentBots::TypebotService#process_message_event`, unless the bot already had the pending conversation; dialogflow → the inbox's enabled `dialogflow` hook (else `BlockError`), `pending!` and `HookJob.perform_later(hook, 'message.created', message:)` as `HookListener`. No agent-bot model change. Gate: new `any:` kind in `BlockAvailability::GATES` (`typebot` flag, or `integrations` + `dialogflow_integration` + hook `dialogflow`), offered when one option is available; `#option_reason` re-checks the chosen bot when it runs; `as_json[:bot_handoff]` = `{ typebot:, dialogflow: }` for the editor. Dry runs record `would_handoff`. The editor names the start's inboxes that have no Dialogflow integration.
- Post in the internal chat (S67): type `team_chat_message`, `data: { chat_room_id, text, responsible }` (`text`: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`). `FlowBuilder::TeamChatMessageNodeExecutor` posts with `ChatRooms::MessageCreateService` (as `ChatRoomMessagesController#create`) in an active text channel (`text_channels.active`); P1: the sender is the block's "Post as" agent (`FlowBuilder::ResponsibleResolver`, as `schedule_message`), who must be `ChatRoom#member?` (the chat page's `authorize_member!`), else `BlockError`. One output (`right`). Gate `chat_rooms`. Dry runs record `would_post` without posting (the rotation is peeked). Retired from the library: it is now `notify_agent` with `mode: room` (see "Alert the team, Transfer and End").
- Notify an agent (S68): type `notify_agent`, `data: { text, responsible }` (`text`: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`, up to 1000 characters). `FlowBuilder::NotifyAgentNodeExecutor` resolves the agent with `FlowBuilder::ResponsibleResolver` (as `team_chat_message`) and creates the notification with `NotificationBuilder`: new type `flow_builder_notification` (value 16, appended to `Notification::NOTIFICATION_TYPES`), primary actor the conversation, `meta: { message, flow_id, flow_name }`; push title `notifications.notification_title.flow_builder_notification` (flow name), push and list body `meta.message`; regular conversation pipeline (`PushNotificationJob`, `EmailNotificationJob` with `ConversationNotificationsMailer#flow_builder_notification`, which reads the text from the agent's latest flow notice of the conversation, and `RemoveDuplicateNotificationJob`, which keeps only the newest notification per agent and conversation). Push flag on for new users (`AccountUser#create_notification_setting`) and existing ones (migration `20261027130000_enable_flow_builder_notification_push`, only bit `1 << 15`); email off. `NotificationBuilder` skips agents without access to the conversation (`ConversationPolicy#show?`) and blocked contacts: `BlockError`. One output (`right`), no gate. Dry runs record `would_notify` (the rotation is peeked). Phase D: it is "Alert the team", with `data.mode` `notification` (default, also when absent) or `room` (see "Alert the team, Transfer and End").
- Dyte video call (S69): type `dyte_meeting`, `data: { responsible }`, outputs `right` / `error`. `FlowBuilder::DyteMeetingNodeExecutor` calls `Integrations::Dyte::ProcessorService#create_a_meeting(agent)` (the reply box's `VideoCallButton` / `DyteController#create_a_meeting`): the meeting plus its `integrations` call message sent as the agent; the message events carry the flow as `performed_by`, so `FlowBuilderListener` does not take it for an agent reply. P1: the agent is the block's `FlowBuilder::ResponsibleResolver` (assignee by default). Website and API inboxes only (`video_call_inbox?`, `BlockAvailability#inbox_gates` `inboxes[id].video_call`, `INBOX_GATES`), elsewhere `BlockError`. Gate `integrations` + `dyte_integration` + hook `dyte`. Dry runs record `would_create_meeting` and go on. The editor names the start's inboxes where it does not work; with `result_name` it keeps `meeting_id` and `message_id` (there is no shareable link: each participant joins with a token of their own).
- Advanced catalog action (S70): type `account_action`, `data: { action, params: { <path param>: value }, query: [{ key, value }], responsible, result_name: "accion1" }` (`params.*`, `query.*.value`: `ParameterSchema` + `LIQUID_NODE_TYPES`), outputs `right` / `error`. `FlowBuilder::AccountActionNodeExecutor::ALLOWED_ACTIONS` is an explicit allow-list of read-only `AccountActions::Definitions::COMMON_TOOL_SPECS` entries (contacts_get/search/conversations, contact_labels_list, contact_notes_list, conversations_get(_labels), labels_list, canned_responses_list, companies_get/search), also checked against `MegaAssistant::ActionCatalog#read_tool?` and each entry's `feature` (`#features_enabled?`); no writes, deletes, merges, bulk actions, users, billing, roles, settings or other accounts. It runs through `MegaAssistant::OperationService` (path parameter check) → `MegaAssistant::ApiDispatcher` (the real controller and policies, `Current` restored). P1: the actor is the block's "Run as" agent (`FlowBuilder::ResponsibleResolver`, library default: a chosen agent), with its own permissions, who must pass `MegaAssistantThreadPolicy.access?` (else `BlockError`); nothing is widened. Configuration is admin-only (`FlowBuilder::FlowPolicy`). `vars[result_name]`: `{ status, data }`. Gate `mega_ai_assistant`; `as_json[:account_actions]` = `{ <name> => { available, path_params } }` for the editor. Dry runs record `would_call` and go on with an empty answer. Phase D: "Read account data (advanced)". The editor offers only `contacts_conversations`, `contact_notes_list`, `conversations_get`, `messages_list`, `labels_list`, `canned_responses_list` and `companies_search` (`AccountActionNodeExecutor::OFFERED_ACTIONS`; the other names keep running in saved blocks); "Which contact?" / "Which conversation?" writes `{{ contact.id }}` or `{{ conversation.id }}` (or a variable) into the path id, and the query fields come from the action's `input_schema`. `messages_list` returns the latest messages with `message_type`, `private`, the sender (and its type), `sender_category` (`contact`, `human`, `bot`, `rule`, `campaign` or `system`, from `Message#sender_category`), `content_attributes` and `created_at`; `before` (a message id) in the query gets older ones. A failed action leaves by `error` when it is connected.
- Reply window (S38): `MessageSender#outside_window?` (`FlowBuilder::ReplyWindow`, `Conversation#can_reply?`) sends no text, interactive or files outside the window (templates and Direct Send exempt); the step is `skipped` with `outside_window` and leaves by the optional `window_closed` handle when connected, otherwise the execution ends with `run_data.stopped_reason: window_closed`; dry runs record `would_be_blocked`. The card shows the output only with inboxes that have a window or once it is connected. A block with no route follows its first connection other than `window_closed` and `no_reply` (`ExecutorService#normal_edges`), so the order they were connected in does not change the path of a sent message.
- Testing: `ManualTestService` (dry real test) never creates sessions or schedules jobs; it records `would_wait`. The editor's "Test" is an in-browser simulator (`utils/simulator.js`, `FlowSimulator.vue`) that walks the graph with sample data and never calls the backend. A Scheduled start is tested without a conversation, as if its slot were now; Card updated, on the latest card that has a conversation (`kanban.change` = `created`); Message sent by the team, on the latest public message an agent sent. `kanban_search` really searches (it only reads) and `run_flow` records `would_call` and starts nothing.
- Validation (`FlowValidator`, `ReplyConfig`): one start, existing edges, no cycles except the ones through a message that waits for a reply (not its `window_closed` or `no_reply` outputs) or a `loop`'s `item` output (`FlowGraph#cycle?`, same as `hasCycle` in `graph.js`), max 100 blocks, up to 10 options with a text (1–20 character titles up to 3 options and 1–24 from 4); in interactive mode, the composer limits: one of the `OptionsFormat::BLOCK_TYPES` types (see above), 1–3 unique buttons up to 20, 1–10 rows up to 24 with descriptions up to 72, list button 1–20, section title up to 24, header and footer up to 60, link with a text up to 20 and an http(s) URL, 1–5 action buttons with text and value, `save_reply_to.key`, `no_reply.amount > 0`, splits with at least 2 branches adding up to 100, `retry` with a message and `max` from 1 to 5, and `go_to` to an existing block other than the start and itself, with no connections of its own, and each drawn output `(source, sourceHandle)` leading to one block only (`FlowGraph#shared_output_errors`: `ExecutorService#next_node` follows only the first connection; in the editor `ADD_EDGE` replaces the connection of an output already in use). Warnings (with `warning_codes`: `orphan`, `unreachable`, `loop_no_return`): a loose block, a connected block no path from the start reaches (the walk includes the virtual `go_to` edges) and a `loop` without its way back. Suggestions (`suggestions`): an unconnected "Other reply" on text options (not interactive or template ones) and an unconnected `no_reply` on a message that waits for the reply with `no_reply.amount > 0`. In the editor `validateGraphLocally` returns `issues` `{ message, nodeId, level, code }`: the banner shows the first error or warning with "Show block", cards are marked with their short text, and an unconnected "Other reply" is `level: suggestion`, shown only on the block. After a valid server check the local warnings stay, and only server warnings with a code the editor does not know are added (`withServerWarnings`). New warnings: a block that acts on the comment under a start that never receives comments (it is skipped), and `set`/`webhook` (valid, but skipped). In the editor, a `kanban` `delete` on a loop's path without `confirm_loop_delete` and "Another card" with an id typed by hand block publishing.
- Frontend (`routes/dashboard/flowBuilder`): `FlowCanvas.vue` with `nodes/FlowNode.vue` cards (`cards/*` bodies, `VariableText.vue` renders variables as tags), per-row handles from `utils/graph.js#outputHandles`, zoom controls, "Arrange" and "+ Add block"; a 400px `FlowSidePanel.vue` with `BlockLibrary.vue` or the block editor (`editors/*`); `VariablePicker.vue` on top of `utils/variables.js`/`payloadSchema.js` inserts `{{ path }}`; below `md`, `FlowStepsList.vue` shows the step list and edits in a `BottomSheet`. The `flowBuilder` store drops edges whose handle no longer exists when a block is edited. Executions keeps the input/output panels (`FlowNodePayloadPanel`) and shows the `waiting` status. The `NodeInspector` modal, `FixedOrExpressionField`, `ConditionList`, `NodePalette`, `AddNodeMenu` and `usePanelLayout` were removed.
- Limits: question, set and webhook are not offered when adding blocks (Code is under Advanced; `FlowValidator` warns that set and webhook are skipped) (existing ones open in a simple editor); two near-simultaneous replies resume only once; Captain does not hand the conversation back to the flow.
- Editor redesign (S71): 480 px panel. Library: 2-column grid, chips per catalog section (`ChipGroup`), footer with the focused block's description; no "New" badge (`isNew` removed). Every editor opens with "What it does" (`useBlockSummary.summaryFor`, the card text) and ends with "Outputs" (`BlockOutputs`: `shownOutputHandles` + store edges). Kanban uses chips; Actions uses an in-panel grouped picker (`ActionPicker`, `groupedActionTypes`) with its own labels in `FLOW_BUILDER.ACTION_EDITOR.ACTIONS`. New keys under `FLOW_BUILDER.BLOCK_PANEL`.
- Test (S72): the simulator keeps visited blocks, taken outputs and followed edges (`simulationPath`); `FlowCanvas` hands them to `FlowNode` through `provide('flowTestPath')` (done in teal, waiting in amber with "Now", the rest faded; followed edges in teal). System lines carry `nodeId` and `handle` and render as step marks (`SimStepMark`); the decision card (`SimDecisionCard`) uses `titleFor`/`summaryFor`; Path tab (`SimRoute`). Output colors live in `components/simulator/handleTones.js`, shared with `FlowNode`.
- Flow list (S73): `GET /flow_builder/flows` returns `runs_today` and `errors_today` per flow, from one grouped query over today's executions in the account reporting timezone, excluding test runs (`run_data.execution_mode = 'test'`). The list (`components/list/FlowList.vue`, `FlowListRow.vue`) reads the trigger and the inbox from the definition; the whole row opens the flow.
- Executions (S74): the view reuses `FlowNode` in a read-only mode (`provide('flowExecution')`: status, duration, taken outputs). `runSteps()` (`utils/executions.js`) merges a paused step with the step that resumed it and marks the step a run is paused at as waiting. `ExecutionStepDetail` shows "What happened" (a sentence per step for message, condition, switch and actions; a generic one otherwise) and "Technical data" (input, output and configuration JSON). `ExecutionNode`, `ExecutionEdge`, `ExecutionNodeDataModal` and `FlowNodePayloadPanel` were removed. Phones: list → steps → bottom sheet. Phase G: see "Observability".
- Execution writes: `ExecutionLogger` writes `run_data` when a run starts, pauses and ends, and at most once per `FLUSH_INTERVAL` (5 s) in between, broadcasting `flow_builder.execution_updated` only on those writes (each write rewrites the whole jsonb). A run of a published version does not copy its definition: `run_data.definition_snapshot` is kept only by runs without `flow_version_id` (draft test runs), and `GET /executions/:id` fills it from the version, so the response is the same. `GET /executions` reads only the listed columns and `steps_count` from SQL (never `run_data`); `per_page` defaults to 25, at most 100.
- Comment received (social comments): start event `social_comment_received`, offered by `block_availability` (`events.social_comment_received`) only with the `social_comments` feature. `FlowBuilderListener` sends a comment as `social_comment_received` (it never resumes a waiting session), and `EventTriggerService` runs it while the feature is on, applying the start's optional filters through `FlowBuilder::CommentFilter`: `comment_platforms` (facebook / instagram; empty = both), `comment_post_url` (link or id compared without query, trailing slash, scheme or www), `comment_keywords` (any of them, case-insensitive) and `comment_top_level_only`. The same comment never runs a flow twice (`executions.message_id`, test runs excluded) and a deleted comment starts nothing. `action` block actions (`ACTION_FEATURES` → `social_comments`): `reply_to_comment` and `send_private_reply` (text with variables), and `hide_comment` and `delete_comment`; they act on the comment that started the run (also after a resume, from `execution.message`), are skipped with `no_comment` without one, and what Meta or the rules refuse (a second private reply, after 7 days, a deleted comment) is the block's error. Variables `{{comment.text}}`, `{{comment.platform}}`, `{{comment.permalink}}`, `{{comment.post_caption}}`, `{{comment.is_ad}}` and `{{comment.username}}` (on Facebook it falls back to the contact name). Phase E: the editor no longer offers the `comment_*` filters; a start that saved them keeps filtering, and "Move to a Condition" turns them into a condition after the start (`{{ comment.platform }}`, `{{ comment.permalink }}` or `{{ comment.post_id }}`, `{{ comment.is_reply }}`, `{{ comment.text }}`) and clears them. The comment actions are added as the comment blocks.
- Comments in "Message received" and AgentBot: start option `include_comments` ("Include post comments (Facebook/Instagram)" in `TriggerEditor`, shown when the comment event is available or when it is already on). `EventTriggerService#matching_event` runs the comment in the flows of its own event and, when a message start (`message_created`/`message_received` or `agent_bot` mode) has `include_comments`, as `message_created`, unless a session is waiting in the conversation. `already_ran?`: a comment is limited only per comment (`ran_for_comment?`, `executions.message_id`, test runs excluded); `once_per_conversation` still applies to DMs. `ExecutorService#comment_message` takes the comment from `session.execution.message` or from the message for `COMMENT_RUN_EVENTS`. `FlowBuilder::CommentStart.apply` (in `Flow#set_defaults`, before validation, and in `Flow#publish!`) turns `include_comments` on in a message start when the flow uses `{{ message.kind }}` in a `condition` block, an action from `ActionExecutor::COMMENT_ACTIONS` or a `message` block with a comment `send_as`; it never turns it off.
- Ignore contacts: start options `ignore_groups` and `ignored_targets` ("Ignore contacts" in `TriggerEditor`, in both modes and on every event), with the rules of AgentBots and Captain. `FlowBuilder::IgnoredContact` (identifier and `source_id`; entries with @ are identifiers, the others phone numbers, digits only) makes `EventTriggerService` skip the flow before "once per conversation"; in AgentBot mode a pending conversation goes to `open` without `ai_assignee`, as `AgentBots::WebhookFilterService#handle_ignored_contact!` does. Publishing copies both keys to the flow bot `bot_config` (`BotBindingSyncService`), so `Conversation#apply_bot_logic` opens the new conversations of an ignored contact for the agents. It only filters the start: a session already waiting carries on, and a test run does not apply it. The editor picks the contacts by searching them by name, phone or email and saves their phone number, or their identifier when they have none; saved values do not change.
- Type condition: `ExecutionPayloadBuilder` exposes `message.kind` (`comment` when `Message#social_comment?`, otherwise `direct_message`); `utils/conditions.js#conditionValueOptions` shows the value as a picker (`FLOW_BUILDER.CONDITIONS.VALUES.MESSAGE_KIND`), labeled "Type" in the "Last message" group.
- Comment blocks: `utils/nodeCatalog.js` adds the `comments` group (`FLOW_BUILDER.CATALOG.COMMENTS`, "Comments (Facebook and Instagram)") with four preset `action` blocks (`preset`: `reply_to_comment`, `send_private_reply`, `hide_comment`, `delete_comment`; copy in `FLOW_BUILDER.NODES.COMMENT_ACTIONS`); an Actions block holding a single comment action shows as that block (`commentPresetItem`).
- Send as (`send_as` of the `message` block, `FlowBuilder::CommentReplySender`): `direct_message` (default, also when absent), `comment_reply` (`SocialComments::PublicReplyService`) or `comment_private_reply` (`SocialComments::PrivateReplyService`). `FlowValidator` rejects unknown values and, in comment modes, attachments, files, templates, options, interactive messages or buttons (text with variables only, rendered with `LiquidVariables`). `ExecutorService#execute_comment_reply` applies neither the 24 h window nor a wait; without a comment the step is `skipped` with `no_comment` and goes on by the normal output; what Meta or the rules refuse (`CustomExceptions::SocialComment`) is the step's error and the run goes on; test runs record the preview. In `direct_message` mode the block sends a DM and honors the 24 h window, which a comment does not open. Phase D: the editor no longer offers the comment modes for new blocks and offers to turn the block into the comment block (an `action` with `reply_to_comment` or `send_private_reply`); saved ones keep running.
- Start kinds (S13): `trigger.data.mode` is `event` (default), `agent_bot`, `schedule` or `webhook` ("Event", "Agent bot", "Scheduled", "Webhook"; `trigger_mode` is the older alias). The event picker offers `message_created`, `message_sent`, `social_comment_received`, `conversation_created`, `conversation_updated` and `kanban_item_updated`; `conversation_opened` and `conversation_resolved` leave the picker but keep running and publishing. `agent_bot` takes only `message_created` and answers every message (`once_per_conversation` is not used there). `TriggerEditor` shows one line and one example per kind. The start only keeps inboxes, `once_per_conversation`, `ignore_groups`/`ignored_targets`, `include_comments`, `start_on_flow_changes` and `one_run_per_contact`: fine filters go in a Condition or By value after the start.
- Scheduled start (S12): `mode: schedule` with `schedule` `{ mode: interval, every, unit: minutes|hours|days|weeks, at, weekdays }` or `{ mode: cron, expression }` (5 fields), never more often than every 5 minutes, in the account's reports time zone (UTC without one). `FlowBuilder::ScheduleTickJob` (every minute, `config/schedule.yml`) starts the published flows whose slot is due, once per slot (a dedupe key per flow and slot) and never over a scheduled run of the same flow that is still going. A slot is not caught up: when no tick ran within 2 minutes (`slot_missed`), a run was still going (`run_in_progress`) or it could not be queued (`enqueue_failed`, which frees the slot for the next tick inside the window), it is recorded once as an error execution without steps with that `stopped_reason` and a `schedule_slot_skipped` (or `schedule_slot_enqueue_failed`) log line. The run has no conversation (`event_name` `schedule`), so the flow may only hold trigger, http_request, code, condition, switch, loop, account_action (responsible agent or rotation), kanban (with `card_id`, any mode but create), kanban_search, run_flow, end, random_split and go_to; `{{ schedule.* }}` (scheduled_at, timezone, date, time, day_of_week, weekday, year, month, day, hour, minute) gives the slot. Editor: a step select for minutes, a preview of the next runs in the account zone (`utils/cronPreview.js`), an invalid cron message, and a warning (also a validation error before publishing) for a cron that runs less than 5 minutes apart.
- Webhook start (#814): `mode: webhook`, no event and no inboxes. The flow gets a `webhook_token` (encrypted when Active Record encryption is configured), generated when the draft start is a webhook and the token is blank; `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/flow_builder/flows/:id/rotate_webhook_token` (administrator) replaces it and returns the flow. The flow JSON carries `webhook_url` (full URL with the token) or `null`. Public endpoints: `POST /webhooks/flow_builder/:flow_id/:token` and `POST /webhooks/flow_builder/:flow_id` with the `X-Webhook-Token` header. Wrong or missing token or unknown flow: `401`; flow not published or published start not a webhook: `404`; body over 64 KB: `413`, `503` (run not queued; the idempotency key is released); invalid JSON: `400`; otherwise `200 {"status":"accepted"}` and `FlowBuilder::EventTriggerJob` is queued with event `webhook`. JSON (also sent as `text/plain`) or `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` fields; JSON that is not an object is wrapped as `{ "data": ... }`. Rack::Attack throttles `/webhooks/flow_builder/` per flow id. The run context has `webhook = { body, query, headers, received_at }` (headers drop `authorization`, `cookie` and `x-webhook-token` and keep only `content-type`, `user-agent` and `x-*`), stored in the execution's `run_data.context`, so Executions shows the body each call received. Optional `data.idempotency_key` (text with `{{ webhook.* }}` expressions) is rendered against the request; a key already seen for the flow in the last 24 h (Redis `SET NX`, 24 h) answers `200 {"status":"duplicate"}` and runs nothing. Block rule: blocks reachable from a webhook or schedule start before an `outbound_message` block follow the scheduled-flow list; after it every block is allowed. Prefer the `X-Webhook-Token` header: a token in the path shows in server and proxy access logs. Headers that may carry credentials (token, key, secret, signature, auth) and forwarding addresses are not kept; values from the request never run as Liquid in a message.
- Outbound message block (#814): type `outbound_message`, `data` `{ inbox_id, phone_number, email, name, message_type: text|template|interactive, content, template_params: { name, category, language, namespace, processed_params }, interactive, result_name }` (phone, email, name and content accept expressions). It calls `OutboundMessages::CreateService` (sender nil, the inbox's agent bot in agent_bot mode; idempotency key `flow_builder:<execution_id>:<step>`), so it finds or creates the contact by phone with and without the 9th digit (resolving the WAHA `@lid`) and opens the conversation like the outbound messages API. Outputs `sent` and `error`; after `sent` the run's conversation is the message's conversation and every later block uses it (in a conversation flow too). Result vars: `contact_id`, `conversation_id`, `display_id`, `contact_created`, `conversation_created`.
- Card updated (S4, S13): event `kanban_item_updated`, once per kind of change: a card created, moved to another stage or funnel, or gone won, lost or open. `FlowBuilderListener` runs it in the card's main conversation, else its latest linked one; a card without a conversation starts nothing. `{{ kanban.* }}` (`ExecutionPayloadBuilder#kanban_snapshot`): `change` (`created`, `stage`, `status`; `called` in a `run_flow` run with a card), `id`, `title`, `funnel_id`, `stage`, `status`, `from_funnel_id`, `from_stage`, `from_status`. The start does not filter cards: the funnel, stage or status are compared in a Condition after it. Gated as the Kanban blocks. A change a flow made (a Kanban block included) starts only the flows with `start_on_flow_changes`. `KanbanItem#stamp_status_changed_at` keeps when the status changed in `item_details.status_changed_at`.
- Message sent by the team (S7): event `message_sent`: an outgoing, public, non-activity message whose sender is a `User`, not sent by a flow (`performed_by` Flow or `content_attributes.flow_builder`) and not a campaign message (`additional_attributes.campaign_id`). The context carries `message_id`, so `{{ message.* }}` is that message; the inbox filter and `once_per_conversation` apply as for the other events. It never resumes or holds a waiting session. The listener queues the event only while a published flow of the account starts on it (`FlowBuilder::EventTriggerService.listened?`), so agent replies cost no job otherwise.
- Changes made by other flows (S8): start option `start_on_flow_changes` ("Starts on changes made by other flows", default false). `FlowBuilderListener` no longer drops the events a flow caused: it passes `origin_flow_id` and `flow_depth` (the depth of the origin flow's latest execution in that conversation + 1), and `EventTriggerService#skipped_for_origin?` runs them only for flows with the option, never for the origin flow and only while `flow_depth` <= `MAX_FLOW_DEPTH` (3). A flow's messages wake no other flow. Events with no origin flow behave as before.
- One run at a time per contact (S9, S16): start option `one_run_per_contact` (default false). Each run segment takes `FlowBuilder::RunLock` (Redis, keyed by flow and contact, `TTL` 5 min, released when the segment ends or pauses with `Redis::Alfred.delete_if_equals`, check and delete in one WATCH/MULTI step; renewed after each block, at most every `RENEW_EVERY` (2 min), while the segment runs). A start that finds it taken is queued again for that flow every `RETRY_DELAY` (5 s) up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (12, about one minute); then it is not run and stays as an error execution without steps (`FlowBuilder::NotStartedRun`, `stopped_reason: run_lock_busy`) with a `start_dropped` log line; it does not count for `once_per_conversation`. A resume never goes on without the lock (S18): blocked that long, it comes back every `TTL` (5 min, by then the holder's lock has expired) while the session still waits, with a single `resume_waiting_for_lock` log line; a reply or a timer is late, never lost. Once the session no longer waits (another resume or an agent closed it), it stops trying. The first reply that has to wait is kept on the session (`context.pending_message_id`) and wins over later ones: whichever resume claims it first, the reply's retry or the wait's timer, delivers it, so a no-reply timer waking first never wins over a reply that came first, and a lost retry job does not lose it. Without the option nothing changes.
- Condition and By value (S14, phase C): the field is picked first in a searchable `PickerSelect` with icons, grouped (Contact, Conversation with its tasks, Inbox, Card, Last message, Customer reply, Comment, Schedule, Loop, Inputs, Results) and shown as "Group · Field"; custom attributes say whether they are the contact's or the conversation's. Enumerable fields pick their values from lists (conversation status and priority, the account's labels, team, assignee, card status and change, the funnel's real stages, list and checkbox attributes) and free text or a variable (`TemplateParamField`) is left for text fields; the operators follow the type (labels: contains / does not contain / empty; picked values: is / is not). Team and assignee are saved by id (`{{ conversation.team.id }}`, `{{ conversation.assignee.id }}`) and shown by name; an old row by name converts on open only when exactly one exact match exists, otherwise it keeps running by name. `FlowBuilder::ComparedValues` resolves the computed values only when a row names them: `{{ inbox.working_now }}`, `{{ inbox.online_agents }}`, `{{ teams.<id>.online_agents }}`, `{{ conversation.tasks.<status> }}`, `{{ conversation.tasks_by_type.<type>.<status> }}` and `{{ card.* }}` (the card of `card_id` or `card_funnel_id`; the stage is compared as `{{ card.funnel_stage }}` = `"<funnel id>:<stage>"`); `{{ message.attachments }}` and `.0.file_type` tell whether there is an attachment and its type. Summaries, canvas cards and By value outputs show names and "Funnel · Stage".
- Retired blocks (phases C and D): `business_hours`, `agents_available`, `kanban_stage` and `task_condition` become Condition rows; `task_update` becomes `task` with `operation`; `calendar_update`, `calendar_event` with `operation`; `team_chat_message`, `notify_agent` `mode: room`. They leave the library (`legacyNodeCatalog` keeps their names) and their executors do not change, so saved flows run and publish the same. `utils/retiredBlocks.js` converts only with the panel button ("Turn into a Condition", "Turn into a Task", "Turn into an Event", "Turn into Alert the team", "Turn into Kanban", "Turn into Send message", "Turn into a comment block"): it keeps the connections, drops the outputs with no counterpart and leaves a configuration that cannot be moved as it is (a team that no longer exists, for example). Renames: `ai_condition` → "Yes/No question to Captain" (Captain and AI group), `switch` → "By value", `go_to` → "Go back to a step of this flow" (searchable picker and the step highlighted on the canvas), `run_flow` → "Start another flow", `code` under Advanced. `config/flow_builder_block_reference.yml` marks each retired block as Legacy and says what replaces it.
- Kanban by card (S5, S14, phase D): the `kanban` block has the modes `create`, `move`, `status`, `delete` ("Card" group) and `assign`, `note`, `checklist_add`, `checklist_complete`, `labels` ("Work on the card" group); `start_timer`/`stop_timer` are legacy. Except for `create`, the card is the one `card_id` names (variables such as `{{ kanban.id }}` or `{{ loop.item.id }}`) or the conversation's, its main one or, with `card_funnel_id`, `card_stage`, `card_status` and `card_labels`, the one that passes those filters (`FlowBuilder::KanbanCardLookup`); `card_source` (`conversation`, `trigger`, `loop`, `custom`) only guides the editor ("Which card?"; `custom` asks for a variable). Without a card it leaves by `no_card` (unconnected it goes on by `right`, as blocks saved before). `labels` + `label_action` `add`/`remove`/`replace` (stored lowercase). `delete` deletes the card and, inside a loop, fails unless `confirm_loop_delete`. `create` makes one card per funnel: a card in another funnel or a won or lost one does not stop it. `result_name` keeps the card (the `kanban_search` keys). The Kanban actions of Actions take the same `card_id` / `card_funnel_id`.
- Find cards (S12): type `kanban_search`, `data: { funnel_id, funnel_stage, status, min_hours_in_stage, status_changed_within_hours, labels, limit (1-100, 20 by default), result_name }` (filters accept variables). `FlowBuilder::KanbanSearchNodeExecutor` finds the account's cards, the ones longest in their stage first, and keeps `{{ vars.<name>.count }}` and `.data` (`id`, `title`, `funnel_id`, `stage`, `status`, `stage_entered_at`, `status_changed_at`, `labels`, `conversation_id` (display id), `contact_id`) for a loop. Outputs `right` (found) and `none` (none; unconnected it goes on by `right`). It needs no conversation and only reads, so test runs search too. Gated as the Kanban blocks.
- Start another flow (S12, S16): type `run_flow`, `data: { flow_id, card_id, conversation_id, card_source, inputs: [{ key, value }] }`. `FlowBuilder::RunFlowNodeExecutor` queues another published flow of the account (`event_name` `flow_called`), which begins at the block after its start and takes no notice of its event, inboxes, `once_per_conversation` or ignored contacts (its `one_run_per_contact` still holds), in the conversation of the card `card_id` names, else the one `conversation_id` (display id) names, else the running one; it reads `{{ inputs.<key> }}` and, with a card, `{{ kanban.* }}` (`change` = `called`). The step output says `outcome`: `queued` (with `call_id`; the called run's context has `caller_execution_id` and `caller_call_id`), `skipped` (`no_conversation`, `card_not_found`), `refused` (`max_depth`, past 3 hops) or `would_call` in test runs; a call that cannot be queued fails the step. It leaves by `right`. In the executions panel the step says whether the call was requested, skipped or refused, with its call id.
- Task and Event (phases A and D): `task.data.operation` `create` (default, also when absent), `complete`, `cancel`, `reopen`, with `target` (`flow`: the task a `task` block of this run created, else the latest one this flow created on the conversation; `conversation`: every open one, not with `reopen`), `outcome_summary`, `link_card` (links the new task to the card `card_id`/`card_*` pick) and `result_name`; outputs `right` and `not_found`. `account_tasks.flow_builder_flow_id` (migration 20261102120000, FK `on_delete: :nullify`) remembers the flow and the tasks API exposes it. `calendar_event.data.operation` `create`, `reschedule`, `cancel`; `start` relative `{ amount, unit }` or `{ mode: at_specified_time, run_at }` (a fixed date read in the account's reports time zone, or a variable); the event keeps `metadata.flow_builder_flow_id`, and `reschedule`/`cancel` act on the latest one this run scheduled, else the latest still scheduled one of this flow on the conversation; outputs `right` and `not_found`.
- Alert the team, Transfer and End (phase D): `notify_agent` "Alert the team" with `mode: notification` (default) or `room` (`chat_room_id` of a channel, posted in the name of the `responsible`, who must be a member; `chat_rooms` gate; an archived or missing room fails the step). `handoff` adds `to` ("To whom?": `inbox` by default, `team` with `team_id`, `agent` with `agent_id`, who must be assignable in the inbox) and `end_flow` ("End the flow": `true` on new blocks ends the run there and the block has no outputs; false or absent goes on by `right`, as before). `captain` warns in the panel when an inbox of the start has no assistant. `end` says it does not resolve the conversation.
- Actions (phase D): for new rows the `action` block offers only the conversation's housekeeping (assign, labels, priority, status, notes, SLA, email, participants, `send_webhook_event`); saved Kanban, `send_message`, `send_attachment` and comment rows keep running as notice rows that point to the new block and, when they are the only action, convert (Kanban with `no_card` when there is no card; message with `window_closed` connected to the same next block, so it goes on outside the window as before). `error` output: once every action ran, when one failed (`status: error`; `skipped` and `unavailable` do not count), and the step records the first failure; unconnected it leaves by `right`, as blocks saved before. `normal_edges` ignores `error` edges on every block.
- Privacy and retention (S10): `http_request.data.redact_response` ("Don't keep the answer in the history"): later blocks read the answer during the run and, when it ends, its body is stored as `[redacted]` in `run_data` (vars, the block's step and the next step's input). A copy of the body in another variable (with Code, for example) is the flow's responsibility. Size: an answer over 16 KB (from an HTTP request of up to 1 MB) is stored short in `run_data.vars` while the run is not waiting (when it ends and in the writes in between): its first 50 keys, each value cut to 200 characters, with `body_truncated: true` and `body_bytes` (the full size) (`FlowBuilder::StoredResult`); later blocks read it whole during the run, and a waiting run keeps it whole since it resumes with it; with `redact_response` the redacted body keeps neither `body_truncated` nor `body_bytes`. `FLOW_BUILDER_EXECUTION_RETENTION_DAYS` (installation config, 0 = keep forever): `FlowBuilder::ExecutionRetentionJob` (daily at 03:45 UTC, `purgable` queue) deletes in batches of 1000 the finished (`success`/`error`) executions older than that; running and waiting ones, and the one a waiting session points at, stay.
- Observability (S16): no start is lost silently. A start dropped by the lock (`run_lock_busy`) and a scheduled slot that did not run (`slot_missed`, `run_in_progress`, `enqueue_failed`) stay as error executions without steps, with `error_message` and `run_data.stopped_reason`, and a log line (`FlowBuilder::Telemetry`). Slots missed together (a worker outage) are one run per reason for the latest slot, with `run_data.context.missed_slots` and `first_missed_slot`. `GET /flow_builder/flows/:flow_id/executions/:id` adds `stopped_reason` (also `window_closed`, or why a wait was closed, such as `expired`), `failed_steps` and `skipped_steps` (`node_id`, `node_type`, `label`, `status`, `error`, `reason`, `route`, `started_at`), `caller_execution_id` and `called_executions` (the runs its `run_flow` blocks started, read at request time: `id`, `flow_id`, `status`, `call_id`, `stopped_reason`, `error_message`). The panel shows a run without steps as "Did not start" with its `error_message`, a `run_flow` step as requested, skipped or refused with its call id, and skipped steps in amber. The MCP tool descriptions explain it; after the deploy, `db:mega_prepare` reindexes their embeddings.
- API, MCP and Mega Assistant: every `flow_builder` endpoint (flows CRUD, `validate`, `publish`, `pause`, `test`, executions, connections, `block_availability`, `http_requests/test`) is documented under the `Flow Builder` tag in `swagger/paths/application/flow_builder/` and reaches `swagger.json`, the public spec, the Application tag group and `postman_collection.json` (`rake swagger:build`; Super Admin → API Docs). The same endpoints are `flow_builder_*` entries of `AccountActions::Definitions::COMMON_TOOL_SPECS` with `feature: 'flow_builder'` and `admin_only: true`, so the account MCP server lists them and Mega Assistant can call them only when the flag is on and the user (or the MCP integration user) is an administrator; create/update/validate/test carry typed `input_schema`s (`definition` = `{ nodes: [{ id, type, data }], edges: [{ source, target }] }`, whole-graph replace). Their embeddings for semantic discovery are refreshed by `MegaAssistant::ToolEmbeddingIndexingJob`, which `db:mega_prepare` enqueues on every deploy; `rake mega_assistant:index_tool_embeddings` is only needed when a deploy skips it. `GET /flow_builder/block_reference` (tool `flow_builder_block_reference`, `FlowBuilder::BlockReference`, content in `config/flow_builder_block_reference.yml`) gives the graph rules and each block's summary and outputs; with `types=trigger,message` it adds the required and optional settings and an `example` of those blocks (a spec checks that it covers `FlowValidator::ALLOWED_NODE_TYPES` and that each example passes the validator; adding or changing a block means updating the YAML). The node `type` is an `enum` of those types. In Mega Assistant, `MegaAssistant::Guides` has the `flows` topic (also in `RequestRouter::GUIDE_TOPICS`), `Vocabulary` maps flujo/fluxo to `flow` and `ActionCatalog.read_only?` treats `flows#validate` as a read, since it saves nothing, so it asks for no confirmation; on MCP it keeps `readOnlyHint: false`, like every POST. `flow_builder_executions_get` adds `stopped_reason`, `failed_steps`, `skipped_steps`, `caller_execution_id` and `called_executions` (see "Observability"). `?detail=` answers less of a run: `summary` (the run and that diagnosis, without steps), `errors` (plus only the failed and skipped steps, with input and output), `steps` (plus every step) or `full` (the default and the previous answer, with `run_data` and `definition_snapshot`); a smaller answer carries `detail`, and an unknown value answers 422 (`FlowBuilder::Execution::DETAILS`; the tool offers it as `query.detail`). On a 10-step run it went from 128.8 KB (`full`) to 0.5 KB (`summary`). `contacts_conversations` returns each conversation's `kanban_items` (status and `label_list`) and `conversations_get` gives its card with status and `label_list`.

Bots:

- Agent Bots per inbox with smart handover; manual assignment selectors expose only active bots configured on every requested inbox.
- Human replies to Webhook bots: by default `AgentBotListener` skips `message_created`/`message_updated` for human responses (dashboard `User` sender) and WhatsApp `external_echo` messages. Setting `bot_config.send_human_replies` (switch in the bot form, Webhook bots only) delivers them with the usual payload; bots tell them apart by `message_type: outgoing`, `sender.type` and `content_attributes.external_echo`. Typebot never receives them.
- Webhook bots only get messages on their turn: `AgentBotListener#process_message_event` sends `message_created`/`message_updated` to a Webhook bot only while the conversation is `pending`, is assigned to that bot (`ai_assignee`), or the bot has `bot_config.send_human_replies`. After a handoff (`open`) the customer's messages no longer reach the bot; `conversation_updated` follows the same rule, so the team's edits (labels, assignee, attributes) after a handoff are not sent; the lifecycle events (`conversation_opened`, `conversation_resolved`, `conversation_status_changed`) are still sent in every status so the bot learns about the handoff. Typebot already applied the same `pending` rule in `AgentBots::TypebotService`.
- New conversations and senderless campaigns for an active Agent Bot stay pending with the bot as owner; explicit human assignments are preserved, and handoff, human open, or bot disconnection clears bot ownership. Dialogflow, Captain, and ignored targets do not receive an Agent Bot owner.
- The shared `Conversation.unassigned` scope requires both `assignee_id` and `assignee_agent_bot_id` to be null, so bot-owned conversations do not affect the human Unassigned queue's list or count.
- Webhook session expiry uses the canonical handoff: it opens the conversation, clears bot ownership, and enables human auto-assignment only during that transition. With no eligible agent, the conversation remains open and unassigned with the existing bounded retries.
- ReplyBox detects `AgentBot` ownership on pending conversations, forces the effective `NOTE` mode without overwriting reply drafts, and the takeover banner reopens and assigns the conversation to the current agent while updating the local assignee type.
- Extended Typebot with MEGA_CMD commands for agent/team assignment.
- Typebot ignores WhatsApp reactions to avoid artificial starts or messages.
- Per-channel webhook signatures for outbound authenticity validation.
- Account auto-resolve runs as its own Sidekiq cron entry (`trigger_conversations_resolution_job`, every 5 minutes, `AUTO_RESOLVE_CRON` overrides it; `config/schedule.yml` is rendered with ERB and invalid entries are logged). The minimum inactivity an account can set defaults to 10 minutes and follows `AUTO_RESOLVE_MIN_MINUTES`; a value saved under an older, lower minimum stays valid until it is changed.
- The `send_email_to_team` automation action only emails teams of the rule's account (`@account.teams`), even if the rule holds team IDs from another account.

### 3.5 Captain AI

- Captain display name (white-label): `CAPTAIN_DISPLAY_NAME` (Super Admin → Settings → Captain, Name section; `Major` by default, empty also uses `Major`) changes only what people see. Models, tables, `/captain` routes, i18n and config keys, and `captain_*` tool names stay the same. Dashboard: `dashboard/i18n/assistantName.js` replaces the word Captain/Capitán/Capitão in the translation messages when they load (before they compile, so customer values interpolated at render time, such as an assistant named "Captain Bot", are untouched); lowercase `captain` in search keywords is kept and the new name is added. MCP: `initialize` returns `instructions` with the name, and each tool's title and description use it (the `tools/list` cache key includes the name). Mega Assistant keeps its own name: its instructions say what people call Captain, and the guides and the Decision Router recognize the configured name (`Captain::DisplayName`). `ConfigLoader` creates `Major` on new and existing installations without overwriting a saved name. The name is for the accounts: Super Admin is internal and keeps saying Captain (it only adds the field). Server texts (`config/locales/*.yml`, API errors) also still say Captain.
- Captain replies and takeover: if the model writes a tool call as reply text (a JSON object such as `{"query":...}` or `to=functions.`), `Captain::Assistant::AgentRunnerService` retries once (unless a tool already ran or a newer message discarded the turn) and, if it happens again, raises `LeakedToolCallError`, which hands off to a human without charging a credit (`ResponseParts#leaked_tool_call?`; traces record `credit_used: false`). The "Take over" banner (`ReplyBoxBanner.vue`, `isAIAssigneeType`) shows on pending conversations owned by an AgentBot or Captain; it assigns the agent first (`POST /assignments`, which opens the conversation), and legacy auto-assignment no longer overrides an assignment made in the same save. Administrators stay assigned when a conversation opens even if they are not inbox members (they are assignable in every inbox, `Inbox#assignable_agents`). For Captain the reply box stays available: replying opens the conversation as before. Twilio: media, recordings and outbound calls use `Channel::TwilioSms#basic_auth_credentials` / `client` (API key + secret when configured).
- Assistant guards: Mega Assistant replies and confirmed actions run under a lease (`MegaAssistantLeasable`: `lease_token`, `lease_until`, `attempts`, migration `20261024180100`). `ResponseJob` claims the reply with a 10-minute lease, renews it on every step and writes and charges the reply only while it still holds the token; a worker that lost it stops at its next step (`MegaAssistant::LeaseLostError`). `MegaAssistant::RecoverRunsJob` (every 5 minutes, replaces `StaleRunsJob`) takes over expired leases under the thread lock: a reply is generated again once with the arguments it was started with (`run_arguments`, so the turn after a confirmation still only reports it) and then fails as `interrupted`; a confirmed single write past its 20-minute lease fails as `interrupted` and is never run again; runs from before the leases keep the 20-minute inactivity rule. A pending proposal identical to a new one is returned instead of duplicated, so a regenerated reply does not propose twice. The thread is never left busy forever (no new messages, no delete). The Copilot API (`copilot_threads`, `copilot_messages`) requires the `captain_integration` feature on the server (`Captain::Copilot::Guard`, 403 otherwise), and follow-up messages honor the Captain credit limit like new threads: without credits they get the limit message instead of a generated reply.
- Captain and Copilot (Wave 4): Copilot keeps a history of past chats ("Past chats / New chat", grouped by day, 20 per page with `meta`; loaded when the panel first opens), each charged reply passes its answered message as `reference:` to `Llm::UsageTracker`, and the prompt explains conversation status and priority. Nested `Llm::UsageTracker.track` contexts for the same account keep the outer reference. The old label-suggestion card and `LabelSuggestionService` are gone (migration `RemoveLabelSuggestionSettings` clears the leftover keys on accounts, OpenAI hooks and plans); the MCP/account action `captain_tasks_label_suggestion` keeps its path and `{ message }` shape and answers from `Captain::ConversationClassifierService`. The dashboard Playground posts `async: true` and receives the reply over Action Cable (`captain.playground.response`, `Captain::Playground::ResponseJob` + `ResponseBuilder`, matched by `request_id`, 120 s timeout); without `async` the endpoint stays synchronous for API/MCP callers. Custom tools accept static `headers` (up to 16; reserved, auth and `x-chatwoot-` names are dropped so they never override auth or metadata; editable in the form and through the MCP action). `Billing::AiCredits.consume!` emails administrators once per plan period when more than 80% of the AI Actions are used (`AdministratorNotifications::CaptainNotificationMailer#response_usage_alert`, marker in `internal_attributes`).
- Captain Playground test setup (Wave 4): the playground endpoint takes an optional `playground_config` (`scenario_ids`, disabled ones allowed; `temporary_scenarios` with `client_id`, `title`, `description`, `instruction`; `response_guidelines`; `guardrails`; `knowledge_text` up to 10,000 characters). `Captain::Playground::Configuration` validates it before the run or the job is queued (422 with `errors` by field, also for more scenarios than handoff slots) and never saves it; omitted keys use the saved assistant setup. `AgentRunnerService` takes it as `runtime_configuration:` and `Concerns::Agentable#agent` overrides the prompt context and, for temporary scenarios, the agent name (`scenario_temp_<sha256(client_id)[0,16]>_agent`); the pasted knowledge goes into the assistant and scenario prompts as untrusted reference material. `Captain::Playground::RunTrace` keeps the trace fields and adds `handler`, tool `arguments`/`result_preview` with credentials redacted (500 characters), `status` (`failed` for `ERROR:` results), handoff titles, `temporary_knowledge_attached` and `duration_ms`. In the playground, built-in, HTTP and MCP tools answer failures as `ERROR: <message>` (`Captain::Tools::FailureResult`); live conversations keep the plain message and `McpTool::TerminalOutcomeCommitted` still ends the run (the runner does not re-raise the run error). Playground replies are paid: the endpoint refuses with 422 (`captain.playground_limit`) when a cloud or native-plan account has no credits, and `Captain::Playground::ResponseBuilder` charges one AI Action (`captain_playground`) per reply inside `Llm::UsageTracker.track`; failed runs are not charged. `Captain::Document` accepts `markdown_content` (`Concerns::CaptainMarkdownDocumentable`: attached `.md` file, `MARKDOWN:` external link, available at once, not syncable); the document payload adds `markdown_document` and `syncable`, and `faq_stats` adds `faqs` (approved FAQs not generated from documents). The MCP action `captain_assistants_playground` publishes the optional schema. The dashboard panel (desktop aside from `xl`, `SidePanel` below) is `PlaygroundTestSetup` + `usePlaygroundSession`; only administrators get the tool picker and the save buttons.
- Webhook and OAuth hardening: Twilio voice callbacks (`call`, `status`, `conference_status`, `recording_status`) must carry a valid `X-Twilio-Signature` for the channel's `auth_token`, checked against the request URL or the same path on `FRONTEND_URL` (`Concerns::TwilioWebhookVerification`); otherwise they return 403 without touching calls. Twilio signs with the Account Auth Token, so `channel_twilio_sms.account_auth_token_configured` records when `auth_token` holds it: new voice inboxes set it, and turning voice on for an API-key SMS inbox (whose `auth_token` is the API key secret) requires the Account Auth Token in Voice settings (`Concerns::TwilioVoiceCredentials`). `rake twilio:verify_voice_account_tokens` confirms existing voice inboxes against Twilio and lists the ones that still need the token. Shopify: the OAuth `state` expires after 10 minutes, the callback rejects shops outside `*.myshopify.com`/`.io` before contacting them, verifies the query HMAC and respects `Shopify::FeatureGate`, and the connect endpoint rejects invalid shop domains. `Shopify::FeatureGate.globally_enabled?` reads `ENABLE_SHOPIFY_INTEGRATION` from ENV, then the stored config, without writing it.
- Conversation classifier: `captain_classifier` enables the authorized `/conversations/:conversation_id/suggestions/labels`, `/contact_labels`, and `/priority` endpoints. `Captain::ConversationClassifierService` sends up to 20 recent public messages through the existing AI provider route `conversation_classification`, which follows account override, plan default, installation default, then feature default. The model returns up to three labels not yet applied to the conversation or contact and a priority; each new request calls the model, and contact label changes are saved separately. When the recommended priority matches the current value, the sidebar shows it as already applied. Accepting labels clears the previous response; if a new response has no labels, it keeps only earlier pending suggestions for the same transcript. The classifier does not consume Captain response credits. Captain Decision Router: `conversation_classification` has the `decisions` capability and the classifier asks through `Captain::DecisionService#ask(state:, questions:)` with typed questions (one `noul` per candidate label, a `choice` for priority). When the route model has `decisions`, `Llm::SystemOneClient` calls the System One API (for example Jev) and records a `decision` `LlmUsageEvent`; otherwise a chat model answers through a JSON schema built from the questions. Both paths return the System One shape (`noul` 0..1, `choice` with `confidence`).
- Captain conditions in automations: rules on every event accept a `captain_condition` with `detects` / `does_not_detect` and one description of up to 500 characters, offered in the form only when `captain_classifier` is on. `AutomationRules::ConditionsFilterService` asks Captain only when its answer can change the result (it evaluates the rule with every Captain condition unmet and met first) and binds the answers as booleans in the AND/OR chain. `Captain::AutomationConditionService` answers all Captain conditions of a rule in one `Captain::DecisionService#ask` on the `conversation_classification` route (Jev or a decision/chat model per installation), with the latest message (first 8,000 characters), the transcript of `Captain::ConversationTranscript` and the custom attributes from `Captain::CustomerContext`; probability ≥ 0.5 means detected. Each evaluation that asks Captain charges one AI Action (`automation_condition`, reference = conversation) and is tracked in `Llm::UsageTracker`. Feature off, no credits, a private note, no readable messages or a decision error leave the Captain conditions unmet without failing the rule. Delayed rules check their conditions again before running, so they ask again. Saving a new or changed Captain condition requires `captain_classifier`; rules saved before the feature was revoked can still be edited, turned off or cleaned up. `captain_condition` is a reserved custom attribute key.
- Conversation monitors (`conversation_monitors` account feature, appended to `config/features.yml`; needs `captain_integration` too): natural-language conditions checked on every conversation (up to 20 active per account, `CONVERSATION_MONITORS_LIMIT`), under `enterprise/` (`ConversationMonitors::*`). Tables: `conversation_monitors`, `conversation_monitor_evaluations` (one per monitor and conversation, with the model that answered), `conversation_monitor_scans` (initial 7-day history, catch-up of a paused period, skipped `from_now` gaps) and `conversation_monitor_work_items` (one durable row per conversation: revision, full-history and live-activity revisions, lease, attempts). Public customer/agent messages, edits, redactions, deletions and finished audio transcripts request work (`MessageTracking`, `AttachmentTracking`, `ConversationTracking`; imported messages through `Enterprise::DataImports::Importer`); accounts without the flag skip the hooks after a bitmask check, so messages sent while it is off are not caught up later (edits still invalidate). `ConversationMonitors::Evaluator` builds the state (`ContextBuilder`: the last 5 public messages live, the whole conversation for history, edits and transcripts, trimmed to 28 KB, plus `Captain::CustomerContext`) and asks `ConversationMonitors::Judge`, which calls `Captain::DecisionService#ask` on the `conversation_monitors` route (`decisions` capability, default `gpt-6-luna`; Jev through System One or a chat model through a JSON schema) with one `noul` question per monitor, up to 20 per request; requests over 60 KB are halved before asking. Each request is one AI Action charged inside `Llm::UsageTracker.track(feature: 'conversation_monitors', reference: conversation)`; governed accounts without AI Actions get `credits_exhausted`, which holds the work for about an hour without burning attempts and keeps the whole conversation pending. Threshold 0.6 (tuned for decision models). Failures retry with backoff up to 5 attempts; a missing route is `not_configured`. Scheduling: `Internal::DispatchConversationMonitorsJob` runs every minute (sidekiq-cron, `scheduled_jobs`) and enqueues `ConversationMonitors::DispatchJob`, which is one existence query without monitors, takes a Redis lock (55 s) and enqueues at most 25 conversations per account and 1000 per run, pending scans, rechecks and automation deliveries, and purges deleted monitors. Live evaluation runs on `low`, scans and rechecks on `deferred` (no new Sidekiq queues). API `/api/v1/accounts/:id/monitors` (index/show/create/update/destroy, `preview` + `preview/:token`, `timeseries`, `conversations`, `retry_evaluations`, `resume`): report viewers read, administrators manage, drill down and preview (5 recent conversations, one AI Action each, 30 s cooldown, only ids cached); lists and charts return `configured` and `credits_available`; `monitor.updated` Action Cable events refresh open reports. Automations: event `monitor_matched` with `automation_rules.monitor_id` and `monitor_event_activated_at`; the first match found on live activity records one `conversation_monitor_automation_deliveries` row that `ProcessAutomationDeliveryJob` re-checks and runs once; pausing or deleting the monitor turns its rules off. Live activity is only tracked while such a rule is active and never for automation messages; then the evaluator first judges the evidence up to that activity without automation replies and records a match found only with them as history (one more request). UI: Settings › Captain › Monitors (list, templates, side panel with preview, report page with chart and drilldown), the automation form's monitor picker; en/es/pt_BR. Upstream's Langfuse tracing, OpenRouter/Jev client, daily/monthly call quotas and Chatwoot Cloud plan gating were replaced by the route, `Llm::UsageTracker` and AI Actions.
- Monitor Slack alerts: migration `20261113120000_add_slack_channel_id_to_conversation_monitors` adds `slack_channel_id` (`[A-Z0-9]`, up to 30 characters, blank stored as null, valid only while the account has the Slack hook). `PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/monitors/:id` takes `slack_channel_id`; the app joins the channel through `Integrations::Slack::ChannelBuilder#join` and answers 422 `invalid_slack_channel` when it cannot. `ConversationMonitors::ResultWriter` enqueues `ConversationMonitors::SlackAlertJob` (`SlackAlertService`) on a monitor's first live match of a conversation, written in the account's language. Disconnecting Slack clears every monitor channel (`Enterprise::Concerns::IntegrationsHook`), and `list_all_channels` adds `is_private`. Live activity tracking also opens for monitors with a Slack channel, which can use extra AI Actions. UI: `MonitorSlackAlerts` in Settings › Captain › Monitors.
- Decision routing: the internal features `captain_routing` and `mega_assistant_routing` (capability `decisions`, no default model) stay off until Super Admin picks a model; the installation global model does not turn them on (`Captain::DecisionService.configured?`). They are set in the "Captain Decision Router" section of the account Captain tab, with a per-account mode (`settings.captain_routing_mode`, `AccountCaptainRouting`): `off` (default) routes by keywords without asking a model, `shadow` asks the Captain scenario decision and only records it, and `active` applies the Captain (scenario and tools) and Mega Assistant decisions. `Captain::Runtime::RoutingService` asks a `choice` between the scenarios and `none` (minimum confidence 0.5) with the last 8 messages and the scenario that answered last, and stores `router: decision|keywords` in `Captain::RuntimeDecision`. In `active` mode, the turn starts directly in the selected scenario (`Captain::Assistant::ScenarioAgentRunner`) instead of staying with the agent that spoke last; the scenario can still hand the conversation back to the orchestrator; `Captain::Runtime::ToolCatalogService` ranks custom tools by `noul` once there are more than 12. `MegaAssistant::RequestRouter` makes one decision per reply with a `noul` per guide (threshold 0.5) and a simple/complex `choice` when the account has a complex-tasks model; it does not run on the automatic turn after a confirmation. On error or without a model the keyword rules apply.

- Super Admin configures OpenAI, Anthropic, and Gemini credentials and Captain's global default provider/model. Account administrators may inherit that default or choose an available compatible model for each feature; credentials are never exposed to account APIs. Captain V1, Copilot, and V2 agents inherit the effective provider/model and compatible per-model request options; `reasoning_effort` is forwarded only to OpenAI-compatible routes. Audio, embeddings, PDFs, and legacy file flows remain OpenAI-only.
- Assistants: inbox-level configuration with custom instructions and context. `config.auto_resolve_mode` is persisted per assistant as `evaluated`, `legacy`, or `disabled`; existing assistants without it fall back to the account mode. With Captain V2, `config.auto_resolve_after` accepts 5 to 1,440 minutes and is normalized in five-minute increments, while `send_inactivity_resolution_message` controls the public message. An optional validated audience condition tree and `response_window` (`always`, `business_hours`, or `outside_business_hours`) decide eligibility when conversations are created or reopened; conversations already pending continue uninterrupted. The API safely merges config updates and serializes effective values.
- Assistant avatar: `Captain::Assistant` has an `avatar` attachment set with `PATCH /captain/assistants/:id` (`assistant[avatar]`) and removed with `DELETE /captain/assistants/:id/avatar` (administrators; replies go back to the default logo); the assistant JSON adds `avatar_url`. `AssistantBasicSettingsForm` uploads and removes it, and the widget groups consecutive messages by sender type and id so each assistant shows its own avatar.
- Assistant settings and scenario routes (seven in `captain.routes.js`) require `administrator` in the dashboard, as the API already did. `Captain::ResponseSchema` describes `reasoning` as "Brief basis for the answer" (it asked for the thought process), so the stored reasoning is shorter.
- Captain runs a single runtime (V2) for every account with `captain_integration`; the V1 runtime, its false-promise detector, the V1 action classifier, and the `captain_false_promise_harness_enabled` setting were retired. `captain_integration_v2` and `captain_v1_action_classifier` stay in `config/features.yml` as `deprecated` entries so stored bits keep their meaning, and billing no longer turns them on. `Captain::Llm::SystemPromptsService` keeps only the FAQ, notes, attributes, and Copilot prompts. The response scheduler's burst coalescing applies to every account; it waits `config.runtime_burst_coalesce_ms` after the last customer message (0.8 s by default, clamped to 0.5–10 s), editable under the assistant's Schedule settings as "Wait before replying". Tools with external effects (handoff, notes, labels, priority, resolve, non-GET HTTP, and MCP) are skipped once a newer customer message arrives. `docs/captain_v1_sunset.md` describes the query that finds accounts still on V1 and the instruction migration before upgrading.
- Cloud billing records a scheduled Stripe subscription cancellation and exposes its date to Billing. Cancellations end at the current billing-period boundary, including Stripe flexible billing payloads that use `cancel_at`; an account deletion queues the same cancellation safely for retry.
- One bot provider per inbox: `InboxBotStatus#conflicting_bot_provider(provider)` returns the provider (`:agent_bot`, `:dialogflow`, `:flow_builder` and, in Enterprise, `:captain` through `Enterprise::Inbox`) already answering the inbox, and `bot_provider_conflict_error` builds the `errors.inboxes.bot_provider_conflict` error with the names from `InboxBotStatus.provider_name` (Captain uses `Captain::DisplayName.current`). The link models check it only on connect or re-enable, so an inbox that already had two keeps saving: `AgentBotInbox` (new or `status` change), a `dialogflow` `Integrations::Hook` (enabled, new or `status` change), `FlowBuilder::FlowInbox` (new or re-enabled) and `CaptainInbox` (`on: :create`). It answers 422 on `inboxes#set_agent_bot`, `integrations/hooks#create`/`#update` and `captain/assistants/:id/inboxes#create`. Exception: an AgentBot-mode flow and Captain coexist. A new conversation on a flow inbox stays pending without Captain as `ai_assignee` (`Enterprise::Conversation#another_bot_answers_first?`, which also covers `external_bot_active?`); the Captain hook skips messages while the flow owns the conversation (`flow_owns_conversation?`: flow active and `ai_assignee` other than the inbox assistant), and the "Reply with Captain" block (`FlowBuilder::CaptainNodeExecutor`) assigns Captain and asks it to answer the message that reached the block. On inboxes that already had a flow and Captain, Captain answers only after the handover. Captain does not schedule replies or automatic resolution for inboxes with an Agent Bot or Dialogflow.
- Conversation assignment: the `captain_integration` account feature exposes only the Captain configured for every requested inbox to the typed assignment selector. Assigning Captain makes the conversation pending; clearing Captain or replacing it with a human opens it. Accounts without the feature cannot select or assign Captain, while the legacy `include_agent_bots` request remains limited to Agent Bots. `Enterprise::Inbox#captain_active?` also requires the `captain_integration` feature, so without Captain access it is neither assigned nor answering. Opening a conversation (reopen, bulk action, macro, snooze expiry) clears `ai_assignee` in `Conversation#clear_invalid_ai_assignee` before auto-assignment; a resolved conversation of an active bot that a message reopens as pending keeps it. `assign_agent` in macros and automations (`ActionService`) goes through `Conversations::AssignmentService`, so taking over a Captain conversation opens it like a direct assignment.
- Assistant overview: the refreshed overview (`OverviewV2`) is the only Captain Overview; the legacy page and its `captain_overview_v2` local flag are gone. The legacy stats/drilldown endpoints (`Captain::AssistantStatsBuilder`, `Captain::AssistantDrilldownBuilder`, MCP `captain_assistants_drilldown`) stay for API/MCP callers. Ranges: last 7/30/90 days, this/last week (weeks start on Sunday) and this/last month (`Captain::AssistantStatsWindow`, also in the MCP stats schema). `Captain::OutcomeTrackingHistory` caches per account (Redis, 30 days) when outcome tracking began; the overview returns it as `tracking_started_at`, ranges that start earlier are offered as insufficient history (all ranges stay available when the account has no outcomes), and untracked periods return nil metrics instead of zero (also durable rate with nothing assessable, CSAT without ratings and trend previous values). Trend comparisons shift by whole weeks and include `previous_starts_on`/`previous_ends_on`.
