Admins open drilldowns from the metrics, handoff reasons and the resolution-flow sankey: `GET /captain/assistants/:assistant_id/stats/drilldown` (`Captain::AssistantOverviewDrilldownBuilder`, 9 outcome metrics, handoff reason filter, pagination; 422 for unknown metric/reason or timezone). The runtime quality card counts sessions inside the selected window. A server-generated `overview_summary` derives up to three localized insights of 180–220 characters, skips inactive reports, and shares a one-hour Redis cache by account, assistant version, range, timezone, locale and tracking start. Estimated time saved uses a fixed 2-minute agent effort per public assistant reply.
- Captain model routing separates customer and internal features in Super Admin. Text features resolve account override, then the configured global Captain model, then the YAML default; unavailable providers cannot be selected. The internal `conversation_completion` evaluator retains its compatible OpenAI installation route, and customer preferences continue to hide internal features.
- Copilot reply editor: measures rendered suggestions after the DOM update and temporarily grows to fit them, capped at 350px. Manual resizing remains authoritative and is restored when the suggestion closes; loading and suggestion states cross-fade in place.
- Copilot panel: the chat follows the latest message only when the agent was near the end (it does not move if they scrolled up to read) and an opened chat starts at its latest message; the input takes focus back on send, chat switch and header buttons so pasting works while a message is sending, except on phones (`canAutoFocus`). `Captain::Copilot::ReplySuggestionJob` is enqueued after the transaction commits (`enqueue_after_transaction_commit`), so it finds the thread just created.
- Copilot stop: `POST /captain/copilot_threads/:id/stop` (thread owner only, 404 for others) runs `CopilotThread#stop!` under the thread row lock, marks the pending user message `status: stopped` and returns it (`push_event_data`), or 204 when nothing is running; a late reply and its credit charge are dropped (`CopilotThread#reply_to`). The send button turns into **Stop** and sending is disabled while Copilot works; other tabs do not see the stop (there is no message-updated event), and `copilot_messages#create` keeps accepting messages while one is running (API and MCP contract unchanged). The expand button (md and up; off on Kanban and chat rooms) widens the panel, and on the Captain overview the chart column shrinks so the panel fits. The usage-limit notice is created with `status: failed` so API clients can tell it from an answer.
- Copilot reply suggestions: an explicit request validates agent access to the conversation and an incoming latest public message. A dedicated job uses the Assistant's prompt, FAQs, citations, and GET custom tools only, without scenarios or handoff; it rechecks access and freshness before persistence, discards stale drafts, and consumes a credit only for a valid saved reply.
- Copilot workflows (premium flag `copilot_workflows`, appended last in `config/features.yml`, which works only with `captain_integration` also on; off ⇒ `Captain::Copilot::ChatService` answers as before): `Captain::Copilot::TurnService` (on `Llm::BaseAiService`, model route `copilot_workflows`, default `gpt-5.1`; a Super Admin global default model or an account override takes precedence, there is no Cloud-only default) answers with its own prompt (`enterprise/lib/captain/prompts/copilot_workflows.liquid`, `Captain::Copilot::TurnPrompt`) that names the AI with `Captain::DisplayName` and replies in the agent's language. Every step is saved, so a retried or resumed turn continues where it stopped: `copilot_runs` (STI `CopilotRun::Turn`, `Collection`, `Review`, `Action`; lease token, status, transcript), `copilot_tool_calls` (one row per tool call with its result), `copilot_review_findings` (one per reviewed conversation) and `copilot_action_items` (one per record of a proposed change), migrations `20261121150000` and `20261121160000`. Jobs: `Captain::Copilot::TurnJob`, `BackgroundRunJob`, `ReviewJob` and `ReviewConversationJob` (`:low`, up to `CopilotRun::CONVERSATIONS_PER_TURN` = 300 conversations per turn), `ActionJob`, `ApprovalExpiryJob` (5 minutes) and `RecoverRunsJob` (from `TriggerScheduledItemsJob`). Tools: `get_report` (V2 report builders, `ReportPolicy#view?`, `since`/`until` or `last_days` counted as the reports pages do, summaries ranked by the first metric with `order`), `get_account_directory`, `get_data` (resources and tables in `enterprise/config/copilot_tables.yml`; secret columns, and JSON keys named like secrets (`Resources::Columns::SECRET_KEY`), are left out of what Copilot reads), `review_conversations`, `continue_review`, `display` and `act`. Screening goes through `Captain::DecisionService` on route `copilot_screening`, which has no default, so it stays off until Super Admin routes a model. Answers keep only numbers and links a tool returned (`NumberChecking`, `LinkCheck`). `act` proposes one of 17 actions (`Captain::Copilot::Actions`) as an `assistant_approval` message; `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/captain/copilot_runs/:id/approve` and `/reject` (thread owner, `Captain::Copilot::Guard`, 422 with the flag off) decide it, and `ActionJob` re-checks each record with the approving user's permissions (`PermissionFilterService` + `ConversationPolicy#show?`, or `contact_manage` + `ContactPolicy#update?`). `CopilotThread#stop!` rejects pending approvals. Approval and progress cards (`assistant_progress`) update over Action Cable with `copilot.message.updated` (`COPILOT_MESSAGE_UPDATED`). Credits: one `increment_response_usage(feature: 'copilot_workflows', reference:)` per answered turn.
- Copilot generative UI (with `copilot_workflows`): the workflow tool `present_blocks` (`Captain::Tools::Copilot::PresentBlocksService`) validates blocks with `MegaAssistant::BlockSchema` restricted by its new `types:` option to text, markdown, table, metric, card, chart, timeline, contact, conversation and actions (no page, form, kanban or HTML report); contacts and conversations load as the agent and need Pundit `show?`, links only reach `/app/accounts/:id/`. Blocks are add-only with `schema_version: 0`, so an action button sends its prompt as a plain Copilot message; at most 3 calls, 12 blocks and 250 KB per turn. Like chart data, `blocks_data` is saved on the tool call and hidden from the model (`Captain::Copilot::Blocks`), and `finish` attaches the run's blocks to the answer as `message['blocks']`; `CopilotAssistantMessage` draws them with `AssistantBlocks` (compact) and disables the buttons while a turn runs. No step line is written for the tool.
- Conversation FAQ suggestions: a low-priority mutex job extracts only public customer and human-agent messages plus business context, rejects unsuitable conversations, and groups semantically matching observations by assistant and language; the Enterprise review API lists and previews only sources available to the current agent, supports edit/approve/dismiss while open, and locks reviews against concurrent approval. Approval creates an approved FAQ and retains its source observations; approved FAQs and dismissed suggestions suppress new duplicates.
- Generation details: Enterprise `GET /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/captain/agent_sessions/:message_id` authorizes the message conversation, hydrates citations and scenario titles, and supplies the Captain message popover. Sessions are cached per message; a handoff session is attached to its non-empty private reason note. Model and credit data render only for super administrators or development.
- Trusted Captain V2 citations: `faq_lookup` returns typed run-local indexes without URLs; the main agent remains free of `response_schema` and emits `[[citation:N]]` markers that the server converts to persisted parts, filters against the run mapping, and renders only public HTTP(S) documents owned by the assistant. Credentials, private destinations, PDFs, and signed parameters are rejected; `faq_ids` retains retrieved results while `used_faq_ids` and `cited_document_ids` contain only valid selections. History restores clean text, and legacy sessions with null new columns still hydrate from `faq_ids`.
- Captain V2 runtime: bounded recent context is measured before each run; omitted history receives an LLM semantic summary that treats transcript content as data and falls back deterministically when unavailable. Messages after the summary grow until they exceed the budget or 16 messages, and then the older half is folded in at once (`captain_conversation_summaries.summarized_through_message_id` marks the last folded message), so long conversations call the summarizer every few turns instead of before every reply. Its latency, token usage, and fallback state are persisted as safe session metrics and shown in generation details; typed active memory and task records are account/assistant scoped. Knowledge retrieval rejects candidates above its configured cosine-distance threshold and caches by response version. Root custom-tool selection is capped, write effects use account-scoped idempotency ledgers, and sessions persist prompt version and terminal status. Routing records shadow-only scenario/profile decisions, grounded fast paths reduce the turn cap, and configured golden evaluations (`runtime_golden_evals` with `name`, `query`, and expected `scenario_id`, `tool_ids`, and `grounded`) run daily without side effects. The Overview quality panel shows routing rollout, tool failures, golden pass rate, and semantic-summary latency/fallbacks for its selected period.
- Captain Documents: upload, indexing, plan-based auto-sync with jitter, a purgable queue, configurable per-account and global limits, and a details view for crawled content, source metadata, generated FAQ counts, and administrator-only conversation usage.
- Knowledge usage analytics: `Captain::ConversationUsageBuilder` calculates distinct live conversations from delivered assistant sessions (`credits_consumed > 0`) using GIN indexes on `document_ids` and `used_faq_ids`; it paginates drilldowns at 25 records, excludes handoffs and deleted conversations, supports document sorting by usage, and exposes user-created FAQ usage only to administrators.
- Captain Scenarios: activation rules and priority ordering; management APIs return enabled and disabled records, while only enabled scenarios execute. The API preserves explicitly submitted `tools`, normalizes scalar IDs or `{ id }` metadata, accepts saved references to disabled custom tools, and keeps `tool://` references when the field is omitted. Account MCP publishes dedicated create and update schemas.
- Captain Custom Tools: HTTP integrations with GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, and DELETE; they accept JSON Schema fragments for complex parameters, and Account MCP publishes direct contracts to list, create, inspect, update, delete, and test custom tools. The detail response reports the number of enabled scenarios using a tool, so the dashboard can warn before deactivation; disabled tools are excluded from execution.
- Captain custom tool email verification: `captain_custom_tools.settings` (jsonb, `{}` by default, migration `AddSettingsToCaptainCustomTools`) holds `requires_email_verification`; a parameter with `source: "verified.email"` (string only) also requires it. Nothing derives the setting from `risk_level` on the server; the form only turns it on by default for new tools with a write method (risk `write`), until the admin uses the switch. The API returns `settings.requires_email_verification` (stored value), rejects unknown or non-boolean settings and non-text sources with 422 instead of dropping them, and merges `settings` on update; the MCP account actions accept both optional fields. In a customer conversation (any source other than `playground` and `copilot_reply_suggestion`, including runs without a source) `Captain::Tools::HttpTool` refuses a gated tool with a plain-text instruction and makes no request until `Captain::EmailVerification` reports a verified email for that conversation, read from Redis on every call, never from the run state; verified parameters are hidden from the model and filled last, and every request carries `X-Chatwoot-Verified-Email` when there is one (`X-Chatwoot-Contact-Email` stays the unverified contact email). Agents get `captain--tools--send_email_verification_code` and `captain--tools--verify_email_code` plus a "Customer Identity" prompt section (assistant and scenarios) only when an enabled tool needs verification. The 6-digit code is emailed by `Captain::EmailVerificationMailer` with the inbox name as sender and the account locale, encrypted in the job arguments and never in the subject, tool results or application logs (ActionMailer logs whole mail bodies at `LOG_LEVEL=debug`, the development default, so keep production at `info`); it is stored only as an HMAC digest, compared in constant time and consumed atomically, expires in 30 minutes and allows 5 attempts. Sends are limited to 3 per hour per conversation, 5 per hour per contact and 5 per hour per address (`Redis::RateLimit`); a verification lasts 1 hour for the conversation (3 hours on email inboxes). In the playground and reply suggestions the model fills verified parameters and the result notes when the value is not the verified email. `high` tools still require human confirmation in `Captain::Runtime::ToolExecutionService`; verification does not replace it.
- Captain tool runtime: preserves each MCP server's complete `inputSchema`, forwards objects and arrays without converting them to text, excludes disabled or disconnected servers, refreshes stale connected catalogs every 10 minutes, and caps every provider request at 128 tools including handoffs. A transient connection failure retains the last usable catalog and remains eligible for retry; permanent errors do not advertise a stale catalog as connected. Neither V1 nor V2 Playground supports direct selection through `@` or `tool://`. V1 tests the base legacy assistant without direct MCP tools; V2 keeps the main assistant's normal tools, performs handoffs, and loads only each scenario agent's assigned tools, matching live conversations. Scenario configuration continues to support `tool://` references. The proxy accepts JSON objects and strings containing JSON objects, but rejects invalid JSON or non-object values instead of silently emptying them.
- Native account MCP servers: dedicated endpoints per slug at /mcp/:account_id/:slug. Captain executions to a native MCP carry a signed one-time proof bound to the account, assistant, server, endpoint, tool, and arguments; the handshake remains neutral and external MCP calls retain their normal identity.
- Account MCP for agents (issue #2353, all additive; `docs/mcp/`): `Mcp::ToolMetadata` adds `_meta` `mega/module`, `mega/intent`, `mega/operation_type`, `mega/side_effects`, `mega/idempotency`, `mega/sensitivity`, `mega/required_feature`, `mega/required_any_feature` and `mega/required_role` (from the same gates as `tools/list`) and `annotations.openWorldHint` to every tool; `Mcp::ToolInventory` writes `docs/mcp/tools-inventory.md` (`rake mcp:inventory`, a spec fails when stale). `account_context` adds `capabilities` (`Mcp::Capabilities`: role, tools by module, tools left out by `feature_disabled`, `administrator_required`, `not_in_allowed_tools` or `route_unavailable`). `Mcp::ErrorDetails` adds `error` to a failed tool result and `data` to a JSON-RPC error (`code`, `retryable`, `retry_after`, `request_id`); every call gets one request id, sent as `X-Request-Id`, and the proxy keeps `retry-after`. `Mcp::IdempotencyGuard` deduplicates by `idempotency_key` (Redis, 24 h, keyed by account, server and tool; the identity includes multipart files by name, size and SHA-256; `account_action_call` and allowed dynamic actions count as the tool of their route; each call owns its claim with a token and a 5-minute lease deadline, and only the owner stores, frees or marks it, through WATCH/MULTI) the send and schedule tools whose API does not (replay with `_meta` `mega/idempotent_replay`; other arguments refused; `in_progress` while running; `outcome_unknown` after a read timeout). It is opt-in: a call without `idempotency_key` runs every time, so its retry sends again. Descriptions of similar tools name their alternatives (`docs/mcp/selection-guide.md`). `flow_builder_flows_list` takes `detail=summary` (no `definition`, adds `nodes_count`). The proxy path check now stops at a segment boundary. Cache namespace `mcp/rpc/tool_definitions/v8`. Compatibility: `spec/fixtures/mcp/tool_contract_baseline.yml` pins the 456 tools from before; agent cases: `spec/fixtures/mcp/agent_tool_cases.yml`; measurements: `docs/mcp/benchmark.md` (`script/mcp_benchmark.rb`).
- MCP client diagnostics run at INFO level so authorization headers and execution proofs are never emitted by the dependency's DEBUG transport logger.
- Native self-connections may use `MCP_INTERNAL_BASE_URL`; development defaults to `MCP_INTERNAL_PORT`/3000 and never inherits a worker's per-process `PORT`. Only the origin is replaced, so the account endpoint path and signed request binding remain unchanged while avoiding public-DNS hairpin timeouts.
- A native `conversation_message_send` closes the Captain turn only from a structured successful result that confirms the active conversation, outgoing public message, and Captain sender. Its lifecycle marker, usage charge, and completion event are idempotent; stale turns are discarded before the MCP side effect.
- The MCP POST endpoint keeps JSON-RPC over `application/json` and supports a `multipart/form-data` extension: `payload` contains the complete JSON-RPC request and `attachments[]` carries local files.
- Multipart uploads are restricted to `conversation_message_send`, its legacy alias, and `outbound_messages_create`; they enforce `MAXIMUM_FILE_UPLOAD_SIZE`, with 15 combined attachments for conversations and exactly one for outbound.
- Multipart is a Mega HTTP extension that requires explicit client support; standard JSON-only MCP clients do not use it automatically.
- The conversation direct-upload JSON handshake accepts the effective MCP user's API token, validates account and conversation access through the API/Pundit stack, and returns Active Storage's signed upload target without browser CSRF.
- `outbound_messages_create` exposes the complete universal contract through MCP: `body` retains the inbox, one recipient identity, text/media/template, and a signed blob; `idempotency_key` is forwarded only as `Idempotency-Key`. Media accepts exactly one source among signed ID, multipart, `file` with a temporary HTTPS URL, and `file_base64`; for template messages, that source is assigned to `template.parameters.header.media_file` instead of a regular attachment.
- The `_meta["openai/fileParams"]` descriptor lets ChatGPT deliver `file.download_url` and `file.file_id`; Claude and other clients can use the same descriptor, multipart, or the JSON base64 fallback. URLs pass through `SafeFetch` with SSRF protection and size limits, and temporary blobs are purged if the API fails.
- The outbound service verifies that every media signed ID resolves to a persisted blob with positive size and an existing storage object. An invalid, expired, or byte-less reference returns `422 invalid_attachment` before creating a message or invoking the provider.
- MCP OAuth: .well-known metadata, register, authorize, token, refresh token, and PKCE.
- Dual authentication: Bearer OAuth or static Api-Access-Token.
- Curated MCP catalog for daily use: stable domain tool names (conversations, contacts, inboxes, help center, reports, kanban, and more).
- Published MCP tools: base tools (account_context, account_actions_list, account_action_call) + curated catalog; includes message scheduling, tasks, templates, campaigns, SLA, policies, calendar, reports, Captain, notifications, internal chat, and the complete Help Center lifecycle; it does not publish data import or export tools; explicit dynamic tools via allowed_tools.
- Auto-resolve mode: evaluated, legacy, or disabled per assistant. Evaluated mode sends conversation status and labeled non-private message content to the evaluator; both resolving and handoff paths lock and reload the conversation before transitioning, create messages in that transaction, and emit events only after success. Pending handoffs and follow-ups are kept open. Language of the fixed messages: without a custom message, the default handoff and resolution texts go out in the customer's language (`Captain::CustomerLocale`), reported by the evaluator (`language` in its answer) or by the agent when it calls `handoff` (`customer_language`); without it the account language is used. The inactivity handoff also sends the default text when no message is configured, like the `handoff` tool. Inactivity review outcomes: the evaluator answers `outcome` (`complete` / `waiting_on_customer` / `needs_human`, enum in `enterprise/lib/captain/conversation_completion_schema.rb`, replacing the `complete` boolean) with `reason` and `language`. `Captain::InboxPendingConversationsResolutionJob` resolves `complete` and `waiting_on_customer` (closing message in the customer's language) and hands off only `needs_human`. A waiting close saves `additional_attributes['captain_waiting_resolved_at']` with the status change and the private note "Auto-resolved, waiting on customer: …"; `Enterprise::CsatSurveyService#should_send_csat_survey?` skips the survey while no incoming message arrived after that close (this also applies to the Flow Builder "Ask for CSAT" node). When the customer writes again the conversation goes back to pending and a later resolution sends CSAT normally.
- Auto translate (`auto_translate` account flag, last bit of `feature_flags_ext_1`, off by default; account settings `auto_translate` and `translation_provider` `captain`/`google_translate`): `Account#translation_service` is the chosen provider, else `google_translate` when the account has a Google Translate hook, else `captain`; `auto_translate_enabled?` also needs the service available (`captain_tasks` or the hook). Contact side: the dashboard posts the untranslated contact/bot text messages to `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/conversations/:cid/messages/auto_translate` (`message_ids`, 403 when off for the account or the conversation, 422 for ids outside the conversation), which enqueues `Captain::Conversation::ContactMessageTranslationJob` per message into the agent's `ui_settings.locale` (or the account locale); `ContactMessageTranslationService` detects the language (`MessageLanguageService` stores `source_language` and moves `conversation_language` to the language of the newest message, among the contact's last 5 with a detected language, that has at least 3 words (in scripts without spaces each character counts; a word needs a letter) or the same language as the one before it; with none reliable it keeps the language unless the conversation has none yet) and stores `translations[locale]` under a row lock, or `translation_failed`. Reply side: `Enterprise::Concerns::Message` marks agent text replies and templates with `translation_pending` on create (never private notes, bots, forwarded messages, `template_params`, `direct_send`, `campaign_id` or `interactive`), and `SendReplyJob` runs `AgentMessageTranslationService` before dispatching: the reply goes out in `conversation_language`, the agent text is kept in `translations` (email replies translate only the text above the quoted history and rebuild the stored email html), and a failure (provider error, failed detection or any exception, which is reported) sends the text as typed with `translation_failed` without holding the send back. `Captain::Conversation::TranslatorService` picks Captain (`Captain::Llm::MessageTranslationService`, route `message_translation`, charged as AI Actions by the `Enterprise::Captain::BaseTaskService` wrapper inside `Llm::UsageTracker.track`; `Captain::Llm::LanguageDetectionService`, internal route `language_detection`, not charged) or Google (`Integrations::GoogleTranslate::ProcessorService#translate`/`#detect`). Both routes use the model's own reasoning options (`Llm::Models.thinking_for`). The manual Translate action (`messages#translate`, MCP `messages_translate`) keeps Google Translate unless the account has `auto_translate` with Captain as service; every translation records `translation_provider`. Google Translate hooks are checked with Google on create and whenever their `settings` change (`Integrations::GoogleTranslate::CredentialsValidator`), and only accept `type: service_account` keys with the required fields as strings (other Google credential types read local files or URLs taken from the key); key errors are logged, not reported. Per-conversation off switch: `additional_attributes.auto_translate_disabled` (boolean, 422 otherwise; `PATCH /conversations/:id` saves it only while the account has `auto_translate` on and otherwise ignores it with 200, and it travels in `conversation.updated` through `Conversation::CONVERSATION_UPDATED_ADDITIONAL_ATTRIBUTE_KEYS`) makes `Conversation#auto_translate_enabled?` false: replies are not flagged `translation_pending` and `messages/auto_translate` answers 403; in the dashboard, `MoreActions` (Turn off/Turn on auto-translate), and each real change posts an activity message naming the agent (`Enterprise::ActivityMessageHandler#handle_auto_translate_change`; unset → on and changes without `Current.user` post nothing). UI: `getAutoTranslate`/`getMessageTranslationState` getters, `TranslationToggle` (pending, failed, View original/translated with language names from `Intl.DisplayNames` in the agent's locale), Settings → Conversation workflow → `Translation.vue` (Captain marked Recommended with its AI Actions note; Google Translate sits behind Use other providers, which opens when Google is saved and then offers Switch to Captain), Captain settings model picker, and the widget `TranslatingContent` with View original on auto translated replies. Known gaps (as upstream): webhooks and the widget SDK see the untranslated reply on `message_created`; html emails from contacts are not translated.

### 3.6 CRM and contact management

- Contact type classification: `Contacts::SyncAttributes` promotes a visitor to lead when it has an email, phone number, `identifier`, `whatsapp_bsuid` (as `Contact.resolved_contacts` already counts BSUID-only contacts, so they stay listed with `crm_v2`) or social details. `Conversation` has an `after_create :mark_contact_as_lead` (inside the insert transaction, not a commit callback) that writes only a visitor contact, so a contact that is already a lead or customer gets no extra `contact.updated` event. `POST /contacts` creates leads, CSV, Intercom and Freshdesk imports always import leads and promote a matched visitor, and `ContactMergeAction` keeps the higher type. A lead never goes back to visitor; existing contacts are not backfilled.
- Contact conversation navigation: `GET /contacts/:id/conversations` keeps its default (latest `last_activity_at` first, 20 items, same payload; read by Mega Assistant and the MCP `contacts_conversations` tool) and adds an opt-in `conversation_id` (display id) that returns that conversation between the permitted ones created right before and after it (`(created_at, id)` tuple), with the same preloads; an inaccessible id is 404. `useContactConversationNavigation` stores that window in `contactConversations.neighbours` and `MessagesView` renders `ContactConversationLink` pills: older once the first message is loaded (a backward page shorter than `MessageFinder::PAGE_LIMIT` now marks the history loaded, and re-activating a fetched conversation keeps that flag), newer only on resolved conversations or while the list is scoped to the contact. `ViewAllConversations` dispatches `applyConversationFilters` with a lone `contact_id` filter and pins `created_at_desc` (`appliedFiltersSortBy`, cleared by any other filter); `ChatListHeader` then shows the contact name and only the clear button, and `ContactPanel` hides the previous-conversations accordion. Phones and the expanded layout move to the list first.
- Contact notes in the conversation sidebar (`ContactNotes`) turn off the editor character counter (`show-character-count=false`), so long notes are not shown against a limit.
- Reading mode: `ConversationView` provides one `useContactConversationNavigation` instance (`CONTACT_CONVERSATION_NAVIGATION`) to `MessagesView` and `ViewAllConversations`; the inbox view's `MessagesView` creates its own. A plain click on a pill (`openConversation`) scopes the list to the contact before navigating; modified clicks open the link. While the list is scoped to the open conversation's contact and it is not the latest one, `isReadingHistory` feeds `useReplyBoxCollapse` (`isReading`/`leaveReading`), so the existing `ReplyBoxFold` folds on every screen without saving `is_reply_box_collapsed` and shows `OlderConversationBar` instead of `CollapsedReplyBar`; `expand`/`reveal` (bar, Alt+L/Alt+P, pasted files, reply-to, insert-into-editor, AI assist) leave reading mode for that conversation. Clearing a contact scope from the list header opens the latest conversation (not on phones or the expanded layout).
- Custom attributes by data type and automation usage.
- Role-based visibility controls for sensitive attributes (Enterprise).
- Labels on contacts and conversations.
- The conversation context menu's label submenu uses fuzzy search with `picoSearch`, shows assigned labels first without changing each group's source order, and supports repeated selection without moving focus away from search. Blank queries show every label, and the context menu closes only when focus leaves it.
- Companies grouped by domain with unified timeline.
- Company enrichment (premium flag `company_enrichment`, appended to `feature_flags_ext_1`, off by default): `Companies::EnrichmentService` posts the domain to Context.dev `/v1/brand/retrieve` with the installation `CONTEXT_DEV_API_KEY` (the same key as onboarding branding), fills blank `description` (truncated to `Limits::COMPANY_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH_LIMIT`) and `additional_attributes` (`industry`, `sub_industry`, `employee_count_range`, `employee_count`, `city`, `country`, `country_code`, `phone`, `social_profiles`, `enriched_at`, `enriched_domain`). A domain already enriched is not looked up again and failed requests return nil without raising, so `Companies::EnrichJob` (queued `after_create_commit` only when the flag and key are present) is never retried, not even when the response or the save fails after the paid call (`discard_on StandardError`). `POST /companies/:id/enrichment` (administrators, `CompanyPolicy#enrich?`) refreshes with overwrite; 403 without the flag, 422 without a domain/key or when Context.dev has no profile. The company payload now includes `additional_attributes`; the company page shows the facts and a refresh button. The company pages redesign is deferred.
- The Companies sidebar entry and its CRUD authorization are available to administrators, standard agents, and custom roles with the `companies_manage` permission.
- Contact payloads expose `company_id` when Companies is enabled; contact updates can set or clear the company and keep `additional_attributes.company_name` synchronized.
- Contact import and export available to administrators and Enterprise roles with the `contact_manage` permission.
- Contact CSV export uses `CSVSafe` to prefix values beginning with formula characters and prevent their execution in spreadsheets.
- Intercom and Freshdesk imports are administrator-only and feature-gated by `data_import`; source credentials and durable mappings are stored per account.
- Contacts and conversation pages are processed through Sidekiq jobs, with idempotent item/mapping records, skip/error logs, resumable runs, and source-bucket API inboxes.
- Imports inactive for 15 minutes can be retried through an authorized account endpoint; retry acquires account/import locks, rotates the run identifier, and preserves cursor, statistics, and recorded errors.
- Connection drops: `DataImports::Importer::CONNECTION_ERRORS` (`ActiveRecord::ConnectionNotEstablished`, `ActiveRecord::ConnectionFailed`) are re-raised instead of failing the item or the import; the Intercom and Freshdesk base jobs `retry_on` them with `wait: :polynomially_longer, attempts: 3`, drop the broken connection before retrying and unwrap them from `ActiveJob::DeserializationError`; after the last attempt the import fails. `DataImports::Freshdesk::Normalizer` prefers `description_text`/`body_text` (`body_format: text`) over HTML; a single message whose HTML hits the parser limits (`HTML_PARSE_LIMIT_ERRORS`: tree depth, attributes per element) is recorded as a failed message and skipped instead of failing the import.
- Active WhatsApp blocking to drop inbound messages from blocked contacts.
- Per-contact message preferences: boolean columns `ignore_greeting`, `ignore_csat` and `ignore_agent_name` on `contacts` (default `false`), exposed in the contact payload and `PATCH /contacts/:id`. Honored only when `Inbox#contact_message_preferences_supported?` is true (WhatsApp with `Channel::Whatsapp::CONTACT_MESSAGE_PREFERENCES_PROVIDERS`: Evolution, WAHA, UAZAPI). They short-circuit `MessageTemplates::HookExecutionService#should_send_greeting?`, `CsatSurveyService#csat_enabled?` and `MessageContentPresenter#prefixed_content`. UI: `contact_message_preferences` section in the conversation panel (not for groups) and the attributes tab of the contact page.
- Out-of-office exceptions: `inboxes.out_of_office_config` column (jsonb: `ignore_groups`, `ignored_targets`, `ignored_labels`), saved whole and normalized by `InboxesController#format_out_of_office_config` and exposed in the inbox JSON. `Inboxes::OutOfOfficeExceptions#skip?` (through `Inbox#out_of_office_skipped_for?`) applies the bot rules: a group when `Conversation#whatsapp_group?` or an identifier ends in `@g.us`, entries with `@` against `contact.identifier`/`source_id`, the rest by phone digits, and contact labels. Honored by `HookExecutionService#should_send_out_of_office_message?` and `Template::OutOfOffice.perform_if_applicable` (Captain handoffs). UI in `WeeklyAvailability.vue`, Exceptions section.

### 3.7 Campaigns

- Ongoing campaigns for widget/live chat.
- One-off campaigns for WhatsApp, SMS, and API Channel.
- Meta template builder with approval lifecycle and sync.
- Template sync requests `previous_category` and `correct_category` from Meta and stores them in `metadata`; the `template_category_update` webhook triggers the same sync as `message_template_status_update`. The list and detail views flag a category that changed or is about to (`getCategoryChange` in `templateHelper`). Sync and stale-template cleanup set `skip_provider_sync`, which also stops the `MessageTemplate` callbacks from editing or deleting the template on Meta.
- `message_templates.active` (default `true`) is toggled by admins from the templates list; sync leaves it untouched and the change is not sent to Meta. The inbox payload exposes `inactive_message_template_ids` (`platform_template_id`) and the `inboxes/getAgentWhatsAppTemplates` getter (plus `getReplyBoxWhatsAppTemplates`) drops them from agent pickers; campaigns, Kanban automations and inbox settings keep using `getFilteredWhatsAppTemplates` with every template. When `active` changes, `MessageTemplate` invalidates the account inbox cache so open dashboards refresh.
- The cache-only inbox endpoint lists native and Twilio WhatsApp templates, applies the provider-specific exact-name key, and exposes the last synchronization-attempt time without calling Meta or Twilio.
- Rate control, multi-inbox rotation, and execution metrics.
- Campaign label in the conversation: `GET /conversations/:id/messages` returns `campaign` (`{ id, title }`, `id` = `display_id`) on messages with `additional_attributes.campaign_id`, preloading the campaigns in one query (`Message.campaigns_by_id`, which skips history separators). `push_event_data` (realtime events and the conversation list) carries `campaign` too, with the title cached per campaign (`Campaign.message_label_cache_key`, expired when the campaign is edited or deleted); `push_event_data_for_contact` leaves it out and `ActionCableListener` sends contacts their own payload without the campaign, on message and conversation events; webhooks and Agent Bot payloads get it too. `MessageCampaignLabel.vue` shows it above the bubble and, for administrators, links to the WhatsApp, SMS or API report by inbox; the message avatar is a megaphone, even when the provider echoes it back, and `bubbleClasses` paints the bubble `bg-n-violet-9` over the agent, bot, echo and template variants (failed ones stay red). In the report, each contact carries `conversation_id` and `message_id` (`Campaigns::ReportBuilder`) and its name opens the conversation at the campaign message (`?messageId=`).
- When editing WhatsApp and API campaigns, forms send `campaign_inboxes_attributes` with persisted row IDs for retained inboxes, no ID for new inboxes, and `id`/`_destroy: true` for removed inboxes, including when rotation is disabled. Initial form hydration preserves the persisted selection and individual quotas until the shared quota is changed; backend uniqueness validation remains in place.
- WhatsApp delivery failures retain the provider reason and normalized code in campaign reports; recipient failures continue processing, while inbox authentication, sender, and template failures pause resumable processing.
- Delivery funnel: `Campaigns::StatusSummary` and `Campaigns::BatchStatusSummary` also count `delivered` (latest campaign message per contact `delivered` or `read`) and `read`, returned additively as `delivered`/`read` on the campaign payload (index and show, so `campaigns_list`/`campaigns_get`) and on `report`; `sent`, `failed`, `processed`, `it_sent`, `status_send`, `status` and `read_status` keep their meaning. Each report contact carries `delivery_status` (`sent|delivered|read|failed`, `progress` counts as `sent`, `Campaigns::StatusSummary.delivery_status`) and, when failed, `failure_code`/`failure_reason` (the builder already computed them, but `show.json.jbuilder` did not render them). `CampaignReport.vue` shows Audience, Sent, Delivered, Read, Failed, Send rate (sent / attempted, the old "Delivery" tile), Delivery rate (delivered / sent) and Read rate, labels and filters by `delivery_status` (Sent keeps delivered and read, Delivered keeps read). Delivered and read depend on provider receipts (Cloud/NotificaMe webhooks, WAHA/Evolution/UazAPI acks). No new table: everything comes from `messages.status` and `campaigns_failed`.
- `Campaign has_many :campaigns_failed, dependent: :delete_all`: deleting a campaign removes its failure rows (they used to stay orphaned); conversations keep being nullified and sent messages stay. The delete dialog says so.
- Compared with the upstream campaign-history/batch-sending change, our design already covers it: every provider creates a real `Message` with `additional_attributes.campaign_id` in the contact's conversation (history in the timeline plus the campaign label), processing is one contact per `ProcessWhatsappCampaignJob` step with a lock, provider pacing, inbox rotation, business-hours pause and stored `processing_state`, and a contact with a campaign message is never sent again. The upstream `campaign_recipients` table, `campaign_history` endpoint, 100-contact batch job and `campaign_analytics` plan flag were not adopted.
- CSV audience import (WhatsApp and Channel API): `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/campaigns/audience_imports` (`import_file`, `label`; same authorization as creating campaigns) creates or reuses the label (lowercase, spaces as `-`), uploads the file as an ActiveStorage blob and enqueues `Campaigns::AudienceImportJob`; `GET .../campaigns/audience_imports/:id` returns the progress, kept in Redis for a day (`Campaigns::AudienceImportStatus`, account-scoped key, no new table). `Campaigns::AudienceCsvImportService` accepts `,` or `;` and the headers `phone_number`/`phone`/`telefono`/`telefone`/`celular`/`whatsapp`, `name`/`nombre`/`nome` and `email`/`correo`; it normalizes the phone to E.164, skips repeats within the file and finds existing contacts through `Whatsapp::PhoneNumberNormalizationService#phone_number_variants`, then by email. The optional `etiquetas`/`etiqueta`/`labels`/`tags` column (comma-separated) adds each row's own labels, normalized and checked against `RegexHelper::UNICODE_CHARACTER_NUMBER_HYPHEN_UNDERSCORE` (invalid ones are skipped without rejecting the row), and creates the missing `Label` records; the result returns `stats.labels`. Labels are only added (`add_labels`), never removed. An existing contact only gets the labels (and the phone if it had none); name, email and attributes are never overwritten. New ones are created as `lead` with the label. Up to 50,000 rows; the result lists up to 50 rejected rows (`missing_phone`, `invalid_phone`, `invalid`) and the blob is purged at the end. The form (`CampaignAudienceImport.vue`) polls every 2 s and adds the label to the audience on completion; campaigns still store a label audience. UTF-8/BOM cleanup and separator detection moved to `DataImport::CsvText`, shared with `DataImportJob`.

### 3.8 Help Center

- Slugs and public view: the article slug can be edited from the properties (`ArticleSlugField`, with "use title", a warning that the old URL stops working and a link to the new one) and the model validates it as unique and URL-safe (`[a-zA-Z0-9_-]`) instead of hitting the unique index and returning a 500; automatic slugs carry a random suffix, so two same-title articles created in the same second don't collide, and the new article page saves once even if edits keep coming while it is being created. On the public portal, the logo keeps the current locale, category pages hide the authors line when every author was deleted, and previews strip Markdown table syntax.
- Multi-language articles with per-language draft state. Switching language on a public article or category page opens the same article or category in the chosen language when a published translation exists (`Article#translation_family`, `Category#translation_family`, `@locale_switch_urls`), and the portal home of that language otherwise.
- Original article: `associated_article_id` links a translation to its original ("Original article" picker in `ArticleEditorProperties`); linking to a translation points at its original (families are one level deep) and an article with translations cannot be linked to another. The article JSON adds `associated_article_id`, `locale` and `root_article` (`id`, `title`, `locale`). `Article#unique_locale_in_family` allows one article per language in a family and is only checked when an article joins a family or changes language, so older families with repeated languages stay editable. AI bulk translation (`bulk_actions` translate, `Captain::Articles::TranslateJob`) answers 422 (`cannot_overwrite_original`) when the target language is the original's, even with `force`.
- Removing a locale: `DELETE /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/portals/:id/remove_locale` with `locale` (administrators) runs `Portal#remove_locale!` in one transaction: it drops the locale from `allowed_locales`, `locale_translations` and `popular_content` and destroys its articles and categories; 422 for the default locale. `LocaleList` confirms with the article and category counts. `config/languages/language_map.yml` quotes the `'no'` key (unquoted YAML read it as `false`), so Norwegian gets its name.
- Changed elsewhere: the article edit page compares the article on the server with the editor when the tab becomes visible again or the connection comes back (after its own save settles); when it differs, `ArticleEditorHeader` offers **Load latest version**, which drops the queued save first.
- Public images: `PortalImageHelper#portal_image_src` rewrites Active Storage URLs on the installation origin (`FRONTEND_URL`/`HELPCENTER_URL`) to relative paths in article Markdown and author avatars, so they follow the help center's current domain without changing saved content.
- Search engines: `portal.config.disable_search_indexing` (boolean; Settings › SEO, `PortalSeoSettings`) and `article.meta.noindex` (boolean; article properties, off while the portal is hidden) make `Public::Api::V1::Portals::BaseController` send `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, and `Portal#search_indexable_articles` leaves them out of the sitemap; the pages stay public.
- Author filter: the article list takes `author_id` from the URL (author dropdown with teammate search) instead of the Mine tab; the status counts honor the selected author while `mine_articles_count` stays the current user's, the single GROUP BY count is kept and `/articles/mine` still works.
- Dark logo: `Portal` has a `dark_logo` attachment (`dark_logo_blob_id` on create or update; `DELETE /portals/:id/logo?dark=true` removes it); the portal JSON adds `dark_logo`, and the public `portals/_logo` partial shows it in dark mode.
- Help Center navigation: the dashboard routes require `administrator` or `knowledge_base_manage` (the `agent` role was removed, as the API already required); agents still insert articles in replies. `LlmFormatter::ArticleLlmFormatter` adds `Public URL` (custom domain or help center root, `/hc/<portal>/articles/<slug>`) for published articles, so Captain shares real links.
- Published article title/content edits are staged in draft columns, with review, publish, and discard flows that preserve the live version until publication.
- Selectable portal layouts: classic landing page or documentation sidebar mode.
- Portal analytics are stored in `portal.config.analytics`; only administrators can update the whitelisted provider identifiers, which the model validates before the public layout renders their tracking snippets.
- Recommended content per locale is stored in `portal.config.popular_content`, with ordered lists capped at 3 categories and 6 articles; deleted records and unpublished articles are omitted while popularity-based fallback remains available.
- Editor with slash menu, native tables, background image uploads (progress, cancellation and retry), and MP4 uploads or supported video URLs; media keeps its position while uploading and video dimensions persist in the editor and public portal.
- The article-editor slash menu exposes `horizontalRule`, which inserts the existing ProseMirror horizontal-rule node and moves the caret to the paragraph below it.
- When the ProseMirror selection is inside a table, the slash menu filters out block commands that Markdown cells cannot persist and retains only inline marks; Arrow Up/Down and Ctrl+N/P are handled by the menu while it has items.
- Article creation directly from category views.
- In-editor image resizing for article composition.
- Conversation insertion flow with stable popover search.
- Embedding search in Enterprise for semantic retrieval.
- Assisted FAQ generation from PDF with additional context and selective publishing (Enterprise).
- Public Help Center: `Public::Api::V1::Portals::ArticlesController#set_article` uses `not_archived`, so an archived article returns 404 (404 page for HTML, `head :not_found` otherwise); drafts stay reachable by slug for the dashboard preview. The public article JSON only lists published translations.

### 3.9 Sales Kanban (Mega)

- Funnels with configurable stages and default stage support.
- Board and list views for distinct team workflows.
- `ListTab` consumes `GET /kanban_items` in 30-card pages, replaces state on page one, and appends unique items on later pages; switching funnels resets cards and pagination metadata before requesting the new first page.
- With a search or header filters active, `ListTab` reads `GET /kanban_items/filter` instead (same 30-card pages, `query` plus every filter, debounced 300 ms) so search and the date filter reach the whole funnel; `KanbanItems::FilterService#apply_query_filter` is the text match shared by `/search` and `/filter` (card id or `#id`, title, description, customer name or email, LIKE wildcards escaped). The endpoint parameters are documented in Swagger, which also feeds the `kanban_tasks_filter` MCP tool, Postman and the Super Admin API docs.
- `GET /kanban_items/report_export` uses the Kanban policy scope and report filters without pagination. It emits a UTF-8 BOM CSV with CRLF rows through Ruby CSV, localized headers/priorities, configured stage names, and explicit item currency before the account default.
- Filters by inbox, channel, stage, and activity.
- Conversation label filters in board/list views and stage statistics.
- The shared item-creation form loads account labels and sends selected titles through `kanban_item.labels`; the create endpoint assigns them to the new item before its single persistence operation, without changing linked-conversation labels. Item cards retain the item-level label management endpoint.
- 360 item workspace: checklist, notes, attachments, offers, agents, attributes.
- The Kanban item API accepts funnel global fields in `item_details.custom_attributes` as either a `{name, type, value}` array or a name-keyed object; the latter is limited to fields configured on the funnel.
- Long Kanban notes use a details dialog with rendered Markdown, viewport-bounded scrolling, forced wrapping for unbroken text, attachment previews, and a permission-gated direct edit action.
- Remote conversation/contact lookup in item relationship selectors.
- Account-level base currency via `accounts.update` (`settings.default_currency`).
- Active monetary consumers resolve currency through the shared helper/composable with offer → item → account → locale precedence; old/new historical values are formatted independently and zero amounts remain visible.
- `funnels/:id/stage_stats` retains `count` and `total_value` and adds per-stage `value_totals` (`currency` or `label`, code, total, and distinct item count) across the complete filtered policy scope; `KanbanColumn` prioritizes that complete aggregate, uses local grouping only as a compatibility fallback, and exposes an accessible semantic-token tooltip.
- Selector counts skip offer-value calculations. Full totals project only the required offers and currency; search/filter cache keys reuse preloaded conversations, and reports preload funnels and omit unused columns. Calendar reminders filter candidates by their configured lead time before loading and locking events, preserving the final locked check and cron frequency.
- Monetary custom offers support per-offer currency (`item_details.offers[].currency`) as an override over account default.
- Items without offers render without currency and are excluded from monetary totals to prevent fallback-based mixing.
- Native links to contact and conversation.
- History compares normalized IDs before/after each save (`conversation_display_id`, `item_details.conversation_id`, and `conversation_ids`), records each link/unlink, and resolves inbox names exclusively within the item account. Events append under an item row lock with unique IDs and a 500-entry limit; link/unlink messages go to the event conversation. The interface translates these actions in ES/EN/PT-BR. Previously stored incorrect names are not rewritten automatically. The job ID prevents duplicate history events on retry, and the timestamp captured when emitting the event preserves chronological order despite worker delays.
- Account-level Google Calendar sync can upsert scheduled/deadline items into `CalendarEvent` records without overwriting legacy `item_details` Google fields.
- Calendar reminders run every minute, create one idempotent `Notification` with `CalendarEvent` as actor for `created_by_user_id`, and reuse targeted ActionCable, Web Push, and snooze delivery; guest email metadata never determines the internal recipient.
- Kanban item calendar controls mount only when the account has a connected `GoogleCalendarIntegration` and a `CalendarConnection#calendar_id`; they then read `CalendarEvent`/`ExternalCalendarEvent`, expose Google links when available, and keep legacy Google IDs as fallback-only data.
- Stage-driven automations and quick-message options.
- Timed `send_message` stage rules capture the primary conversation ID and latest incoming message ID when scheduled. `StageTimeAutomationJob` revalidates the stage entry and rule, while `KanbanItems::StageFollowUpService` locks the item, cancels after any newer contact reply, and deduplicates with an item/rule/stage-entry key in message content attributes. Text and persisted funnel media use `Messages::MessageBuilder`; the shared Woot editor exposes ReplyBox Liquid variables and the message `Liquidable` concern resolves them against the target conversation. Approved templates are exposed and accepted only for configured `whatsapp_cloud` inboxes, retaining their inbox-specific `template_params` and the existing 24-hour window enforcement.
- Entry stages persist `ignore_group_conversations`; when enabled, `AutoCreateItemJob` does not create items for conversations whose `contact_inbox.source_id` ends in `@g.us`, without changing legacy condition-based stages.
- Automatic stage-template delivery is keyed by `kanban_item_id` and the stable stage-template ID in the outgoing message content attributes. The template editor enables the standard variable picker when `{{` is typed; `Liquidable` resolves values such as `{{contact.name}}` when creating the outgoing message. Existing templates default to one delivery per item; `resend_on_entry` opts a template into delivery on every qualifying stage entry, while manual quick messages do not consult this history.
- Quick message: with `funnel.settings.quick_message_enabled`, a linked conversation and edit permission (`canMutateKanbanItems`), `KanbanItem` (card ⋯ menu), `KanbanItemDetailView` (drawer header, desktop and phone row) and `KanbanDealDetailPage` (phone header on the board, not when opened from a conversation, in a `BottomSheet`) open `SendMessageTemplate` for `item.conversation.display_id`. The same switch keeps gating the automatic stage templates (`KanbanTemplateMessageHandler#quick_message_enabled?`). The old right-click `CustomContextMenu` entry was never opened by anything.
- `notify_team` stage rules resolve selected team members and current item assignees at execution time, exclude the user who performed the move, deduplicate users, and create live per-user `kanban_stage_automation` notifications. A non-blocking Kanban banner displays one alert directly or groups multiple alerts into an expandable list with individual and bulk dismissal; email delivery is intentionally excluded.
- A pending checklist task with a due date schedules an internal alert for its assigned agent. The job verifies the task, recipient, and deadline are still current before delivery, locks the Kanban item to prevent duplicate concurrent alerts, and recreates a dismissed alert only after the funnel-configured interval while the task remains pending.
- `KanbanItems::ChecklistService` normalizes checklist keys when scheduling alerts. After saving a duplication, it schedules only copied entries with their new IDs and the destination card ID; merging lists does not reschedule existing entries. Missing dates or assignees do not schedule alerts, and past deadlines enqueue for immediate execution.
- Realtime synchronization with chat list and contact panel.
- `GET /kanban_items?contact_id=<id>` keeps the authoritative Kanban policy scope, resolves membership through all linked conversation display IDs, and filters open items before pagination only for funnels with `settings.contact_panel_contact_wide_items` enabled. The setting defaults to false; ContactPanel unions the expanded result with `currentChat.kanban_items` and refreshes it on Kanban events.
- The contact/conversation panel reuses the funnel agent candidates and persists assignment/removal through the Kanban item endpoints.
- The board opens a linked conversation from its channel icon without reinitializing board data. On mobile, item details separate business content from the profile/accordions and reuse status, move, and agent dialogs from ContactPanel.
- The contact/conversation panel Kanban block is hidden when the user has no visible items and no accessible funnels to create items.
- The main sidebar Kanban entry is hidden for non-admin users when they have no accessible active funnels.
- Funnel agent changes emit a realtime event to refresh the sidebar, funnels and visible items without a reload.
- A Kanban item can link multiple conversations: `conversation_display_id` keeps the primary conversation for compatibility and `item_details.conversation_ids` stores the full set; visibility, filters, chat-list realtime, and the ContactPanel Kanban block consider any linked conversation. The relationship picker is limited to the funnel inboxes and shows the channel icon/inbox name.
- `AutoCreateItemJob` guarantees one open item per contact and funnel: it serializes processing with the contact lock, reuses the oldest open item, and appends the new conversation when its inbox belongs to the funnel. Separate contacts are not inferred from their attributes, funnels are evaluated independently, and `won`/`lost` items allow a new opportunity.
- When a conversation opens from the Kanban drawer, `ConversationSidebar` passes `hidePreviousConversations` to `ContactPanel` to hide the previous-conversations accordion; the standard conversation panel keeps that accordion. The card deduplicates serialized conversations by `inbox.channel_type` and, when absent, `inbox_id`, to display one icon per channel and filters the selector to the clicked icon's channel.
- Kanban card unread counts: `KanbanItems::UnreadCountsService` decorates `GET /kanban_items`, `/kanban_items/filter` and `/kanban_items/:id` responses after serialization (outside the per-item cache and the `kanban.item.*` broadcasts). It filters linked conversations with `Conversations::PermissionFilterService` and counts `incoming` messages after `agent_last_seen_at` in one query grouped by `display_id` (index `conversation_id, account_id, message_type, created_at`). It adds `unread_count` to `conversation` and every `linked_conversations[]` entry, plus `unread_count` and `unread_conversation_ids` (display IDs) on the item. In the frontend, `kanban.conversationUnreadCounts` keeps the count per `display_id`: `KanbanItem` seeds it from the payload only when `unread_count` is present, an incoming `message.created` increments it when a card shows that conversation, and the drawer resets it on open and close; opening also dispatches `markMessagesRead`. Channel buttons add up conversations of the same channel, the selector sorts them by unread count, and `settings.card_field_visibility.showUnreadBadge` (default `true`) hides it per funnel. Card `<button>` elements use `p-0` so they no longer inherit the global padding that misaligned the `+` button.
- If a linked conversation is deleted, the Kanban item remains as history and the broken relationship is cleared.
- Consistent access scope across the API, cache, and realtime events: administrators can view every funnel and item; `agent` and the custom-role permission `kanban_view` receive only authorized resources.
- The current administrator can assign themselves to any item, individually or in bulk, and remove themselves individually, even when absent from `settings.agents` and the funnel inboxes; this exception does not allow assigning other ineligible users.
- The `kanban_view` custom-role permission can operate visible items; `kanban_manage` also creates funnels and is added to `settings.agents` on the funnel it creates. Only assigned funnels may have their content and structure edited; it cannot delete, set the default, or change `unassigned_visibility`.
- Items retain `created_by_id`, so the creator always keeps visibility. With a valid linked conversation, the current assignee can view it only when also selected on the funnel, and any manually assigned item agent can view it; a stale link is visible only to the administrator and creator.
- `unassigned_visibility` supports `everyone` (the legacy/default value) and `assigned_only`; `everyone` grants every funnel agent visibility of every funnel item, including assigned ones, while `assigned_only` preserves the authorized-agent scope.
- Any account member with Kanban access can be added to `settings.agents`, independently of `settings.inboxes`; a custom role requires `kanban_view` or `kanban_manage`. With configured inboxes, new manual item assignments still require access to at least one; moving an item automatically adds its assigned agents to the destination funnel without changing their inbox permissions.
- Global configuration is readable by `agent`, `kanban_view`, `kanban_manage`, and administrators; create, update, and delete require an administrator. Global automation endpoints are administrator-only; `kanban_manage` modifies assigned funnels only.

### 3.10 Integrations and extensibility

- Universal outbound API: `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/outbound_messages` requires an `api_access_token` and inbox authorization; it accepts exactly one of `phone_number`, `email`, `contact_id`, or `source_id`, resolves or creates the contact/contact-inbox/conversation, and hands text, one attachment, or a WhatsApp template to `Messages::MessageBuilder` and `SendReplyJob`. For templates, it renders the synced approved BODY with its variables before persisting the message, so the dashboard and webhooks expose the sent content. Native WhatsApp channel templates (excluding Twilio) with media headers accept `header.media_file` as multipart data or a signed blob ID; the service stores it and generates `header.media_url` for the provider. `Idempotency-Key` is optional: when omitted, every request is a new send; when provided, an identical retry returns the original response and a different payload returns `409`. HTTP `202` confirms local queueing, not provider delivery.
- Webhooks with enriched payload and global HMAC-SHA256 secret.
- On hosted installations, API-token access and outbound webhook delivery require the `api_and_webhooks` account feature; self-hosted installations remain enabled by default.
- `inbox_updated` webhook event for inbox state changes and disconnects.
- Dashboard Apps for embedded iFrame extensions by context; authenticated account users can read them, while only administrators can create, update, or delete them.
- The JSON `appContext` event preserves `conversation`, `contact`, and `currentAgent`, adding `customAttributes` from `attributes/getAttributes` and `theme` (`light`/`dark`) from the `body` class. Definition changes (including store mutations), theme changes, and tab reopening resend context to visible loaded frames. `chatwoot-dashboard-app:fetch-info` identifies the requesting iframe by `event.source`; unmounting removes the listener and theme observer.
- Dashboard Scripts (Super Admin) for global customization without core edits. `SuperAdmin::DashboardScriptsController` owns index/new/create/edit/update/destroy (show redirects to edit); load order is `DashboardScript.ordered` (creation order). The preview opens the real dashboard of a super admin account and injects unsaved code after the iframe loads; other active scripts also load there. While editing, `DashboardController` skips the open script only in a super admin preview to avoid running it twice. On phones, the top bar shows back or create, the editor keeps its own scroll, and the preview fits the iframe to the available width.
- Agent Bots (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::AgentBotsController` replaces the Administrate index (search by name, description, URL, account name or `#id`; `scope` filter global/account with counts; 25 per page, newest first) and adds `GET /super_admin/agent_bots/account_search` for the form's account picker. Create/update/destroy stay on Administrate; the generic form takes `field_partials` to swap the `account` field for the picker, whose empty `account_id` makes the bot global. The bot page lists `agent_bot_inboxes` with inbox, account and status, and shows the `secret` (`X-Chatwoot-Signature` signing) and access token masked.
- Platform apps (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::PlatformAppsController` replaces the Administrate index (search by name or `#id`, 25 per page) and extends `show` with account search, the `access` filter (granted/none) and pagination. Account, user and bot counts come from `platform_app_permissibles` and skip records deleted since. `grant_account`, `revoke_account` and `grant_all_accounts` return to the same search with translated notices; `grant_all_accounts_access` uses a switch partial through `field_partials` and still never revokes when unchecked.
- Platform banners (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::PlatformBannersController` replaces the Administrate index (search by title, content or `#id`; `status` filter live/scheduled/ended/inactive with counts) and adds `POST /super_admin/platform_banners/:id/toggle_active`. The form uses `field_partials` for type and roles as radios and `active` as a switch: the type used to be a selectize (jQuery) select whose changes did not fire the native `change` the preview listens to. `_preview` renders from the saved banner on its page and live inside the form; the `info` tone uses the brand color with white text like `StatusBanner.vue`. `.sa-seg` is now `position: relative` so its hidden radios do not widen the page when the strip scrolls.
- Feature announcements: `PlatformBanner` adds `banner_type: feature_announcement` (4; 3 is already `success`) and `video_url` (an image or a video). `media_type` resolves it to `video` when it matches the `config/markdown_embeds.yml` regexes except script-based embeds (`github_gist`, `wistia`, `tiktok`), otherwise by extension (Marcel) or, for `/storage/file/:blob_key`, by the blob's `content_type`; without a type the announcement is invalid. The form uploads the file to `POST /super_admin/uploads` (like custom branding) and `PublicStorageController` serves byte ranges (`206`, up to 10 MB per request) so videos can seek and play in Safari. Changing the media also bumps `content_updated_at`. `StatusBanner.vue` fetches banners once the current account is set (on a direct load it was not set at mount and nothing was fetched). They come through the same `GET /api/v1/accounts/:id/platform_banners` (targeting, role, dates, `ENABLE_PLATFORM_BANNERS` and `PlatformBannersChannel`): `StatusBanner.vue` keeps them out of the bar and hands them to `components-next/feature-announcement/FeatureAnnouncement.vue` (outside onboarding), a `<dialog>` that shows the image or plays the embed template's `src` (video files use `<video>`), uses the YouTube thumbnail and renders the content with its own markdown-it with lists (MessageFormatter turns them off). Closing it calls `POST .../platform_banners/:id/dismiss` per announcement, so the dismissal lives in `platform_banner_dismissals` and comes back if the announcement is edited; the bar's `dismiss_all` no longer includes them. In Super Admin `_preview` switches between the bar and a panel with the video and shows `video_url` or `status_url` depending on the type. The Super Admin flash hides again after 4 s (`data-flash-message`) and the open single selectize stays dark in dark mode
- WhatsApp providers (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::WhatsappProviderServersController#index` filters by `q` (name, key or URL) and `provider`, and counts every server's inboxes in one query grouping `Channel::Whatsapp` by provider and `server_key` (empty = `server_1`). `WhatsappProviderServer#channels` holds the channel scope used by `inbox_count` and the server page (first 50 inboxes). `api_url_env_name`/`admin_token_env_name` return the provider's base variables for the legacy slot, so the page and `api_url_value` show the real URL. Deleting a server with inboxes returns to its page with a translated alert; updating still returns to the list.
- Push diagnostics (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::PushDiagnosticsController#user_search` (`GET /super_admin/push_diagnostics/user_search?q=`) finds users by name, email or id (up to 8) and counts their `NotificationSubscription` rows in one grouped query; it feeds the shared `data-sa-picker` search, which submits `user_query` on pick. `create` still delegates to `Notification::PushTestService` (which now sends `body` in the web push payload `public/sw.js` reads too) and keeps the selection (`subscription_ids`) when the page renders again. `SuperAdmin::PushDiagnosticsHelper` names each device by its endpoint host (`fcm.googleapis.com` = Chrome, Mozilla = Firefox, `web.push.apple.com` = Safari, `notify.windows.com` = Edge) or as the mobile app, and lists the other `subscription_attributes` (no keys or tokens). The delete button carries `data-sa-confirm` itself: `_ui_script` now reads the confirmation from `event.submitter` as well as the form. Styles in `_push_diagnostics.scss` (`.sa-pd`)
- Revoked browser push subscriptions: a browser that revokes a subscription points its endpoint at a reserved `.invalid` host (Chrome: `permanently-removed.invalid`). `NotificationSubscription#endpoint_revoked?` detects it and `Notification::PushNotificationService` and `Whatsapp::CallPushService` delete the subscription instead of failing on every push.
- Settings pages (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::AppConfigsHelper::APP_CONFIG_SECTIONS` holds the sections of each `?config=`; `app_config_sections` crosses them with `@allowed_configs` (which Enterprise widens per plan) and adds an `other` section with anything none lists. `app_config_kind` picks the control (switch, secret, code, image, color, select or Captain's provider and model selects) and `app_config_feature` finds the feature by `config_key`, so `?config=facebook` shows Messenger. Section titles live under `super_admin.app_config_sections`; `WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT`, `MAXIMUM_FILE_UPLOAD_SIZE`, `ACCOUNT_EMAILS_LIMIT` and `ACCOUNT_EMAILS_PLAN_LIMITS` got a title and description under `super_admin.installation_configs`. The save notice comes from `super_admin.pages.app_config.saved` / `saved_restart`. The shared switch takes `code` to show the variable name, and the `.sa-form` field rule no longer styles `input[type=color]`. Styles in `_app_configs.scss` (`.sa-ac`)
- AI providers (Super Admin): new `SuperAdmin::LlmProvidersController#show` action (`resources :llm_providers` now includes `:show`); `validate_capabilities` returns to the provider page when called with `from=show` and to the list otherwise. `SuperAdmin::LlmProvidersHelper` sums up each capability of the provider (`passed` when every model passed, `failed` when any failed, `not_tested` otherwise), builds the per-model state, the failed tests and the credit or quota warnings (`insufficient_credit`, `quota_exceeded`). The form keeps the same `model_rows` params; its JavaScript in `entrypoints/superadmin.js` now replaces the index in the reasoning select of new rows too and no longer copies the model name into the price fields. The results dialog and its `_model_status` partial are gone. Styles in `_llm_providers.scss` (`.sa-lp`)
- AI providers on RubyLLM 2 (Super Admin): `llm_providers.preset` (migration `20260930200000`, existing OpenAI-compatible rows mapped by host). `LlmProviderPresets` runs `openrouter`, `xai`, `deepseek`, `mistral`, `perplexity`, `ollama` and `gpustack` on RubyLLM's own provider and every other preset on its OpenAI provider; the DeepSeek-through-Anthropic detour is gone. `Llm::ProviderCapabilityValidator` tries a gateway model on `/responses` then `/chat/completions` and stores `capabilities.chat.<model>.protocol`, read by `LlmProvider#protocol_for` at every chat site. `Llm::Config.configure_openai_endpoint` picks Responses for api.openai.com and sends a `system` role to gateways. `LlmProvider#thinking_options` turns the configured effort into a level, a budget (`THINKING_BUDGETS`) or nothing from the regenerated `config/llm_models.json`; `Llm::Temperature` drops temperature while a model thinks.
- Model prices (Super Admin → AI providers): `LlmProviderModelPricing::PRICE_FIELDS` adds `cache_write`, the price per 1M tokens written to the cache. `Llm::UsageInstrumentation` passes `cache_write` (RubyLLM's `cache_creation_tokens`) to `Llm::UsageTracker`; `input_tokens` still includes them, so the reports do not change, and `token_cost_usd` prices them at `cache_write` or, when empty, at `input`. They are not stored separately: `LlmUsageEvent.backfill_missing_costs!` prices them at `input`. In the form, each model groups its AI Actions and reasoning on one side and the provider prices on the other; the fold shows input / output (`llm_model_cost_summary`) or "No prices", and the audio minute only shows when the transcription use is checked
- Plan features (Super Admin): `billing_plans/_form` reuses the pieces of `accounts/_tab_features` (`.sa-fgrid`, `.sa-frow`, `.sa-frow-h`, `.sa-frow-tags`, `.sa-ftools`) with the category names from `super_admin.accounts_ui.feature_categories`. The filter (`data-feature-filter`) combines with the search; the filter and category counts (`super_admin.billing.plans.feature_filters` and `group_count`) are recalculated on every change. In the form the grid uses as many 230 px columns as fit (`.sa-plan-features`, in `_billing.scss`), since the section index takes width. The `billing_plan[features][]` parameter is unchanged.
- Sign in (Super Admin): `SuperAdmin::Devise::SessionsController#create` renders `new` again with status 422 and `flash.now[:error]` (`super_admin.login.invalid`) when the credentials fail, instead of redirecting without the email; signing in redirects to `stored_location_for(:super_admin)` or `super_admin_root_path` (before, always to Users). The view uses `LOGO` from `GlobalConfig` with `INSTALLATION_NAME` as alt text, title and texts in `super_admin.login` (`title`, `show_password`, `hide_password`, `submitting`). The view uses `.sa-login-panel` and `.sa-login-main` (`_login.scss`); the panel's shades mix `--woot-500` with black or white (`color-mix`), so they follow `BRAND_PRIMARY_COLOR`. Mega messages live in `super_admin.login.messages` (one at random per load). The language picker reloads with `?locale=`, which `SwitchLocale` honors and the form sends back; on sign in, `remember_login_locale` stores it in `ui_settings['locale']` when it is in `SuperAdmin::ApplicationController::SUPPORTED_LOCALES`.
- Super Admin theme: `_theme.scss` defines on `:root` one CSS property per palette color with its light value and, under `:root.dark`, the dark one; every `$sa-*` in the Super Admin SCSS points at its `var(--sa-*)`. The `.sa` tokens have a dark version plus `--sa-inverse` for dark floating surfaces; buttons, segmented controls, hovers and links use semantic tokens (`--sa-control*`, `--sa-hover*`, `--sa-brand-hover`, `--sa-brand-text`, `--sa-danger-*`, `--sa-seg-*`) that keep the previous values in light; sprite icons without `fill` take the text color; text on color uses `$sa-on-color` (fixed white). Tailwind utilities that still reach it (the Vue dashboard and playground, older views, the plan card the license server sends) and Administrate's multi select are remapped in dark. `super_admin/shared/_theme_script` runs in the `<head>` of the layout and the sign in page: it reads `localStorage.color_scheme` (`light`, `dark`, `auto`, shared with the agent dashboard), sets `.dark` and `color-scheme` on `<html>` and follows the system and other tabs; `window.saTheme.set` stores the menu choice (`super_admin.nav.theme`).
- Platform Apps for high-level external integrations via API.
- Business integrations: Slack, Linear, Shopify, WooCommerce, Stripe, Notion, CRM, Google Calendar, Tasks.
- Stripe in the contact panel (account flag `stripe_integration`, app `stripe` in `config/integration/apps.yml`): unrelated to `Billing::*` and to the installation `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`. `POST /api/v1/accounts/:id/integrations/stripe` (admin) takes `api_key` (`sk_`/`rk_` + `test`/`live`), validates it with `GET /v1/customers?limit=1` and stores it in the encrypted `integrations_hooks.access_token`, with `settings.livemode`, `settings.connected_at` and `reference_id` (the Stripe account id when the key may read it); `GET` shows those details and `DELETE` removes the hook (both still work with the flag off). The generic hooks API answers 404 for `stripe`. `GET .../integrations/stripe/customer?conversation_id=<display_id>[&customer_id=]` authorizes `ConversationPolicy#show?`, then `Integrations::Stripe::CustomerSummary` searches customers by the contact email (exact, case-insensitive), the last 5 subscriptions (product names via `/v1/products`, skipped without that permission) and the last 5 invoices, returning only the panel's fields. `Integrations::Stripe::Client` (Faraday, 5 s open / 10 s read, pinned `Stripe-Version`) never uses the Stripe gem's global key; 401 → `stripe_reauthorization_required` plus `prompt_reauthorization!`, 403 → `stripe_missing_permissions`, the rest → `stripe_unavailable`, never the provider message. UI: `StripeCustomer.vue` / `StripeSubscription.vue` in the `stripe_customer` section of `ContactPanel` (details expand in place), settings page `settings/integrations/Stripe.vue`; app logos may be an icon class (`i-logos-stripe`).
- Slack supports `two_way` (default) and `alert`; the latter preserves sync to Slack but discards incoming thread replies so they never reach the customer.
- Tasks is guarded by the `activities` account feature and an enabled account-scoped `Integrations::Hook`; the same pair controls integration visibility, API access, route access, and the sidebar entry.
- `/activities` persists its calendar/list choice and operational criteria in the route query. Without `status` —or with an invalid value—it starts at `all` and sends no filter; valid explicit values are preserved. Calendar requests remain unpaginated; list requests use server-side title search, ordering, grouping order, and opt-in `page`/`per_page` metadata.
- Calendar requests use explicit `overlaps_from`/`overlaps_to` interval intersection. The client projects a multi-day task onto every visible intersected date rather than only its start date.
- Task authorization uses three custom-role permissions: `task_manage` creates tasks and manages only records assigned to or created by the user, `task_view_all` adds account-wide read-only access, and `task_reports_view` grants task reports and their read-only drilldowns. Standard agents implicitly receive the own/assigned scope; administrators retain full access and exclusive deletion.
- `AccountTask` is the source of truth for type, priority, assignee, participants, guests, contact/conversation, and unique Kanban/`CalendarEvent` links; it persists pending/in-progress/completed/cancelled and derives overdue from `ends_at`; every ID is resolved within the current account.
- `ActivityType#color` stores one of the 22 visual palette identifiers shared with Google Calendar. A migration converts the six legacy colors and the UI reuses one class map; status adds decoration without overriding the task type background.
- `ActivityType#active` is toggled from Settings -> Integrations -> Tasks (a switch per row on desktop, the type sheet on phones); `TaskDialog` only offers active types. `DELETE` on a type with tasks returns 422 (`restrict_with_error`), and the settings page then offers to deactivate it.
