Desktop edits types in place; phones use a grouped list and a `BottomSheet`.
- The Tasks page takes the header, `TasksToolbar` filters and month control of `ScheduledMessagesPage`, and the month grid, phone dots and day agenda of `CalendarPage`; the search also filters the calendar (`q`); chips use `getGoogleCalendarChipClass` (dark text on the light colors 7-9, shared with the Calendar). `TaskDialog` keeps `open(task, date, context)`: desktop follows the event dialog (`4xl`, a 20rem aside with guests, the customer context and Kanban; Kanban stages show their funnel color), phones get a tall `BottomSheet` with grouped rows and picker screens. A new task without a date starts at the next full hour. The contact panel section lists open tasks with the type color and folds closed ones (last 20) under "Previous".
- `outcome_summary` records the closure result and is required by both UI and model for `completed` and `cancelled`; reopening preserves it as editable history.
- `AccountTasks::TriggerDueNotificationsJob` runs every minute and creates one `account_task_due` notification at `ends_at`, exclusively for `assignee_id` and only while the task remains pending/in progress and Tasks is available. The assignee can mark it as seen or snooze it through `snoozed_until`; the global reopener republishes it automatically. Rescheduling, reassignment, or closure removes the previous alert so it can be recalculated. `ActivityDueReminderPopup` and `CalendarEventReminderPopup` keep their own queue and logic and share the `components/reminders/ReminderDialog.vue` view (rows only for data present, a snooze menu on desktop and a list inside the sheet on phones, dates in the account locale through `reminderFormat.js`); any close (action, X, Esc or click outside) moves on to the next reminder. `Dialog` ignores the native `close` event that arrives after a programmatic close.
- Recurrence roots persist type, interval, weekdays, and a date/count limit. `AccountTasks::RecurrenceService` materializes up to 100 independent child tasks, inherits assignee/context/participants/Kanban/Google, updates open occurrences, and preserves closed ones.
- Optional task and `CalendarEvent` projection references use `ON DELETE SET NULL`. Tasks snapshot creator, assignee, contact, conversation, and Kanban context; relation deletion clears live IDs, resynchronizes Google, and keeps readable historical labels. Contact cascading has one owner per level (`ContactInbox` → conversation → message), preventing duplicate jobs.
- Destroying an `AccountUser` synchronously clears that account's assignments, participation, and due alerts, then resynchronizes affected Google events after commit to remove the former attendee. Destroying a task preserves its managed Kanban item, removes task ownership metadata, and retries a failed Google cancellation.
- `/account_tasks` transactionally coordinates the task and one customer-linked or free Kanban item. Editing updates or moves the same item; detaching never deletes it.
- The UI loads and displays Kanban only when the account has `kanban_board`; the API ignores new Kanban links while the feature is disabled and preserves any existing hidden link when the task is edited.
- Selecting a conversation loads its visible Kanban items. Linking an existing item never mutates it and the item may be shared by multiple tasks; “Create new” keeps the managed funnel/stage workflow.
- ReplyBox exposes quick creation only when the Tasks feature and hook are enabled. It loads types, agents, funnels, and Google capabilities on demand, then opens `TaskDialog` with the current contact and conversation; the dialog automatically resolves their related Kanban items.
- ContactPanel renders a dedicated Tasks accordion using the calendar visual pattern and queries `/account_tasks?contact_id=...&status=open`; each card shows the linked conversation's stable `display_id`, while the filter includes persisted pending/in-progress states so derived overdue work and tracking survive conversation changes for the contact.
- `/activities` uses a `CalendarEvent` projection for optional outbound synchronization with exact start/end and deduplicated attendees; provider failures remain visible without rolling back valid local work.
- On mobile, `/activities` reorganizes filters and navigation while preserving the desktop month canvas with horizontal scrolling so its cells are not compressed. `TaskDialog` bounds its scrollable area with `dvh`, stacks its header, and keeps the footer outside scrolling content.
- `TaskDialog` opens existing records in read mode, enters editing explicitly, and requests `outcome_summary` in a secondary dialog for complete/cancel actions. Deletion requires confirmation, is shown only to administrators, and `AccountTaskPolicy#destroy?` enforces the same restriction in the API.
- Kanban item details show the operational Tasks tab only when the feature and hook are enabled; it queries `/account_tasks?kanban_item_id=...`, keeps item history in a separate tab, and prefills the contact, conversation, and item when creating.
- `AccountTasks::KanbanHistoryService` appends `account_task_changed` events to the item's bounded JSONB history inside the local transaction; it records lifecycle and link changes without duplicating them as conversation messages.
- The complete account-scoped Tasks and Task Types CRUD contract is maintained in modular `swagger/` sources, the resolved Swagger/public audience documents, `docs/openapi.yml`, and the generated Postman collection. It documents filters, `q`, interval intersection, safe sort allowlists, opt-in pagination metadata, rich relationships, recurrence, Kanban/Google conditions, permissions, and validation responses.
- Reports adds a route visible only while the feature and hook are active plus `GET /account_tasks/reports?since=&until=`, authorized for administrators and `task_reports_view` only. `AccountTasks::ReportsService` filters by `COALESCE(ends_at, starts_at)`, builds the daily completed series, and aggregates total, pending, in-progress, completed, overdue, and cancelled tasks by responsible agent and type. Each task belongs to exactly one status category. Charts and rows open a drawer that queries `GET /account_tasks` by effective scheduled date, responsible agent, or type; it then reuses `TaskDialog` in read mode. Elapsed-time metrics remain hidden because tasks do not yet persist a completion timestamp.
- Google controls appear only when the feature is enabled, the integration is connected, the outbound connection is active, the `calendar` module is enabled, and a writable calendar exists. Destination and external-guest fields appear only after opting into synchronization.
- Google Calendar uses account-scoped OAuth/config APIs, selected `CalendarConnection`, internal `CalendarEvent`, and provider mapping through `ExternalCalendarEvent`; `settings.import_all_calendars` controls inbound scope independently from the concrete outbound `calendar_id`.
- The operational calendar route `/app/accounts/:accountId/calendar` reads local `calendar_events` for day, week, month, list, create, edit, cancel, sync on save, and manual fallback sync. `CalendarSync::PollConnectionsJob` runs every five minutes and imports Google changes with per-calendar `last_polled_at` stored under `CalendarConnection.settings`; deleted Google events remove their mapped local projections, and recurring instances are expanded within a rolling one-year lookback and five-year lookahead.
- Calendar navigation, the operational route, composer action, and conversation-panel section require the account feature and a connected `GoogleCalendarIntegration` with `CalendarConnection#calendar_id`; custom-role users additionally need `calendar_manage`, while integration configuration remains administrator-only.
- The month view limits each cell to two visible events to reserve space for a `+N more` control; right-click opens event actions and each row still opens the editor. Color is stored in `metadata.color_id`; permanent deletion requires an administrator and removes a linked Google event before deleting its local record.
- `calendar_events` responses include lightweight contact, conversation, and Kanban item summaries for searchable relation selectors; edit payloads send `null` to unlink relations.
- The calendar relation selector searches Kanban items across the account instead of inheriting the currently selected funnel, and accepts item IDs (including `#ID` format).
- Checklist tasks have independent Google Calendar sync settings and destination calendars; each synchronized task is stored as its own event linked by `checklist_item_id`.
- The assigned checklist agent is added as a Google attendee and receives the platform reminder; completing or deleting the task cancels its event and pending reminder.
- The conversation reply composer loads the account connection and writable calendars before opening the shared `CalendarEventDialog`; the explicit “Create and send” action formats its `saved` result with localized event fields and sends it once through `createPendingMessageAndSend`, using `metadata.google_meet_url` without retrying event creation if messaging fails.
- The conversation contact panel filters `calendar_events` by `conversation_display_id`, recalculates Start-to-End progress every 30 seconds for a pulsing green (<50%), yellow (50–90%), or red (≥90%) dot, fades expired entries, reuses `CalendarEventDialog`, and consumes saved-event updates.
- `GoogleCalendar::EventMapperService` maps event metadata into Google fields for location, attendees, reminders, simple recurrence, availability, visibility, guest permissions, and Google Meet.
- The responsive event editor uses icon-led rows, an IANA time-zone selector, and removable guest chips; the sidebar places installation-branded context below guest permissions with floating searches and the inbox channel icon on every conversation result; it persists the writable destination and returned Meet URL.
- Google Calendar supports manual inbound import through `google_calendar_integration/import_events` and legacy Kanban backfill through `google_calendar_integration/backfill_kanban`; Flow Builder triggers remain deferred.
- Application PWA assets and splash screens are generated dynamically from `LOGO_THUMBNAIL`, rasterizing SVG through librsvg/libvips and falling back to `NOTIFICATION_ICON`; system Push artwork and badges use `NOTIFICATION_ICON` (default `/favicon-badge-16x16.png`) with the reciprocal fallback, but the monochrome badge is not advertised as an installable app icon. The HTML splash clears two frames after Vue's first render, with a 180 ms transition and a 4 s safety timeout. Backgrounds are configurable and caches are invalidated by asset, color, and blob timestamp; the favicon keeps the application artwork, switches to notification artwork while hidden or unfocused, and restores on return.
- PWA load: production builds write `.br` and `.gz` copies of text assets that Rails serves on its own (`ActionDispatch::Static`), so compression doesn't depend on each install's proxy. The Twilio voice SDK, Amplitude, the Mega Assistant panel, the new-conversation form and the conversation thread load on demand; the thread is prefetched once the list is idle, and a chunk missing after a deploy reloads the page once. The service worker keeps up to 500 fingerprinted assets, stores them without delaying the response, uses navigation preload and refreshes offline branding at most once a day. While loading on a phone, the dashboard shows a skeleton screen, first from the layout HTML and then from the app. The installed app shows its splash once per session, so a refresh goes straight to the skeleton. On phones, every page's first load and every change of page show a skeleton shaped like that page (`PageLoader`, `PageSkeleton`, with `config/page_skeletons.yml` mapping each path to its shape); larger screens keep each page's spinner. The splash icon is rounded on iOS, in the HTML splash and in the manifest icons (`/pwa-icon?rounded=true`), including `maskable`, which Chrome on Android draws on its opening screen, so its corners take the brand color (`on_brand=true`); an installed Android app needs a reinstall to pick it up. Apple touch icons stay square because iOS cuts them.
- Loading skeletons: `PageLoader` shows its `PageSkeleton` at every width (the Kanban board keeps its own columns through `keep-desktop-slot`); `AppSkeleton` and the server boot skeleton (`layouts/_boot_skeleton`, `PageSkeletonHelper`, `config/page_skeletons.yml`) draw the desktop frame (rail and panel) instead of a spinner, and the dashboard shows the skeleton on desktop route changes too. The conversation screen uses one `ConversationScreenSkeleton` overlay (list, thread and contact panel at their real widths) revealed once when the list, the thread with its messages and the contact panel are ready (15 s safety timeout; not shown again on later switches); `ConversationBoxSkeleton` and `ConversationSidebarSkeleton` replace the empty async placeholder and the inline pulse. The layout (`contactPanel`, `expanded`) is copied to the `localStorage` key `mega-skeleton-layout` for the boot and app skeletons.
- PWA reliability keeps authenticated navigation out of Cache Storage, bounds fingerprinted Vite assets, makes Rails serve `/sw.js` with mandatory revalidation headers, reconciles realtime state only after `RoomChannel` confirmation, and maintains browser Push opt-out/logout/VAPID-key lifecycle per user and device; activation accepts both Base64 and binary bootstrapped VAPID keys and reports unsupported, blocked, unconfigured, expired-session, and registration failures separately. Reverse proxies and CDNs must preserve the worker headers.
- Linear: `unlink_issue` checks with `linked_issues` that the `link_id` belongs to the conversation (404 otherwise, 422 when Linear returns an error) and the conversation activity uses the issue identifier Linear returns, not a client-sent `issue_id`; IDs interpolated into the `lib/linear` GraphQL queries and mutations are escaped as GraphQL values.
- Firecrawl (Captain): the webhook token is an HMAC-SHA256 with `secret_key_base` over the API key, assistant and account, compared in constant time; crawls in flight during the deploy get 401 and must be started again.
- Platform API: `POST /platform/api/v1/users` with an existing user's email only returns it to the platform app that already manages it (401 otherwise); `POST/PATCH/PUT /platform/api/v1/agent_bots` return 401 (`Non permissible resource`) when `account_id` is an account the app does not manage; keeping the bot's current account is always allowed.

### 3.11 Signup and onboarding

- Guided account-profile completion through the dedicated admin endpoint `/api/v1/accounts/:account_id/onboarding`.
- Optional phone at signup: `REGISTER.PHONE_NUMBER` in `Signup/Form.vue` (optional check for 8 to 15 digits with an optional `+`), `phone_number` on `POST /api/v1/accounts` and `AccountBuilder`, which stores `account.custom_attributes['phone_number']` with only `+` and digits and drops it when it is not a phone number. Super Admin shows it in the account header with a `wa.me` link and under Custom attributes (`super_admin.accounts.custom_attributes.phone_number`). It is not copied to the billing profile.
- Website persistence as a fully qualified URL for downstream consumers such as Help Center and brand enrichment.
- Separation between general account updates and completion of the `account_details` step.
- The Instagram OAuth callback preserves the signed `return_to` hint to resume inbox setup when authorization starts from onboarding.

### 3.12 Branded email layouts

- The `branded_email_templates` account feature flag enables account-level fallback and Email-inbox override layouts.
- `EmailTemplate` scopes layouts by installation, account, and inbox, validates Liquid syntax and the `content_for_layout` slot, and limits bodies to 256 KiB (262,144 characters).
- The account layout endpoint and Email inbox update endpoint are administrator-only; the OpenAPI schemas expose the same limit.

### 3.13 Security and compliance

- Audit, verification and performance: sign-in and sign-out audit rows (inserted together with `insert_all!`, which skips the location callback) enqueue a single `Enterprise::AuditLogSessionIpLookupJob` that resolves the IP location for the whole batch, only for accounts with `ip_lookup` and without being able to interrupt the login; the shared `request_uuid` is generated even when the header arrives blank. The Super Admin (internal settings) exposes the `DEVICE_VERIFICATION_ENABLED` switch and the `DEVICE_VERIFICATION_EXEMPT_EMAILS` list (one email per line, exempt from the code), and the wrong-code error points to the 6-digit code sent by email. The suspension history records who suspended the account (`suspended_by`) and shows it. The per-agent assignment limit uses one Redis sorted set per agent instead of a key per assignment counted with SCAN, and `GlobalConfig.clear_cache` deletes with SCAN + DEL instead of `KEYS`, without clearing the cache when there is no new value.
- Message Markdown escapes literal HTML tags and blocks dangerous link and image URLs; articles retain trusted embeds and tables. The dashboard sanitizes Captain HTML and signup terms with DOMPurify; only scenario instructions allow internal `tool://` links.
- UI and operations: while a Super Admin impersonates a user, the dashboard shows a fixed banner with the user's name and email and a button to stop impersonating (`ImpersonationBanner`, using the `useImpersonation` session flag). Feature announcements (`feature_announcement`) are not shown to accounts younger than 3 days (`PlatformBanner.for_user_and_account`); other banners still are. Enter (and Cmd/Ctrl+Enter) does not send while an IME composition is active (`isComposing`: `isComposing` or `keyCode` 229) in the reply box, the editor and Copilot. Transcriptions rename `.oga` to `.ogg` in the temp file (OpenAI rejects `.oga`) when ffmpeg normalization does not run. In Brazil, the lookup variant without the 9 is only generated when what remains is a mobile number, never a landline. A CI workflow (`migration_version_check.yml`) rejects duplicate migration versions; hour-rounded versions are still allowed.

- 2FA/MFA, SAML/SSO, custom roles, and audit logs. SAML allows a user who belongs to several accounts only when every one of those accounts has SAML on and trusts the same identity provider (`idp_entity_id`) and its certificates (`AccountSamlSettings#trusted_by_accounts_of?`, SHA-256 fingerprints); otherwise invitations and authentication are rejected, and bulk provider changes skip those users. Message deletion locks the row, snapshots its original content and conversation, inbox, and sender context, then writes its Enterprise audit entry in the same transaction as the soft deletion; repeated deletes do not create duplicates. Audit-log list responses omit Message payloads and the retained content.
- Custom roles apply to agents only: `AccountUser` turns `role` into `agent` when `custom_role_id` is set (API, SAML, MCP) and clears `custom_role_id` when `role` becomes `administrator`. The agents API answers 422 to `role: administrator` together with a `custom_role_id`, and to an update that would leave the account without an administrator (choosing Agent or a custom role for the last one). `custom_role_id` changes are audited. The `20261108120000` migration turns existing administrators with a custom role into agents; when every administrator of an account had one, the oldest stays administrator without it.
- The Enterprise audit-log endpoint accepts optional `q`, `types[]`, `since`, `until`, and `sort` filters; timestamps are constrained to database-safe Unix epochs, and the account association plus creation timestamp index supports filtered ordering. Dashboard filters are URL-backed and stale fetches are discarded.
- User session records support an agent-editable `custom_name` label; IP metadata remains internal and is not exposed in dashboard session payloads.
- Web authentication sends a validated, persistent 128-bit browser-profile ID as the token client ID. Tabs reuse one logical `UserSession`, reauthentication rotates the same slot, and successful sign-out removes both token and row. New profiles receive the blocking picker only at `MAX_USER_SESSIONS`; mobile clients retain generated IDs and silent eviction.
- Cloud password sign-ins can require an emailed six-digit device challenge when `DEVICE_VERIFICATION_ENABLED` is true. The token and code expire after 10 minutes; Redis enforces one-time redemption, five code attempts, and ten challenges per user per 24 hours. A trusted-device cookie lasts 30 days, and credential changes or `DELETE /api/v1/profile/trusted_devices` invalidate it. MFA and SSO remain separate; verification finishes through the existing session and audit flow.
- REST API tokens (user, agent bot and Platform App) are accepted both in the legacy `api_access_token` header and as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (scheme matched case-insensitively) on Application APIs, conversation direct uploads and Platform APIs. A legacy header keeps authenticating exactly as before, whatever `Authorization` header comes with it (proxies and gateways add their own); Bearer is used only without a legacy header, and an empty or invalid Bearer then returns 401 without falling back to another method. Base64-encoded DeviseTokenAuth credentials in the Bearer header keep authenticating the dashboard session. Rack::Attack keys per-user report and conversation-meta throttles by the same token whichever header carries it, keeps dashboard UIDs, and masks API tokens in throttle logs.
- Conversation list throttle: `Rack::Attack` limits `GET /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/conversations` per user or API token and account to `RATE_LIMIT_CONVERSATIONS_LIST` requests per minute (default 1000, which leaves room for kanban, mobile and polling clients).
- JSON compression: `Rack::Deflater` (before `Rack::ETag`, so ETags and 304s use the uncompressed body) gzips `application/json` responses when the client sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip`; other content types are untouched. `contact_inboxes` has a `(contact_id, inbox_id)` index (created concurrently) for the widget HMAC and contact lookups.
- Account-enforced 2FA: the `enforce_mfa` account setting (boolean in `settings`, off by default, only accepted when Active Record encryption keys exist) requires every member to enrol in TOTP. A password sign-in without 2FA answers `206` with `mfa_setup_required`, a 10-minute `mfa_setup_token` bound to the purpose and the current password, the provisioning URL and the secret; `POST /auth/sign_in` with `mfa_setup_token` + `otp_code` activates 2FA and returns the session plus backup codes. On Cloud, device verification runs first. Google/SAML sign-ins and Super Admin impersonation are not challenged. API tokens (`api_access_token` or Bearer) of unenrolled non-SAML members get `403` `error_code: mfa_enrollment_required` on the enforcing account's endpoints, account creation, profile, sessions, trusted devices, push subscriptions, billing and Kanban automations; account MCP static tokens (acting as the assigned user or, without one, the administrator with the lowest id, the same user the API proxy acts as) and MCP OAuth tokens of unenrolled members are refused the same way. Members cannot disable their own 2FA while it is enforced (`403`), password reset and email confirmation return no session while enrolment is pending, login MFA tokens carry a typed `token_type` claim, and Rack::Attack limits setup attempts (`mfa_setup_login/ip`, `mfa_setup_login/token`). A setup secret that was never activated is dropped when the password changes, so the next challenge hands out a new one. Things admins should know: sessions opened before enforcement stay valid until they end; sign-ins through `sso_auth_token` (Google, SAML, Super Admin impersonation) are not challenged, as with regular 2FA; profile-level endpoints of a member are gated when any of their accounts enforces 2FA; login MFA tokens issued before the deploy are rejected for up to 5 minutes.
- The enforcement toggle lives on Settings → Security (`EnforceMfa`, admin-only, shown when encryption keys exist). The Security route no longer requires the SAML flag or a cloud/enterprise install; the SAML section keeps its own feature and install gate, and the route bypasses the upgrade wall. Login: `MfaEnforcedSetup` reuses `MfaSetupWizard`, sends the browser-session header and stores credentials only after the backup-code step; `MfaVerification` hands a device-verification 206 setup challenge to it. The profile page hides the disable action while `GET /api/v1/profile/mfa` reports `enforced: true`.
- SAML IdP metadata URL: `account_saml_settings.metadata_url` / `metadata_refreshed_at`. Saving a new URL runs `Saml::IdpMetadataService` (SafeFetch with HTTPS required on the entered URL and on every redirect hop, 1 MB cap, 5 s read timeout, ruby-saml `IdpMetadataParser` with the redirect binding) and stores the SSO URL, entity ID and every signing certificate; failures fail the save with a fixed reason (`invalid_url`, `blocked_address`, `http_error`, `unreachable`, `too_large`, `not_metadata`) and never echo hosts or IPs; refused, reset or unreachable connections and truncated responses count as `unreachable`. At SAML login `refresh_metadata_if_needed` refetches only when the newest certificate expires within 15 minutes, at most every 5 minutes, claimed with an atomic `update_all` so concurrent logins keep the stored values; a failed refresh logs a warning and keeps them. Several certificates go to OmniAuth as `idp_cert_multi`, the fingerprint shows the one that expires last, and `POST /api/v1/accounts/:account_id/saml_settings/refresh` (administrators) refetches on demand. The SAML section uses the soft-theme toggle card with Metadata URL / Manual tabs.
- Suspended-account dashboard state keeps the support widget visible and exposes an explicit support action. On Cloud, the route guard and suspended screen allow only administrators to access billing so they can restore the account; agents remain on the suspended screen. Super Admin validates a category and a 256-character reason, appends events under `accounts.internal_attributes.suspensions`, retains unrelated internal metadata, and permits corrections to the latest event without changing its timestamp.
- `POST /super_admin/users/:id/resend_confirmation` is restricted to Super Admin; it resends Devise's standard instructions only for unconfirmed users and does not enqueue email for confirmed users. The detail page hides the action once the user is confirmed.
- Mega license protection in deployment flows.
- Release observability for version-level traceability.
- Multi-language Super Admin: `SuperAdmin::ApplicationController` applies `SwitchLocale` using the super admin's `ui_settings.locale`, then `DEFAULT_LOCALE`, then `en`; only `en`, `es`, and `pt_BR` are accepted (the only locales with `super_admin.*` translations). `PATCH /super_admin/locale` saves the preference from the sidebar selector, shared with the agent dashboard. `config/locales/administrate.pt_BR.yml` mirrors the Administrate gem's `pt-BR` file under our `pt_BR` key. The Vue dashboard uses `superAdmin.json` (en/es/pt_BR) with vue-i18n in the locale rendered by the server. Labels from `features.yml` and `installation_config.yml` are translated under `super_admin.features.*` and `super_admin.installation_configs.*`, falling back to the YAML English copy; Administrate fields use `activerecord.models`/`activerecord.attributes`, and `rails.pt_BR.yml` mirrors the `rails-i18n` base formats.
- Super Admin design: the layout (`layouts/super_admin/application`) holds the `_navigation` sidebar grouped into General, Platform, WhatsApp, Billing and System; on desktop it collapses to icons (state in `localStorage` per super admin) and below 1024 px it becomes an off-canvas menu with a top bar (`_toggle_sidebar`). Styles live in `super_admin/_layout.scss` and the `.sa` tokens of `_accounts.scss` (actions on `woot-700`, focus on `woot-500`). Generic forms group fields through a hash `FORM_ATTRIBUTES` with titles and descriptions in `super_admin.form_sections.*`; custom card forms get the same two-column layout from CSS. Tables with `sa-t--stack` and `data-label` render as cards below 640 px. The accounts list (`enterprise/.../accounts/index`) builds the Administrate search from `filter` (billing tab) and `state` (state or source), which chain; `SuperAdmin::AccountSearch` adds the emails of the account's users and serves both the list and the bulk actions' "select matching". Sorting uses `account[order]` (`name`, `account_users`, `created_at`; default `id desc`). Below 640 px the `sa-t--cards` table turns into cards with `sa_mini_meter`, select mode is the `sa-selecting` class and filters live in the `sa-sheet` `<dialog>`; the shared pagination takes `numbered: true`. Live search (`data-sa-live-form`) fetches the page again 300 ms after typing, swaps the `data-sa-live` blocks and updates the bulk actions' `data-sa-search-term` without losing focus. On the account page, `_tab_billing` takes `section` (`subscription`, `invoices`, `usage`, `ledger`, `history`; without it, invoice or ledger filters and pages open their own section) and only builds that section, so `Billing::UsageReport` runs only for AI usage; `_billing_actions` is the card's `sa-actions` footer, with refund and cancel last. Below 640 px `sa-t--invoices` and `sa-t--members` become grid rows and `.sa-tabs`/`.sa-scroll-x` scroll the active item into view. `platform_banners/_form` adds a live preview; the add-ons index has tabs and a currency switch, and `_price_grid` fills empty prices from USD with one action and edits one interval at a time on phones.

- `DELETE /super_admin/accounts/:id` enqueues `SuperAdmin::DeleteAccountJob` with the account ID and its associated user IDs. After `DeleteObjectJob` removes the account, it destroys only users with no membership in another account and skips `SuperAdmin` records; other account deletion flows retain their own user-handling policy.
- Super Admin 2FA: `SuperAdmin::Devise::SessionsController` asks super admins with MFA enabled for the TOTP code or a backup code after the password. The challenge lives in the session, bound to the password salt, and expires after 5 minutes; failed codes are counted per admin in Redis (5 in 15 minutes cancel the challenge) and Rack::Attack has its own bucket for code submissions (`super_admin_mfa/ip`, 10 per minute) that does not use up password attempts. The Devise password, registration and confirmation routes for super admins are disabled.
- Password reset: `DeviseOverrides::PasswordsController` rejects expired tokens (`reset_password_within` = 6 hours); for a pending invitation (unconfirmed user with an `inviter_id`) the window is 7 days. The account is confirmed only after the new password is saved, so a rejected password leaves the invitation pending.
- Profile: `PUT /auth` (devise_token_auth registration update) also requires `current_password` for any change (`check_current_password_before_update = :attributes`).
- Profile: `PUT/PATCH /api/v1/profile` requires `current_password` when changing the password or changing the email to a different address (422 `Invalid current password`); the profile form shows the current password field when the email is edited.
- Server-side HMAC: `HmacConcern#hmac_verified?` (contact inbox and conversation creation in the account API) computes verification from `identifier` + `identifier_hash` against the inbox `hmac_token` and the contact's identifier, and ignores a client-sent `hmac_verified`. In the public API, `hmac_mandatory` inboxes require the `identifier_hash` of the contact behind the `source_id` on every contact, conversation and message endpoint (`Public::Api::V1::InboxesController#ensure_hmac_verified`, 401), and `contacts#create/update` signs the contact's stored identifier instead of any identifier in the request and returns 401.
- Text instead of HTML: the dashboard escapes contact names (including the merge summary), canned responses, macro names and notification cards; `ContactInfoRow` always renders text and the flag is passed through a slot.
- Dependencies: `addressable` 2.9.2, `aws-sdk-s3` 1.242, `datadog` 2.48, `oauth` 1.1.9 and `oauth2` 2.0.26; `bundle-audit` is clean (the Devise Timeoutable advisory is ignored because it is not used). With `oauth2` 2.0.26 a missing refresh token raises `OAuth2::Error` and `FetchImapEmailsJob` marks the inbox with an authorization error instead of crashing.

### 3.14 Native subscriptions and billing

- Database catalog: `billing_plans`, `billing_plan_prices`, `billing_addons`, `billing_addon_prices`, `billing_credit_packages` and `billing_credit_package_prices`, managed from Super Admin.
- `POST /super_admin/billing_plans/:id/duplicate` copies the plan with `dup` (limits, `features`, `providers`, `addon_rules`, `captain_models`, `mega_assistant_models`) and its prices without `stripe_price_id`. The copy is `active: false`, with no `default_for_signup`, `stripe_product_id` or `shopify_plan_handle`, slug `<slug>_copy` (or `_copy_2`, `_copy_3`…) and a name numbered the same way ("Growth (copy 2)", `super_admin.billing.plans.copy_name` with `count`), and redirects to its edit page; Stripe creates its own product and prices when it is saved.
- Exclusive plans (`billing_plan_accounts`, `Billing::PlanAccount`): a plan with linked accounts is reserved for them even with `public: true`. `Billing::Plan.publicly_listed` excludes linked plans and `Billing::Plan.available_to(account)` adds the account's own. They are used by the catalog (`CatalogPresenter#listed_plans`), `BillingController#find_price` (404 for other accounts on `preview` and `subscription`), `SubscriptionManager#sellable?` (`price_unavailable`) and `SignupTrialService.accept_trial!`. Super Admin picks the accounts in the plan form (`exclusive_account_ids`, `#plan-accounts` section), the plan list shows "Reserved for N accounts" and the account's Billing tab lists its reserved plans. Signup currencies (`Currencies.sold_currencies`) and the signup offer still count public plans only. Links cascade on account or plan deletion.
- `AccountSubscription` (one current per account) stores plan, price, gateway, source (`gateway`, `courtesy`, `manual`, `trial`), period, grace and scheduled changes; `AccountSubscriptionItem` stores purchased add-ons.
- `Billing::SignupTrialService` stores the default plan ID and suspends signup until an explicit choice: `signup_trial_available` when it offers free days, `signup_payment_required` otherwise. The `NativeBilling` signup screen (`SignupSteps`, `SignupPlanCatalog`, `PlanComparison`) puts the plan first with its gateways and groups benefits through `planFeatures.js`, shared with `PlanCatalog`; every public plan with `trial_days > 0` offers its trial in both signup categories. Clicking the trial button accepts the end-of-trial payment condition and `POST billing/start_trial` (optional `plan_id`, a public plan with trial days; defaults to the signup offer) starts the trial once, stores `signup_trial_accepted_at` and reactivates the account. Confirmed payment also reactivates it. `Billing::TrialLifecycleJob` suspends an unpaid account when the trial ends. The signup form sends its locale, which `AccountBuilder` retains for Billing and the onboarding language selector.
- `Billing::EntitlementsService` is the single place that applies features and limits: it evolves `ReconcilePlanFeaturesService`, respects manually managed features and preserves the Shopify flow.
- `Billing::AiCredits` implements the AI Actions ledger (`ai_credit_ledger_entries`): grants per period, debits (plan first, then packages by earliest expiry) and expires balances through an hourly job. `consume!` charges the model the feature passes or, when it passes none, the model and provider recorded on the execution's calls; `units:` multiplies the model's price (started minutes of an audio note) and the ledger stores the per-unit `credit_multiplier` and `units` (migration `20261121170000`).
- One price per model: `Llm::Models.ai_action_cost(model_id, provider_slug:)` resolves the provider's AI Actions in Super Admin → AI Providers, then the `credit_multiplier` from `config/llm.yml`, then 1, for registered ids (`slug::model`) and built-in names alike; dated snapshots are priced as their base model. Charging, the Captain model picker, the plan form, `SuperAdmin::LlmProvidersHelper` and `Billing::ModelActionAdvisor` use it. Features pass their routed model (`Llm::Models.route_model_id`).
- Large operations: Copilot workflows charge each turn (`copilot_workflows`), each reviewed conversation whose result is saved (`copilot_review`) and each screening decision (`copilot_screening`); Mega Assistant charges each reviewed conversation (`mega_assistant_review`); call transcription charges each model call (`Llm::MeteredTranscriptionClient#calls`); audio notes charge per started minute; voice stays per 5-minute block.
- Overdraft: usage with no grant left is a debit with no grant; `Billing::AiCredits.overdraft` is that sum minus the `overdraft_settlement` debits, `available` and `totals` (`overdraft`) subtract it and the balance can go negative. `grant!` locks the account and pays the overdraft first from the new grant. Pre-checks block new work at a balance ≤ 0; work already running finishes. Features that only check the balance (`Account#ai_actions_available?`): classifier suggestions (422), Flow Builder text to speech, FAQs from documents, contact notes and FAQs from conversations (through `captain_active?`) and dictation (402).
- Gateways behind a common interface (`Billing::Providers`): Stripe (Checkout, native proration, Customer Portal), Asaas (recurring card Checkout or PIX/boleto subscriptions, in-house proration and fiscal documents) and Shopify (read-only snapshot).
- Asaas PIX and boleto (`Billing::Gateways::Asaas::InvoicePageBilling`): the recurring Checkout only takes cards, so with `billing_type` `PIX` or `BOLETO` (`POST billing/subscription`, allowed by `PaymentGateway#subscription_billing_types`, setting `subscription_billing_types`, card by default) Mega creates the subscription with `POST /v3/subscriptions`, stores the first charge as the pending invoice and redirects to its `invoiceUrl`; the existing webhooks activate, renew and mark it past due (the first charge stays `open` while the subscription is `incomplete`). `account_subscriptions.asaas_billing_type` keeps the method (empty = card). Prorations and add-ons of these subscriptions are `POST /v3/payments` charges with the same `billingType` (boleto due in 3 days) instead of the card checkout. `POST billing/payment_method` (`billing_type`) switches a running subscription with `PUT /v3/subscriptions/{id}` (`updatePendingPayments: true`); the summary exposes `subscription.billing_type`, `subscription.billing_types` and `allowed_actions.change_payment_method`. One-off checkouts (`billing_types`) also accept `BOLETO`. Unlike the card checkout, these subscriptions never expire on their own: `Billing::ReconcileSubscriptionsJob` (daily) first replays their payments and, when the first charge is still unpaid past the gateway grace days (at least 5, the time a boleto takes to clear), deletes the Asaas subscription and expires the pending checkout. A failure after the remote subscription is created deletes it too, and a refused PIX/boleto proration or add-on charge voids its invoice (`payment_refused`).
- Asaas customer (`Billing::Gateways::Asaas::Customers`): created or updated from the billing details before each checkout and reused by looking it up by `externalReference` + `cpfCnpj`. Asaas derives the city from the CEP; when the response comes back without `city`, Mega looks the CEP up on ViaCEP (`Billing::Gateways::Asaas::CityCode`) and sends the IBGE code of the city as `city`. If the lookup or the update fails, the customer stays as it was and the checkout returns the Asaas message.
- Billing intervals `month`, `semester` (6 months) and `year` (`Billing::PlanPrice::INTERVAL_MONTHS`). The semester maps to Stripe `month` × 6, Asaas `SEMIANNUALLY` and a PayPal template plan of `MONTH` × 6; dLocal Go renewals follow `period_length`. A longer interval is an upgrade charged as the new period value minus the unused part of the current one.
- Idempotent webhooks: `POST /enterprise/webhooks/stripe` and `/enterprise/webhooks/asaas` store the event in `billing_webhook_events` (unique per provider + id) and `Billing::ProcessWebhookEventJob` processes it with retries.
- Credentials encrypted in `payment_gateways`, falling back to `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for existing installations.
- Scheduled jobs: credit expiry, monthly grants for semiannual, yearly and manual subscriptions, grace period, trial lifecycle, scheduled changes and gateway reconciliation.
- `NATIVE_BILLING_ENABLED` turns the commercial side (checkout, trials, grace) on for cloud and self-hosted alike. Counting and blocking AI Actions depend on the account having a native plan (`native_plan_governed?`), even with the flag off; on self-hosted, an account without a native plan is charged nothing and the model picker shows no credits (`useCaptainModelOptions`: Cloud, or the account payload field `native_plan_governed`; `billing_state` other than `legacy`/`incomplete` only for payloads without that field).
- Account API under `/enterprise/api/v1/accounts/:id/billing/*` (plans, preview, subscription, add-ons, packages, invoices and billing profile), available to administrators of billing-suspended accounts.
- Service charges (`Billing::ServiceCharges`): invoices with `kind: 'service'` and no subscription, in the account currency, with `description`, `note` and `created_by` in `metadata`. Super Admin (`POST /super_admin/billing_accounts/:id/create_service_charge`, `void_service_charge`) or the Platform API (`POST` and `DELETE /platform/api/v1/accounts/:account_id/billing/charges`) create them, and `AccountNotificationMailer#service_charge_created` emails the administrators. `POST billing/invoices/:invoice_id/pay` creates a fresh checkout on every attempt through `Billing::Providers.gateway_for` (Stripe Checkout `mode: payment`, Asaas `DETACHED`, dLocal Go checkout and PayPal Orders), because the links expire. Webhooks mark them paid with no other effect; unlike the other one-off charges, a refused payment or an expired checkout does not void them. `Billing::OverdueServiceChargesJob` (daily) turns past-due ones `overdue`; it never suspends the account. The account JSON includes `billing_pending_charges` (count, total, currency and whether any is overdue) for `ServiceChargeBanner`, shown to administrators outside Billing; `NativeBilling` shows its own banner with "Pay now". `Billing::Realtime` sends `account.billing_updated` to the administrators when an invoice is created or changes, or when the subscription status, plan, period, cancellation or grace changes; the dashboard groups the events (1 s), reloads the account and refreshes Billing without the loader.
- Custom gateway (`kind: 'custom'`, several instances): its setup lives in `payment_gateways.settings` (auth type, header, OAuth, `charge_url`/`charge_method`/`charge_body` with `{{variables}}`, the `response_id_path`/`response_url_path`/`webhook_id_path`/`webhook_status_path` paths, `paid_values`/`failed_values`, `status_url`/`status_path`, `due_days`, `requires_billing_profile`) and its secrets in `credentials` (`api_key_value`, `client_secret`, OAuth tokens, `webhook_token`). `Billing::Gateways::Custom::Template` fills the JSON (a string that is only `{{x}}` keeps its type) and reads dot paths with array positions. `SubscriptionService` follows the dLocal Go model with links only: each invoice keeps `external_id` `<gateway id>:<charge id>` and `metadata.gateway_id`; `Billing::CustomGatewayRenewalsJob` (hourly) creates the renewal link at period end and leaves the subscription `past_due` with grace. `POST /enterprise/webhooks/custom/:gateway_id?token=` stores the notification; `WebhookHandler` finds the invoice by id or by the `<kind>-<id>` reference and, when a status request exists, reads the status again before applying it. `Billing::CustomGatewayPollJob` (every 10 min, sidekiq-cron) checks pending charges up to 30 days old. OAuth: `GET /super_admin/payment_gateways/:id/connect` and `oauth_callback` (signed state, 15 min), refreshed by `Billing::Gateways::Custom::Oauth`. "Test" sends a real 5.00 charge and stores the answer in `settings.last_test`.
- Add-on types (`Billing::Addon::KINDS`): `agent` and `inbox` raise limits through `EntitlementsService.limits_for`; `feature` stores the account feature in `billing_addons.feature` (validated against `Featurable::FEATURE_LIST`), is capped at one unit and is not offered on plans whose `features` already include it (`Billing::Plan#addon_available?`). `EntitlementsService.apply` passes the features of the subscription items with quantity > 0 to `ReconcilePlanFeaturesService`, which stores them in `internal_attributes['addon_granted_features']`: reducing the add-on turns off only what an add-on granted, when the decrease applies, and never a feature the account got another way (Super Admin, a flag from before the add-on). Feature add-ons do not join `native_managed_features`. `custom` is free text; with `billing_type: 'one_time'` its prices use the `one_time` interval and are not mirrored into Stripe.
- Add-on reductions are scheduled with `Billing::AddonChange.schedule!` (`scheduled_quantity`, applied by `ApplyScheduledChangesJob` at period end); asking again for the current quantity clears it, and on Stripe `link_stripe_items!` drops the ids of items deleted there so keeping the add-on adds the item back. `validate_decrease!` refuses reductions below usage (`addon_agents_in_use`, `addon_inboxes_in_use`, `addon_in_use`; a Super Admin limit override is not affected). `Billing::AddonChange.purchasable?` decides increases; the catalog also lists held add-ons that are no longer sold (`purchasable: false`, `max_quantity` = current). Super Admin: `POST /super_admin/billing_accounts/:id/change_addon` (`item_id`, `keep`).
- Invoice links and descriptions: `Billing::Invoice#document_url` shows the gateway page only while it is a receipt (Stripe invoice, Asaas `invoiceUrl`) or, for dLocal Go and PayPal, which only give a payment page, while the charge is still payable. Add-on invoices store `metadata.description` ("WhatsApp calls × 1"), used as the gateway concept, in the payment history and in Super Admin, with the Stripe add-on checkout issuing its invoice (`invoice_creation`) and the webhook storing its link; the payment history describes prorations as the plan they moved to (`billing.invoice_descriptions.plan_change`).
- New add-on units are charged at their full price when bought (`Billing::AddonChange.charge_now_cents`, used by the four gateways), not prorated; Stripe adds the item with `proration_behavior: 'none'`, so the next invoice charges plan plus add-ons.
- Counted feature add-ons (`Billing::AddonGrants`), configured on the add-on itself: `scope: 'inbox'` (only `Billing::Addon::PER_INBOX_FEATURES`, today `waha_voice_calls`) caps the inboxes with the feature on to the units bought; `Whatsapp::WahaVoiceCallsGate.quota_available?` (Enterprise override through `singleton_class.prepend_mod_with`) guards `PATCH whatsapp_waha/voice_calls` and `list_apps` returns `voice_calls_quota_available` so `WahaSessionApps` locks the row with a link to Billing. Only units bought cap it: without them, or when the plan, a Super Admin exception or a manually managed feature grants it, there is no cap. `includes_inbox` (only `Billing::Addon::INBOX_PROVIDERS`) reserves one inbox per unit for that `Channel::Whatsapp` provider: `Enterprise::Concerns::Inbox#ensure_create_permitted` lets such an inbox use a free reserved unit and counts the regular limit without the inboxes the units hold. `requires_features` (only the dependencies in `Billing::Addon::FEATURE_REQUIREMENTS`, such as `wavoip` on `channel_whatsapp` or `channel_api`; Super Admin shows them only for those features) hides the add-on from accounts without at least one of them and blocks increases. `Billing::AddonGrants.feature_sellable?` hides and refuses a feature add-on (without its own inboxes) when the account already has the feature another way (plan, Super Admin exception, manually managed feature or a direct flag), or when a Super Admin exception turned it off; one already bought stays listed so it can be reduced. `Billing::AddonChange` refuses a decrease below what is in use (`addon_in_use`); the catalog returns `in_use`.
- Dependencies and plan changes: `Billing::AddonChange.validate_requirements_kept!` refuses (`addon_required`) a change that leaves an add-on with `requires_features` without any of them, counting the removals already scheduled (`Billing::AddonGrants.feature_kept?`: the feature stays when it comes another way or from an add-on that stays). `Billing::SubscriptionLifecycle.apply_upgrade!` and `schedule_change!` call `Billing::AddonChange.drop_included!`, which schedules to 0 the feature add-ons the new plan includes (`Billing::Plan#includes_addon?`); Stripe leaves them out of the scheduled downgrade (`target_items`) and, on an upgrade, deletes their item with `proration_behavior: 'none'`; Asaas updates the value with the scheduled quantities. After a Stripe downgrade applies, the webhook links the items again (`link_stripe_items!`). The catalog and the Super Admin billing tab show `in_use` for add-ons sold by units.
- The account payload carries `inbox_availability` (`Billing::AddonGrants.inbox_availability`: `limit`, regular `available` and free `reserved_available` per WhatsApp provider). `useInboxAvailability` locks the channel cards in `ChannelList` (`ChannelSelector` `isLocked`) and the WhatsApp providers without a free reserved unit, also when opened from the URL; `CustomExceptions::Inbox::LimitExceeded` is translated (`errors.inbox.limit_exceeded`) as the backend safety net.
- One-time add-ons (`Billing::AddonPurchases`): `POST billing/addons/:slug/purchase` (`quantity`) creates an invoice with `kind: 'addon_purchase'` (addon, quantity and description in `metadata`) and each gateway's `purchase_addon` opens a one-off checkout (Stripe Checkout `mode: payment`, Asaas, dLocal Go and PayPal Orders). Webhooks recognize the `addon_purchase` reference and only mark it paid; a refused payment voids it, like an AI Actions package. Paid invoices without `metadata.delivered_at` are listed in Super Admin → Add-ons ("Pending deliveries", `POST /super_admin/billing_addons/deliveries/:invoice_id`) and counted as `pending_addon_deliveries` in `RevenueReport#attention`.
- The billing page sorts the bounded invoices response by its displayed invoice date, applies combined client-side status and concept filters, and paginates the matching result in groups of four.
- `Llm::UsageInstrumentation` records every RubyLLM call in `llm_usage_events`, including provider, account, feature, model, tokens, and cost calculated from provider pricing. `Llm::UsageTracker` groups calls from one execution and links them to a charged AI Action; `Billing::UsageReport` powers the Super Admin dashboard and each account's monthly summary.
- `Billing::RevenueReport` powers the Super Admin revenue dashboard from `account_subscriptions` and `billing_invoices`, keeping every amount in its own currency; it shares the period filter (`Billing::ReportPeriod`) with `Billing::UsageReport`. The trend stacks the `SERIES` (`subscription` + `proration`, `addons` + `addon_purchase`, `credit_package` and `service`); collections by kind group every paid invoice.
- Billing dashboard copy distinguishes today's paying subscriptions, pending invoices and installation-wide subscriber status from selected-month collections and AI usage. The AI tab displays only the month selector; its USD figures ignore currency and payment provider. Its action count groups charged `llm_usage_events` by `action_key`, while credits used sums the profit-and-loss ledger lines; one action can consume multiple credits.
- The Super Admin AI usage dashboard leads with profit and loss, separates distinct charged actions from ledger credits, and labels uncharged calls in its average-cost note. Summary, account, and breakdown data use the selected period; package prices and charging settings are current, model suggestions use the last 30 days, and the current-month provider forecast only appears for the current month.
- `Billing::AiSale` freezes what each consumed AI Action was sold for on its ledger entry (`sale_cents`, `sale_currency`, `usd_rate`) and spreads it over the action's LLM calls (`llm_usage_events.sale_usd`), so changing `BILLING_AI_ACTION_PRICE_<CUR>` or `BILLING_USD_RATE_<CUR>` never rewrites past months; missing values are filled once when a price or rate exists. `Billing::AiProfitAndLoss` (through `Billing::UsageReport#profit_and_loss`) builds the AI profit and loss in USD from those snapshots, per origin of the credit from `Billing::AiCreditOrigin`: packages (income = what was paid), plans paid through a gateway or by hand (income estimated at the reference price), courtesy or trial plans and admin adjustments (cost: value shown apart, never income; a credit's plan is the subscription the account had when it was granted) and calls with no linked credit (`llm_usage_events.ai_credit_ledger_entry_id` null). The same partial (`billing_shared/_ai_pnl`) is used on the usage dashboard, the billing summary and the account page; `Billing::AiMargin` gives income and courtesy per account with the same rule for the plans table.
- `Billing::AiPackageHealth` compares each active package's price per credit with the most a credit can cost (cost mode: USD reference price ÷ (1 + markup); model mode: the period's average cost per credit) and works out the package's minimum price. `Billing::ModelActionAdvisor` suggests each model's AI Actions for the fixed-per-model mode: P90 cost of its actions in the last 30 days × (1 + markup) ÷ the reference price or the cheapest package price, rounded up. `Billing::MegaAssistantBreakdown` groups charged Mega Assistant replies by feature from the tools recorded in `mega_assistant_messages.steps`; each charge references the reply message.

## 4. Operations and validation

### API parity and Postman collection

Supported routes under `/api`, `/platform/api`, and `/public/api` are compared with OpenAPI 3.1 by normalized method and path. Validation detects missing, stale, or duplicate operations; it does not claim test coverage for every response field. `bundle exec rake swagger:build` regenerates Swagger and `swagger/postman_collection.json` in an import-ready structure: Application API resources are top-level folders inheriting `api_access_token`, while Mega Platform APIs and Mega Public APIs retain dedicated folders and authentication. Collection variables centralize `host`, `api_version`, `account_id`, credentials, and path identifiers. Multimedia messages include separate examples for selecting a file with `multipart/form-data` or using a JSON `signed_blob_id`. `Idempotency-Key` is optional and disabled; it can be enabled with `{{$guid}}` or a fixed key to verify retries.

Scheduled messages can be created at account level with `contact_id` and `inbox_id`, without requiring a conversation, inside a conversation (`inbox_id` defaults to the conversation's inbox), or through `POST /scheduled_outbound_messages` with a phone, email, `contact_id`, or `source_id`: the latter resolves or creates the contact/contact-inbox transactionally and leaves the message pending without creating a conversation early. Failed messages recover through explicit, authorized actions: retry makes the record pending and queues it immediately; reschedule requires a future time, preserves content, template parameters, and attachments, and keeps account and conversation boundaries intact. A sent message can be scheduled again as an independent, non-recurring copy with editable future time and content while retaining its recipient, inbox, template, and attachments.

Recurrence: `recurrence_days` is normalized to sorted, unique integers 0–6 before validation (multipart forms send them as text) and read the same way from rows saved as text. Weekly occurrences pick the next weekday in the account's `reporting_timezone` (UTC when blank), editable in Settings → General and through `PATCH /api/v1/accounts/:id` (`reporting_timezone`, validated against `ActiveSupport::TimeZone`). The next occurrence takes the contact from the conversation when the message has none and copies attachments by blob. `ScheduledMessageJob#create_next_recurrence` runs it in a savepoint; on error the message stays `sent` with `error_message` "The next occurrence could not be created: …", which the card and detail show.

UI: the page (`ScheduledMessagesPage.vue`) groups by local day with `getDayKey` (not `toISOString`, which grouped in UTC) and starts on the `all` range (upcoming ascending and, under "Earlier", past descending) of the shared date picker, which now takes `ranges` and `defaultRange` (`scheduleDateRanges`: upcoming, next 7/30 days and the past ranges; Reports and Audit logs are unchanged). Search and the status, inbox and agent filters apply to the list and the calendar. `ScheduledMessageCard`, `ScheduledMessageDetail`, `ScheduledMessageFormPanel` (inside `CampaignFormPanel`) and `ScheduledMessageActions` (detail, form and `Dialog type="alert"` confirmations) are shared by the page, `Conversation/ScheduleMessageModal.vue` and the Kanban tab; `useScheduledMessageStatus` holds the chips, per-status actions, the i18n recurrence summary and Intl dates. The form hides Twilio WhatsApp inboxes (their `content_templates` are not listed) unless the edited message already uses one, requires a template on Cloud/NotificaMe/360dialog and keeps the message's template when editing. `GET /conversations/:id/scheduled_messages` returns `push_event_data` plus `conversation_display_id`, like the account list. `PATCH /scheduled_messages/:id` accepts `attachments[]` (multipart) and `remove_attachment_ids[]`; the 15-file limit ignores the ones marked for removal.

Recommended checklist for feature-level changes:

1. Validate role permissions and account boundaries.
2. Validate realtime events under concurrent usage.
3. Run unit tests for the affected domain.
4. Run browser-level manual validation for full user flow.
5. Verify i18n parity in ES, EN, and PT-BR for new strings.

The dashboard waits 2.5 seconds, checks the latest status and assignee, cancels on resolution or manual takeover, and applies existing audio preferences.
